# History: the full change log

Moved here from CLAUDE.md on 2026-10-13 (it was loaded into every chat and took about 35,000 tokens). Every feature as it was built, with the class names, numbers and tests. Read the part you need when working on a system; add a bullet here for every new feature or fix.

## Change log (was "Current status")
- Mod name: **Ant & Termite Colonies**, mod ID: **antcolonies**, package `com.antcolonies`.
- M0 done: project from the official NeoForge 1.21.1 MDK, build passes, `runClient` confirmed working by the user.
- M1 done: worker ant with placeholder GeckoLib model, walk/idle animations, and spawn egg; user confirmed working in-game.
- M2 deferred: a detailed blocky placeholder worker model exists (bone names: body, abdomen, petiole, thorax, head, mandible_l/r, antenna_l/r_1/2, leg_{l,r}{1,2,3} with _lower and _foot children). User will make the real animations in Blockbench later and must keep these bone names.
- M3 done (user confirmed): queen ant (placeholder, reuses worker model, bigger) + spawn egg; colony brain in `com.antcolonies.colony` (Colony, ColonyManager as SavedData per dimension); queen creates a colony, workers within 32 blocks join it, counts update on death; colony removed when queen dies; `/antcolonies list` debug command. Shared base class `AbstractAntEntity`.
- M4 done (user confirmed): workers dig a 4-deep entrance shaft bite by bite (`DigGoal`, diggable tag), climb walls like spiders (`AbstractAntEntity`, `AntRenderer` tilts the model), carry each bite as a soil pellet shown on the "head" bone (`PelletLayer`) and drop it to build a mound 3 blocks high around the entrance (`CarryPelletGoal`). The mound is made of the new **Burrowed Dirt** block (`ModBlocks`). Ants take no fall damage.
- Nest digging (user's design): shaft stops at block 3 (block 4 can hit stone); the shaft is never widened so its 4 walls stay solid for climbing out; a 3-block tunnel leaves the shaft bottom toward one side (fixed per colony, falls back to another side if the soil doesn't fit), ending in the queen's 5x5 room, one block tall. Code: `Colony.findDigTarget`, `NestPlan`.
- Nursery and food store (user's request, waiting for the user to confirm in-game): two more 5x5 rooms, each at the end of its own 3-block side tunnel that leaves the middle of the main tunnel, the nursery to one side and the food store to the other (see the picture in `NestPlan`). Dug in the order queen room, nursery, food store. A room that would run into stone or water is left out; the side of the main tunnel with the most rooms that fit is used. The whole nest is 87 blocks. For now they are empty and dug right away; later they get their purpose (eggs and larvae in the nursery, seeds in the food store) and are only dug at the right nest stage (M6).
- Queen goes down and settles in the middle of her room as soon as it is finished (`QueenToRoomGoal`, `Colony.getFinishedRoomCenter`) and stops wandering, while the other rooms may still be dug. Climbing ants get against a wall before rising, and never cross the open shaft.
- Ant navigation rewritten (package `com.antcolonies.nav`): own route planner and walker instead of vanilla pathfinding, own `AntMoveControl`, ants climb at a steady speed along routes, wander goal replaces vanilla strolling. Pellets are never dropped on a living creature (a pellet once buried the queen). Worker speed raised to 0.25 (was 0.15, about 1 block per second; the number is in `WorkerAntEntity.createAttributes`). Verified with the automatic tests; waiting for the user to confirm in-game.
- M5 done (user confirmed "works perfectly").
- Ant hill (user's design): the mound is a round, stepped hill: 3 blocks high in the first ring around the hole, 2 in the second, 1 in the third, hole left open (64 blocks; shape in `Colony.moundHeightAt`). It is built from the bottom up, layer by layer, and then it is capped: once it is full, ants stop carrying soil out and just leave it behind. One block per pellet stays; every bite makes a pellet, so the hill is complete after the first ~11 dug blocks. The finished hill makes the hole a 3-block-deeper funnel; ants climb out of it and walk back down.
- Bugs found by the user's crash report and fixed: a route that was only the ant's own cell crashed the game when the ant drifted out of it (`AntNavigator`, with a regression test); the last hill spots could deadlock because several ants stood in the same spot (ants now prefer free spots and step aside before placing a pellet).
- Ants climbing over a ledge (like each hill step) now first walk up to the foot of the wall and only then rise, so they never float up in front of it (`AntNavigator`).
- Sealed nest (user's request, waiting for the user to confirm in-game): the rule is "every block touching a dug-out nest cell must be solid, except the entrance hole". `Colony.getLeaks` finds the openings (a cave, a crack to the outside, a gap between two rooms, even water), and `SealGoal` (workers, runs before digging) sends an ant to the nearest unclaimed one to pack it shut with Burrowed Dirt (4 packs, no pellet needed). The rooms already have a 1-block wall between them (`NestPlanTest`); sealing keeps them apart even if a natural cave touches that wall. Limits: only soft blocks are replaced (air, water, lava, grass), never stone, chests or flowers; a hole right next to the top of the shaft cannot be reached (nothing to stand on), so it stays open. `DigGoal` now tries every open side of a block as a place to stand (a sealed-off pocket next to it used to block the dig). Game test `workersCloseEveryHoleInTheNestWalls` cuts 5 holes into the walls and checks the ants close them all.
- Thin soil fix (user bug report: ants stopped once the shaft was dug): in that world only the top 3 blocks were dirt and the layer where the rooms go was full of stone, so the queen's room fit nowhere and the ants had nothing left to dig. Now the room plan is made before the first block is dug (`Colony.chooseLayout`): full depth (shaft 3) if the queen's room fits there, otherwise the whole nest sits one block higher (shaft 2, `MIN_SHAFT_DEPTH`). If it fits at neither depth the ants still dig the shaft and then stop (no stone is ever dug). Game test `nestSitsOneBlockHigherWhenTheStoneIsTooClose`. Old saved worlds keep their already-dug 3-deep shaft; test in a new world.
- Nest adapts to the ground (user's request, waiting for the user to confirm in-game): before digging, `Colony.chooseLayout` looks at the soil for shaft depth 3 and 2 (`MIN_SHAFT_DEPTH`). A depth only counts if the whole shaft is soil and the queen's room fits; the depth where the most rooms fit wins (deeper on a tie). The queen now digs too (`DigGoal` with a "may I dig" check): she helps with the shaft and her own tunnel and room, stops digging once her room is finished and settles in it. She cannot place blocks (`AbstractAntEntity.canPlaceBlocks`, false for her): her bites make no soil pellets (no hill from her) and she does not seal. Workers do the nursery and food store. Game test `aloneQueenDigsHerOwnRoom` (queen with no workers).
- Water and lava defence REMOVED (user's decision, it got too complicated): the commits ad0fcf4 to ec5253c were undone, so ants again ignore water and lava. The nest is back to the state of commit a41c4a9 (sealed nest, adaptive depth, queen digs). Hazards stay a LATER item in the plan above ("Alarm level and hazards"); the old code is still in git history if it is ever wanted again. Next: M6.
- Shared sealing (user's request, waiting for the user to confirm in-game): up to 4 ants can work on the same opening in the nest walls and every pack adds to the same progress (`Colony.addSealProgress`, a pack is a quarter), so more ants close it faster (1 ant = 4 packs, 4 ants = 1 round). An opening nobody is at yet beats a crowded one (`Colony.findLeak`).
- M6 step 1, seed gathering (user's request, waiting for the user to confirm in-game): once the food store room is dug out, workers fetch seed items lying on the ground within 24 blocks of the hole (tag `antcolonies:seeds`: wheat, beetroot, melon, pumpkin, torchflower seeds, pitcher pod; `GatherSeedGoal`), take ONE seed from a pile in the mandibles (drawn by `PelletLayer`, synced in `AbstractAntEntity.getCarriedSeed`), carry it down (`StoreSeedGoal`) and add it to a heap of the new **Seed Pile** block on the floor of the food store (`Colony.depositSeed`: a layered block like snow, `SeedPileBlock`, 1 layer per seed, 8 layers per block, no collision so ants and players walk through; most seeds go to an existing heap; textures `seed_pile_top.png` and `seed_pile_side.png` are cut from the user's own picture of a seed block (16x16 each; the side is brightened to match the top); breaking a pile drops its layers as wheat seeds). The colony counts it in `food` (saved; `/antcolonies list` shows it). Items underground are never fetched again, and workers ignore seeds once the store is full (25 blocks x 8 = 200 seeds). Game test `workersCarrySeedsToTheFoodStore`. M6 step 2, clipping seeds off short grass (waiting for the user to confirm in-game): workers also walk to tufts of `short_grass` within 24 blocks of the hole (`ClipGrassGoal`, `Colony.findGrass` looks at 24 random spots), nibble about 2.5 seconds, and get one wheat seed that `StoreSeedGoal` carries down. A tuft gives 10 seeds (`Colony.SEEDS_PER_GRASS`, user's request) and then breaks without dropping anything; the count per tuft is saved (`grassClips`); a just-clipped tuft rests 10 seconds. Same rules as seed items: only once the food store is dug out, and not when it is full. Game test `workersClipSeedsFromGrass`. M6 step 3, the queen and her eggs (the user's design, replaces the first version where the queen walked to the nursery and paid 3 seeds; user confirmed in-game "came out perfectly"): the workers feed the queen (`FeedQueenGoal`: a worker takes one seed off a heap in the food store, `Colony.takeSeedAt`, carries it to the queen in her room and gives it to her, `Colony.feedQueen`; any kind of seed, every seed is one layer of a heap). Only when the queen sits in her finished room, she carries no egg, the store has seeds and there are fewer than 10 eggs (`Colony.needsFeeding`, `MAX_EGGS`); the workers on their way are counted so she is never given more than 20. At 20 seeds (`Colony.QUEEN_MEAL`) she carries an egg for 3600 ticks (`INCUBATION_TICKS`, 3 minutes at normal speed, the user changed it from 5 after testing; saved as the game time when it is due, so it keeps going after a reload). She is not fed while she carries it. Then the egg comes out behind her (`QueenAntEntity.customServerAiStep`, 0.7 blocks behind where she faces, in her room) and she starts over at 0 seeds. Then a worker (`CarryEggGoal`, above feeding in priority) picks the egg up in its mandibles (drawn by `PelletLayer` as the same pale grain, lying across the mandibles; synced flag `AbstractAntEntity.isCarryingEgg`), carries it to a free spot in the nursery and puts it down (`Colony.placeEgg`). Only once the nursery is dug out. A worker that cannot finish puts the egg down where it stands, and one that dies drops it. `AntEggEntity` (saved with its colony id, disappears when its colony is gone) is the same as before; eggs do nothing else yet. `/antcolonies list` shows `queen fed n/20 (egg in m:ss)`, `eggs` and `food`. Saved per colony: `queenSeeds`, `eggDue`, `eggsLaid`. Game test `queenIsFedAndHerEggGoesToTheNursery` (the seed tests count `Colony` food + queen seeds + 20 per egg laid). Grass clipping now scans the whole 24-block area every 5 seconds (`grassTufts`) instead of looking at random spots. M6 step 4, eggs hatch into workers (user confirmed in-game "works perfectly"): an egg starts a clock once it lies on a nursery floor cell (`AntEggEntity`, saved as `hatchAt`; so the clock starts only after a worker has carried it there, and also for eggs from older saves). After `Colony.HATCH_TICKS` (2400 ticks, 2 minutes at normal speed; my pick, the user gave no number) the egg is replaced by a real worker (`Colony.hatchEgg`, `WorkerAntEntity.joinColony`) that belongs to the colony and is counted in its headcount. Only while the colony has fewer than `Colony.MAX_WORKERS` (20, my pick) workers; otherwise the egg waits in the nursery until a worker dies. Game tests `eggsInTheNurseryHatchIntoWorkers` and `eggsWaitWhenTheColonyHasEnoughWorkers`. Still open for M6: nest stages (rooms dug only at the right stage) and the colony growing from food and workers.
- Bug fix, ant going up and down one block for minutes (user report, user confirmed in-game "fixed it fully"): found with the user's debug log and a new test. When an ant planned its route while hanging in the air above a pit or ledge (for example after stepping off the edge of the hill), its route started in that air cell. Climbing back out over the wall passes through that same cell, and the walker read it as "back at the start of the route", so it walked back down and climbed again for ever; the stuck check never fired because the ant kept moving. Now the cell above the start of an up-and-over stretch is never read as an earlier waypoint (`AntNavigator`, `cornerOf`), and a safety net replans when the ant reaches no new waypoint for 160 ticks (8 seconds, `CIRCLE_TICKS`; after 3 replans the walk counts as failed as before). Game tests `antClimbsOutOfAOneBlockPit`, `antPlanningAboveAPitFallsInAndClimbsOut`, and four scans that put 440 ants around a hill and send them into the food store (`antsFromAllAroundTheHillReachTheFoodStore`, starting anywhere in their cell, bumpy ground, half-built hill).
- M6 step 5, rooms usable before they are finished, direct feeding and worker roles (user's request, waiting for the user to confirm in-game). Decision (user): all three rooms are still dug right away; nest stages (rooms only at the right stage) are postponed, and the queen's nuptial flight, wing shedding, own hole and 3 eggs from her stored fat are planned as M9 (the first workers of a new colony would come from those eggs). (1) A room does not have to be finished to be used: `Colony.getFoodStoreCells` / `getNurseryCells` / `getQueenRoomCells` list every dug-out floor cell that joins up with the tunnel, once the shaft and the tunnel to that room are dug. Seeds go to any such cell of the food store. Eggs go to the nursery if any of it is dug, otherwise to the queen's room (`Colony.pickEggSpot`); a worker marks an egg as placed (`AntEggEntity.placed`, saved) and it hatches from then on; a placed egg is not moved when the nursery is dug later. (2) Seeds from outside are fetched when the food store has a free spot or the queen still wants seeds (`Colony.wantsSeeds`); an ant that takes a seed while the queen wants one carries it straight to her (`isSeedForQueen`, counted in `feeders`, `FeedQueenGoal` carries on), otherwise it stores it. Nothing is put down without a store: ants only fetch seeds that have a place to go. `needsFeeding` no longer needs seeds in the store; the store branch of `FeedQueenGoal` does. (3) Roles (`ColonyRole`: BUILDER, FORAGER, NURSE; `Colony.roleOf`, not saved): from 3 workers on one nurse, the rest half builders (first) and half foragers. Builders dig and seal (`DigGoal`, `SealGoal`), foragers gather and clip (`GatherSeedGoal`, `ClipGrassGoal`), the nurse carries eggs and feeds the queen from the store (`CarryEggGoal`, `FeedQueenGoal`) and otherwise waits by the queen (`StandByQueenGoal`). A worker with nothing to do for 5 seconds (`AbstractAntEntity.isIdle`, `mayDo`) helps with any role's work; carrying jobs already started (pellet, seed, egg) are finished by whoever holds them. Game tests `workersFeedTheQueenBeforeTheFoodStoreExists`, `eggsGoToTheQueensRoomWhenThereIsNoNursery`, `workersShareTheJobsAndTheNurseStaysByTheQueen`. Still open for M6: the colony growing from food and workers (the 20-worker cap).
- Bug fix, ants hovering at the foot of a wall before climbing out of the hole (user report, user confirmed in-game): an ant that reached the bottom of a straight climb (the shaft, the hill funnel) slid sideways to the wall while already counted as climbing, so it leaned against the wall (model tilted up) while still a block from it. Now it walks to the wall like a normal walk and only starts clinging and rising once it touches it (`AntNavigator`, straight-up stretch, only while `onGround`). The `watch` helper in `NavigationTests` now fails a test if an ant leans on a wall it has not reached for more than 8 ticks.
- Hover fix confirmed in-game by the user. Plan change (user): M7 termites wait until the red harvester ant is polished (M6.5 above); ant bread is the first pick.
- Ant bread, queen part (user's design, waiting for the user to confirm in-game): the queen no longer eats raw seeds, she eats **ant bread**. The nurse (`MillBreadGoal`) takes one seed off a heap in the food store (`Colony.takeSeedAt`), carries it to a free spot in the nursery (the queen's room while there is no nursery: `Colony.pickBreadSpot`), stands there and mills it for 3 seconds (`Colony.MILL_TICKS` = 60 ticks, grinding particles and sound), and the spot gets one more piece of bread: a heap of the new **Ant Bread Pile** block (`ModBlocks.BREAD_PILE`, the same layered block class as the seed pile, own brown textures, drops Ant Bread items when broken). 1 seed = 1 bread. The nurses keep one meal (`Colony.BREAD_STOCK` = 20) of bread ready (`needsBread`, `claimMilling`; counts bread piles plus seeds being milled), also while the queen carries an egg. Feeding (`FeedQueenGoal`, nurse) now takes a piece of bread off a heap (`findBreadPile`, `takeBreadAt`), carries it to the queen (shown in the mandibles as the new **Ant Bread** item, `ModItems.ANT_BREAD`, `AbstractAntEntity.isCarryingBread`) and she needs `QUEEN_MEAL` = 20 pieces for an egg as before; if she wants no more the ant puts the bread back on a heap. Raw seeds stay in the food store as a reserve. Before there is a food store, a forager that picks up a seed outside carries it straight to the mill (`Colony.claimSeedForBread`, `AbstractAntEntity.isSeedForBread`; it replaces the old seed-straight-to-the-queen). `Colony.getFinishedRoomCenter` and `getLeaks` now count a pile as dug-out floor (bread can lie in the queen's room). Saved per colony: `queenBread` (old key `queenSeeds` still read), `bread`. `/antcolonies list` shows `bread n (milling m)`. The bread is for the queen only for now; larvae are a later step. Game tests: new `nurseMillsASeedIntoBreadInTheNursery`; the seed and queen tests count bread too (`seedsAccountedFor`); test seeds no longer despawn. All 28 game tests pass.
- Two nurses (user's request): from 8 workers on two of them are nurses (`Colony.SECOND_NURSE_FROM_WORKERS`), from 3 workers on one. Ant bread confirmed in-game by the user.

- Deep Soil (user's idea, waiting for the user to confirm in-game): a new darker block (`ModBlocks.DEEP_SOIL`, textures cut from the user's picture, hardness 0.8, in the `diggable` tag so ants dig it). World generation: `DeepSoilFeature` (registered in `ModFeatures`, switched on for every overworld biome by `data/antcolonies/neoforge/biome_modifier/deep_soil.json`, step underground_ores) turns the 2 to 3 blocks of plain stone right under the dirt into Deep Soil, in every column whose surface is dirt-family (`#minecraft:dirt`: grass, dirt, podzol, mud...), so sand, stone and snow surfaces get none. The thickness (now 5 to 7, user decision for the deep spiral nest, see below; it was 2 to 3 before, `MIN_LAYERS`/`MAX_LAYERS`) is fixed per biome, chosen from the world seed and the biome name. New chunks only (old worlds and already generated chunks do not get it). Not covered by game tests (they use a flat arena); the datapack loads without errors. Nest rooms can now lie in Deep Soil because the nest reads soil from the diggable tag.
- Larvae eat bread and more nurses (user's request, waiting for the user to confirm in-game): an egg now hatches after `Colony.HATCH_TICKS` = 1200 ticks (1 minute, was 2 minutes straight to a worker; my pick) into a **larva** (`AntLarvaEntity`, new entity `ant_larva`, pale grub drawn by `AntLarvaRenderer`, bigger the more it has eaten; saved with its colony id and `fed`; lies still in the same spot as the egg). The nurses feed it (`FeedLarvaGoal`, priority 5, before `FeedQueenGoal`): take one piece of bread off a heap, carry it to a hungry larva no other nurse is on the way to (`Colony.claimHungryLarva`), the larva eats it (particles, eating sound). After `Colony.LARVA_MEAL` = 5 pieces (my pick) the larva grows into a real worker (`Colony.hatchLarva`) as soon as the colony has fewer than `MAX_WORKERS` (20), otherwise it waits. A larva that is already full when the nurse arrives gets the bread put back on a heap. Eggs and larvae together are the "brood" (`countBrood`, cap `MAX_EGGS` = 10 for feeding the queen); both occupy their cell (no bread or egg is put there). The mill now wants one queen meal (20, while there is room for more brood) plus what the larvae still have to eat (`Colony.larvaHunger`). Nurses: 1 from 3 workers, 2 from 8, 3 from 12, 4 from 20 (`Colony.*_NURSE_FROM_WORKERS`). `/antcolonies list` shows `larvae n`. Game tests changed: `eggsBecomeLarvaeAndNursesFeedThemIntoWorkers`, `larvaeWaitWhenTheColonyHasEnoughWorkers`, the no-nursery test now ends with a larva in the queen's room. All 28 pass. No death or hunger for larvae yet (a hungry larva just waits).
- Deep spiral nest, the user's big project (DESIGN DECIDED, step 1 done, steps 2 to 4 not started). Design (user): the nest becomes four rooms stacked at four depths, joined by a spiral staircase tunnel: level 1 (top) the seed store, level 2 the nursery and the bread, level 3 the queen, level 4 (bottom) the dead ants (refuse chamber, comes with the corpse feature). Rooms are dug top-down. Until the queen's own room is dug she uses the first finished room (the seed store) as her home, eggs, bread and larvae go to where she is (like now with no nursery), and she walks down and settles when her room is finished. The soil is too thin for 4 levels (each level 1 block tall plus a 1-block wall = 2 blocks per level, so about 9 to 10 blocks of soft soil are needed), and the user chose to make **Deep Soil much thicker** instead of letting ants dig stone or dropping levels: Deep Soil is now 5 to 7 layers per biome (step 1, `DeepSoilFeature.MIN_LAYERS/MAX_LAYERS`; new chunks only). Still keep the adaptive fallback (fewer levels if the soil is thin, ants never dig stone). Steps: (2) new `NestPlan` with the stacked levels and spiral, `Colony.chooseLayout` for several levels, the room lookups (`getFoodStoreCells`, `getNurseryCells`, `getQueenRoomCells`), game tests; (3) the queen's temporary home and move down; (4) refuse chamber and corpses.
- Deep spiral nest, steps 2 and 3 DONE (user's request, waiting for the user to confirm in-game). The nest now has up to 4 levels stacked 2 blocks apart (a 1-block room, a 1-block wall of soil), joined by a spiral staircase (`NestPlan`): top to bottom the seed store (`Kind.FOOD`), the nursery, the queen's room, the refuse chamber (`Kind.REFUSE`, empty for now). Each level has a hub (the shaft bottom for level 1); each stair is 3 blocks (one step sideways from the hub, then 2 straight down, which is the next hub) and the stairs turn a quarter each, so the hubs sit on a 2x2 square and wind round it; each hub has its own 3-block tunnel and 5x5 room toward the same side, so the rooms lie one above the other (my pick for the spiral shape and spacing, the user can change them). Dug top-down (`Colony.findDigTarget`: shaft, then each level's stair, tunnel and room). Whole nest 124 blocks. Fewer levels where the soil is thin (`Colony.chooseLayout`: a level counts only if its stair, tunnel and room are all soil and so are all levels above; most levels wins, depth 3 before 2; with 3 levels the uses are seeds, nursery, queen; with 2 seeds and queen; with 1 only the queen's room; ants never dig stone). Queen's temporary home (step 3): her home is the first finished room (the seed store) until her own room is finished (`Colony.getHomeChamber`, `getFinishedRoomCenter` now means her home); then she walks down (`QueenToRoomGoal`). While she lives there eggs and bread go to the nursery if it is dug, otherwise to her home room (`getHomeRoomCells`); bread is found in every room (`findBreadPile`); eggs and larvae keep seed heaps out of their cells. Game tests: `NestPlanTest` (unit), `workersDigTheWholeNestAndBuildTheMound` (124 cells, 4 levels), `nestSitsOneBlockHigherWhenTheStoneIsTooClose` (3 levels), `theNestHasFewerLevelsWhenTheSoilIsThin` (1 level), `queenLivesInTheSeedStoreUntilHerOwnRoomIsDug` (replaces the old "feed the queen before the food store exists" test), navigation tests down to the refuse chamber and back (the test arena ground is now 12 blocks deep). All 31 pass. Step 4 (refuse chamber use, corpses) is not started. Note for old worlds: existing nests keep their old shape only if they were already planned; new colonies get the spiral (their soil must be thick enough; new worlds have Deep Soil).
- Room sizes per kind and the queen's home (user's request, waiting for the user to confirm in-game; it replaces the first 3x3 / 4x4 / 7x7 try and the "queen lives in the seed store" rule of the bullet above). The seed store and the nursery are 5x5, the queen's room is 3x3, the refuse chamber stays 5x5. Sizes live in `NestPlan.roomSize(kind)` (a `RoomSize(length, width)`), still in code and not yet in the species profile. The tunnel enters the middle of the room's first row (an even width would get one more column on the clockwise side; none is used now). The whole nest is 108 blocks (3 shaft + 4 tunnels of 3 + rooms 25 + 25 + 9 + 25 + 3 stairs of 3). The queen's temporary home is now the NURSERY, because the bread is there (user's decision): until her own room is finished she walks down to the nursery as soon as it is finished and lives there, and she does not stop in the seed store (`Colony.getHomeChamber`; only where the soil is too thin for a nursery the first finished room is her home, as before). The nest is still dug top-down (shaft, seed store, nursery, queen), so she waits (and digs, if she is alone) until the nursery is done. With thin soil the plan is checked length by length (`Colony.chooseLayout`): the last level of a shorter nest is always the small 3x3 queen's room and lies inside the room it replaces, so it fits wherever the bigger one would. Unit test `NestPlanTest` checks the sizes, the rectangles and the tunnel entering the middle; game tests changed in their cell counts (`NEST_CELLS` = 108), the lone queen (digs shaft + seed store + nursery, settles in the nursery) and `queenLivesInTheNurseryUntilHerOwnRoomIsDug`.
- Repairs: nest walls and the ant hill (user's request "add the repair framework from before when anything becomes exposed to outside and make it so they can repair their anthill", waiting for the user to confirm in-game). There was no separate "repair framework" in the code; the user meant the sealing (`SealGoal`, `Colony.getLeaks`), which is live all the time: any open block that touches a dug-out nest cell is packed shut by builders, whenever it appears (a player breaks a wall, a cave opens). New: (1) the limit "a hole right next to the top of the shaft cannot be reached" is gone: a hole may now also be closed from a nest cell with no floor but a solid wall to cling to (`Colony.findPlugSpot`: floors first, then walls), so holes beside the shaft or the middle of a stair get shut too. (2) Hill repair: `Colony.openMoundSpots` also finds gaps in the middle of a column (a block knocked out while the blocks above it still hang there; they count only if a hill block is still above the gap). `RepairHillGoal` (workers, priority 8, builders or idle workers, not the queen): once the nest is fully dug (`findDigTarget == null`; before that the digging makes the soil) and the hill has a gap no pellet is on its way to, an ant climbs into the shaft right under the hole (`Colony.getRepairSoilSpot`), scrapes for 1.5 seconds (dirt particles and sound, no block is removed from the nest) and takes a pellet; the normal `CarryPelletGoal` then carries it out and sets it in the gap (lowest gap first). At most 3 ants fetch soil at once and never more than there are gaps (`Colony.needsHillRepair`, `claimHillRepair`, claims last 15 seconds); if a pellet cannot reach its gap (no route) no more repair soil is fetched for a minute (`pauseHillRepair`). Not saved (nothing needs saving: a gap is simply found again after a reload). Game test `workersMendTheHillAndTheNestWallsAfterDamage` (9 hill blocks knocked out in 6 columns, 5 wall holes, all mended; 32 tests pass). Not done: a block placed into the entrance hole itself, a damaged ground around the hill, repairing player-placed stone in the nest, corpses (step 4 of the spiral).
- Bug fix, ants stuck and hovering in the shaft (user report with a picture: ants stall in the shaft when they come up, they "levitate back and forth" instead of sticking to the wall, maybe when a hill block is broken and they come up to repair; user's guess confirmed). Found with per-tick logs of the repair test. Three causes, all in the legs (`AntNavigator`, `AbstractAntEntity`): (1) when a walk ended with the ant hanging in the shaft (the repair ant's soil spot right under the hole, or after a pellet was put in the hill from inside the shaft) the walker stopped climbing and the ant fell to the bottom, climbed up again and fell again about 15 times before it had scraped for 30 ticks. Now an ant whose walk has ended in a cell without a floor, with a wall beside it, keeps clinging to that wall (`holdOnToWall`; a goal cell without a floor counts as reached as soon as the ant is in it). (2) The wall was chosen cell by cell, so where the shaft bottom has openings (tunnel, stair) the ant hugged one wall there, then slid across the shaft in mid-air to another wall in the next cell without rising (8 to 14 ticks, every time). Now one wall is chosen for the whole straight climb (the wall it will climb out over if that is solid along the whole climb, else a side wall of it, `chooseWall`), and an ant in the air keeps rising while it slides to its wall instead of waiting. (3) An ant that had just come off a stair onto the floor of the shaft bottom counted as "in the air" (it was not pressing on the floor), so it slid along the floor tilted upright; now "on the floor" is checked in the world (`standsOnFloor`). The legs animation no longer plays for an ant that hangs still (`AbstractAntEntity.animate` uses the real height change). Game tests: every walk now fails if a climbing ant hangs in the air without rising for more than 4 ticks (`watch` in `NavigationTests`); `antsClimbFromThe...ThroughTheFinishedHill` (four ants per room, one for each side the hill can be left over, rooms 1 to 4); `antStaysOnTheShaftWallWhenItsWalkEndsInTheShaft`; `antStoppedHalfWayUpTheShaftStaysOnTheWall`. The brood tests now count only workers of their own colony (one of them failed once because a worker had wandered out of the test area). Not verified in-game yet.
- Climbing and hovering fix confirmed in-game by the user ("works perfectly"; the repairs worked too).
- Deep spiral nest, step 4: the refuse chamber and dead ants (user's request "lets move to step 4", CONFIRMED in-game by the user). A worker that dies leaves its body (`AntCorpseEntity`, new entity `ant_corpse`, saved with its colony id and a `placed` flag; drawn by `AntCorpseRenderer`: a dark, dried-out ant on its back with six curled legs, texture `ant_corpse.png`; spawned in `WorkerAntEntity.remove` when it is removed as killed, so also after `/kill`; a worker that dies carrying a body drops it). Workers carry the bodies to the refuse chamber (`CarryCorpseGoal`, priority 6, copy of the egg carrying: the builders' job, other roles when idle; fetch the nearest unclaimed body, carry it in the mandibles (`AbstractAntEntity.isCarryingCorpse`, synced, drawn smaller by `PelletLayer`), set it down on a free floor cell of the refuse chamber, one body to a cell, 25 at most (`Colony.pickCorpseSpot`, `placeCorpse`, `findLooseCorpse`, `claimCorpse`; `getRefuseCells`, `getRefuseCorpses`). A body in the chamber stays for good. A body nobody can carry (no refuse chamber dug yet or none planned where the soil is thin, the chamber full, no route, farther than 24 blocks from the hole) crumbles to dust after `Colony.CORPSE_DECAY_TICKS` = 6000 ticks (5 minutes at normal speed, my pick, so nothing lies about for ever). `/antcolonies list` shows `dead ants n (refuse chamber m)`. The queen leaves no body (the colony ends with her). Game tests `workersCarryTheDeadToTheRefuseChamber` (3 bodies outside, 1 in the nursery, 1 killed worker, all end up in the chamber, one per cell) and `deadAntsNobodyCanCarryAwayCrumbleToDust` (thin soil, no chamber); `Colony.eggArea` was renamed `nestArea` (it also finds bodies). All 40 game tests pass. Not done: seed husks in the refuse chamber, a refuse pile outside where there is no chamber, dead enemies (ants of other colonies, termites) as bodies, hunger deaths of larvae.
- Rich ant bread and the egg burst (user's design, CONFIRMED in-game by the user; replaces the first "seed kinds with buffs" idea). Only pumpkin seeds are rich for now (item tag `antcolonies:rich_seeds`, so beetroot or melon seeds can be added in `data/antcolonies/tags/item/rich_seeds.json`; the user first said pumpkin, beetroot and melon, then "pumpkin in each piece": pumpkin only is my reading, to confirm). Rich seeds are counted separately from plain ones (`Colony.richSeeds`, `richBread`; the heaps in the world look the same, only the colony counts), the nurses mill a rich seed into a piece of **Rich Ant Bread** (new item `rich_ant_bread`, shown in the mandibles) while the queen still needs rich bread (`Colony.wantsRichBread`), and the queen is fed rich bread first (`takeBreadAt(.., wantRich)`); larvae get plain bread (rich only if nothing else is left). 5 pieces of rich bread (`Colony.RICH_MEAL`) start a burst: she lays one egg a minute (`BURST_INTERVAL_TICKS` 1200) for 5 eggs (`BURST_EGGS`) and needs no more food meanwhile; the first egg comes a minute after the 5th piece. The limit of 10 young (`MAX_EGGS`) stays: a burst egg waits while there are 10 eggs and larvae. Plain bread still works as before (20 pieces, one egg, 3 minutes). `/antcolonies list` shows `food n (rich r)`, `bread n (rich r, milling m)`, `queen fed x/20, rich y/5` and `burst: n left`. Saved per colony: `richSeeds`, `richBread`, `queenRich`, `burstLeft`. Game test `richBreadStartsABurstOfEggs` (5 eggs exactly 1200 ticks apart); 41 tests pass. NEXT (decided by the user, not built): every worker eats 1 seed a day (the user changed it from 2); still to settle: what happens to a worker that cannot eat (my suggestion: it slows down, then dies after a long time and leaves a body) and where they eat (my suggestion: raw seeds from the food store).
- Worker hunger and beetroot as a rich seed (user's request, CONFIRMED in-game by the user). Rich seeds are now pumpkin AND beetroot (`rich_seeds.json`). Every worker eats one raw seed a day (`WorkerAntEntity`, `lastAte` saved as a game time; a day = 24000 ticks, `MEAL_INTERVAL_TICKS`): a hungry worker (`EatSeedGoal`, priority 1, only with empty mandibles) walks to a heap in the food store, takes one seed (plain ones first, `Colony.eatSeedAt`, counted in `seedsEaten`), eats it for 1.5 seconds with the seed in its mandibles, and is full for another day. A worker that has not eaten for a day and a half (`SLOW_AFTER_TICKS` 36000) moves at half speed; after three days (`STARVE_AFTER_TICKS` 72000) it starves to death and leaves a body (so it goes to the refuse chamber). The clock only runs for a worker in a colony; a new worker starts full. A colony with no seeds in a store just leaves its workers hungry. `/antcolonies list` shows `food n (rich r, eaten e)`. In the game tests the colonies do not get hungry (`Colony.setHungerOn(false)` in `startColony`), the two hunger tests turn it on: `aHungryWorkerEatsOneSeedFromTheFoodStore`, `aStarvingWorkerSlowsDownAndDies`. One flaky timing test (`queenIsFedAndHerEggGoesToTheNursery`, a nurse can pick the egg up in the very tick it is laid) was made robust. 43 tests pass.
- Colony starvation (user's design, CONFIRMED in-game by the user): a larva that has not been fed for 2 days (`Colony.LARVA_STARVE_TICKS` 48000) dies and leaves a body for the workers to carry to the refuse chamber (`Colony.starveLarva`; it reuses the dead-ant body, a placeholder look). The clock is `AntLarvaEntity.lastFed` (saved), restarts at each piece of bread, and does not run for a full larva waiting to grow up. The queen dies after 5 days without bread (`QUEEN_STARVE_TICKS` 120000, `Colony.queenStarves`, `queenLastFed` saved); her clock only runs while she wants food and gets none (it restarts when she is fed and for as long as she carries an egg, has her full meal, or the nursery holds 10 young). When she dies the colony ends as before (`QueenAntEntity.die`), eggs and larvae go, and the nest stays in the ground. Eggs do not starve. `/antcolonies list` shows `queen unfed m of 100 min`. In the game tests the colonies have hunger off; tests `aLarvaThatIsNotFedForTwoDaysStarves`, `aQueenThatIsNotFedForFiveDaysStarves`. Abandoned nests that new queens can move into are noted as a LATER idea in "Nest growth stages". Workers of a colony that lost its queen are not changed (they keep wandering without a colony).
- Bug fix, ants jammed at a gap in the hill (user report with F3 and the log: spot 47, 66, 41 next to the hole, 4 to 12 ants in the same cell, all giving up after 20 seconds, then fresh ants fetching soil again; CONFIRMED in-game by the user). Cause: ants do not push each other, every ant with a pellet walked to the same spot, and the gap lies where every ant leaving the hole passes, so someone always stood in the cell and `Colony.placePellet` (which refuses when any living thing is inside) never got a free moment. Fix: (1) one ant to a spot: `Colony.pickMoundSpot(level, random, ant)` gives each carrying ant its own claim (`moundClaims`, 400 ticks, released in `CarryPelletGoal.stop`); the others pick another spot or put their soil down. (2) `placePellet` nudges ants standing in the spot out onto the new block (`nudgeOut`); a player or animal in the spot still stops it, and the ant then waits instead of picking again. (3) `CarryPelletGoal` logs `[pellet] ... gave up` lines when an ant gives up (kept for bug reports). Game tests `manyAntsWithPelletsAndOneGapDoNotJam` (never failed before the fix, so it does not prove the bug) and `aPelletIsSetEvenWhenAntsStandInTheGap`. 47 tests pass.
- Bug fix, nest plan lost on reload (found in a code review, CONFIRMED in-game by the user): the room layout was not saved and was worked out again from the ground after every reload; a heap of seeds or bread in a room counted as "in the way", so the colony picked a new direction and started a second nest (proven by a test that failed first: rooms toward north became rooms toward east). Now the plan (shaft depth, direction, number of levels) is saved with the colony (`nestDepth`, `nestDirection`, `nestLevels` in `Colony.save/load`), and for old saves without it, heaps of seeds and bread count as dug out when the plan is worked out again (`allSoilOrAir`). Game test `theNestPlanIsTheSameAfterAReload`. 48 tests pass.
- Carried loads are saved (code review step 2, CONFIRMED in-game by the user): what an ant holds in its mandibles (soil pellet, seed and whether it is for the mill, plain or rich bread, egg, dead ant) is written to its save (`AbstractAntEntity.addAdditionalSaveData`: `CarriedBlock`, `CarriedSeed`, `CarryingEgg`, `CarryingCorpse`, `SeedForBread`). After a reload the job carries on, because every carrying goal already resumes when the ant holds its load. A seed that was being eaten goes back to the store (the ant eats again). Game test `whatAnAntCarriesIsKeptAfterAReload`. 49 tests pass.
- M7 step 1, species profiles (waiting for the user to confirm nothing changed in-game): the red harvester ant's colony numbers now live in `data/antcolonies/species/red_harvester_ant.json` (groups `colony`, `worker`, `queen`, `brood`, `food`, `refuse`; times in ticks, 20 = 1 second, a day = 24000). Loaded when the world starts and on `/reload` (`species/SpeciesProfiles`, a data reload listener); a colony reads its own with `colony.profile()` (`species/SpeciesProfile`), so a termite colony will read its own file. A missing or broken file logs an error and falls back to the same numbers built into `SpeciesProfile.RED_HARVESTER_ANT`. The old constants (`QueenCare.QUEEN_MEAL`, `Brood.MAX_EGGS`, `Colony.MAX_WORKERS`, the nurse thresholds, `FoodStore.FOOD_RADIUS`, `Bakery.MILL_TICKS`, worker hunger times, ...) are gone; `Brood.isFull` replaces "count >= MAX_EGGS"; the larva's meal is synced to the client so its drawn size follows the file. Not in the profile yet (still in code): nest shape and room sizes (`NestPlan`, `Nest`), the hill, caste size/health/speed (fixed when the game starts), and the block/item tags. Unit test `SpeciesProfileTest` checks the file reads.
- M7 step 2, termite king and queen (waiting for the user to confirm in-game): two new mobs with spawn eggs, `termite_king` (`TermiteKingEntity`, caste `king`) and `termite_queen` (`TermiteQueenEntity`), species `antcolonies:macrotermes` (`species/Species`, profile `macrotermes.json`; only its `nest` and `queen_body` numbers are used so far, the rest are placeholders). The queen founds the colony where she is put down; a king joins the nearest termite queen without a king within 32 blocks (worker ants now only join colonies of their own species: `ColonyManager.findNearest(.., species)`). The pair digs a founding nest (the profile's new `nest.max_levels` = 1: a 3-deep shaft, a 3-block tunnel and a 3x3 royal chamber, 15 blocks; `Nest.chooseLayout` reads it, the ant keeps 4), then, while the colony has no other members (`nest.seal_while_founding`), the king (the queen if there is no king) climbs under the hole and packs it shut with Burrowed Dirt (`SealEntranceGoal`; the hole is the ground-level block above the shaft, `Nest.getEntrance`, not part of the plan, so nobody digs it again; reopened by a player, it is closed again). The queen settles in the middle of the chamber (`QueenToRoomGoal`), the king on the floor beside her (`StayWithQueenGoal`). Neither makes soil pellets (no mound until workers, step 3). The queen swells (profile's new optional `queen_body`: size 0 to 1 from the colony's members besides the king, `full_size_at_members` 60, at most `growth_per_day` 0.25, never shrinks; `too_big_to_walk_at` 0.5: from then on she cannot move, `isImmobile`); her size is saved (`Size`), synced, and grows her hitbox. `/antcolonies queensize <0-100>` sets the nearest termite queen's size (she stays at it, she only grows). Models: placeholders from `tools/termite_models.py` (rerun it to rebuild `termite_king`/`termite_queen` geo, animation and textures): dark brown with eyes (only royals have them), wing stubs, beaded antennae, no ant waist; the queen's abdomen is 6 segments, each a pale skin with a dark back plate and a lighter belly plate; `TermiteQueenModel` stretches the skins (4.4x longer, 2.9x wider, 3.1x taller at full size) while the plates keep their size, so they drift apart into brown islands; the abdomen sinks to the floor and slow waves run along it (the pulse, in code, stronger the bigger she is). Drawn at 0.75 scale: king about 0.4 block long, full queen about 1 block. Model fixes after the user's first look (step 2 confirmed working in-game): the young queen's plates now cover her completely (she looks like the king, with thin golden seams; the pale skin shows only as she swells), small dark triangular wing stubs at the sides of the thorax, legs splayed (front pair forward, middle out, back pair back, by turning the cubes), antennae held forward with the tips curving down. Then (user: "too wide and stubby") the full-grown queen was made about 2 blocks long and about 4.5 times as long as wide (real proportions; `GROW_LENGTH` 9.5, `GROW_WIDTH` 3.0, `GROW_HEIGHT` 3.4), her skin is two crossed boxes so she looks round, the first segment narrows into the thorax, and her whole body slides forward as she grows so she stays centred on her spot (her tail would otherwise go through the chamber wall). Her hitbox stays square (at most 0.8 wide), so the ends of a full-grown queen can be walked through. Then (user, with a photo of a real queen) she settles as she grows too heavy: between size 0.45 and 0.65 her abdomen comes to rest on the floor and her head, thorax and legs rise to sit halfway up its front end, legs dangling off the ground (`TermiteQueenModel` `SETTLE_FROM`/`SETTLE_TO`, lifts the `body` bone and lowers the segments by as much). This matches the size where she stops walking (`too_big_to_walk_at` 0.5). From 65% to 75% (user: "the same arch for 75% as well") her front (new bone `forebody`, holding head, thorax and legs, jointed where the thorax meets the abdomen; the king has it too) tips forward and down up to 30 degrees and stays so, hanging off the abdomen with the front feet just above the floor (`DROOP_FROM`, `DROOP_TO`, `DROOP_DEGREES`; the front sits 55% of the way up the abdomen, `THORAX_HOLD`). The model checker is now `references/termites/preview/` with one labelled, zoomed picture per camera angle: `front.png` (her face), `front_above.png`, `side.png`, `top.png`, each with the king and the queen at 0, 50, 75 and 100%. Walk (user: "walking moves their legs"): a tripod gait like real insects (front-left, middle-right and back-left step together, then the other three), each leg swinging forward lifted off the ground and pushing back on it (`walk_legs` in the script, `WALK_SECONDS`, `STEP_SWING` 25, `STEP_LIFT` 22; Z tilt negative lifts a left leg, positive a right one, worked out from GeckoLib's mirrored X and not yet seen in-game); `preview/walk.png` shows four moments of a step from the front and from above. The ant's own walk only swings the legs flat and has the two sides out of step (left 1 and 3 with right 1 and 3), worth the same fix when the real ant model is made (M2). The script also draws preview pictures so shapes can be checked without starting the game (see below). Reference pictures in `references/termites/` (AI images, see the step 2 discussion: the soldier's body should be pale, the king smaller). `Colony.getQueen` now returns any queen (`AbstractAntEntity`). Game tests `TermiteTests`: `royalPairDigsTheirChamberAndSealsTheEntrance`, `aQueenWithoutAKingSealsTheEntranceHerself`, `aSwollenQueenCanNoLongerWalk`.
- M7 step 3a, termite workers and the founding batch (waiting for the user to confirm in-game): new mob `termite_worker` (`TermiteWorkerEntity`, caste `worker`, spawn egg; model `termite_worker` from `tools/termite_models.py`: the king's skeleton made pale cream with a yellowish head and a grey abdomen, no eyes, no wing stubs, 0.85 size; uses the king's animations) and the young `termite_nymph` (a second entity type of `AntLarvaEntity`, drawn by `TermiteNymphRenderer` as a small pale termite that grows as it is fed; `Brood` picks larva or nymph, ant or termite worker by the colony's species). Profile's new optional `founding` (macrotermes: `eggs` 5, `egg_interval_ticks` 400, `feed_interval_ticks` 200): once the pair are sealed in and the queen lies in her chamber, she lays the batch one egg at a time (`TermiteQueenEntity.foundingBatch`, `QueenCare.layFoundingEgg`, eggs placed at once on free chamber cells, `NextFoundingEgg` saved), eggs hatch after `brood.hatch_ticks` into nymphs, and the royal pair feed the hungriest nymph one piece every `feed_interval_ticks` from their own fat (no food store needed) until it has its `larva_meal` and grows into a termite worker (capped by `max_workers`). `Colony.isFounding()` = no members but the queen and kings: with the first worker the founding is over, the plug in the entrance becomes the first thing to dig (`Nest.findDigTarget`, `isEntrancePlugged`, `canDig`: the plug counts as diggable although Burrowed Dirt is not in the tag; the worker bites it from the top of the shaft, `standSpotsFor`; `DigGoal` counts an ant clinging right under its block as within reach, the plug's centre is 1.5 blocks from its feet), and nobody seals it again. Workers then dig nothing else yet and wander near the nest; they make no pellets (`canPlaceBlocks` false) until the mound (3b). No termite corpses yet (a dead termite worker just leaves the headcount). Game test `theFoundingBatchGrowsIntoWorkersWhoOpenTheEntrance`.
- Bug fix, ants and termites floating across the shaft when they get a new job (user report with pictures; the user tested it: the crawl round the corners looks unnatural, ants slide sideways, spin round 180 degrees while climbing and still float at times, so a proper climbing rewrite is being planned, see below): an ant hanging on one wall of a shaft (after a walk ended there, or biting the entrance plug) that got a new route picked the wall it wanted (the one it climbs out over, often the opposite one) and slid straight across the open shaft to it. Now (user's idea) it crawls over the walls: along the wall it is on into the corner, round onto the next wall, and on to the one it needs (from the opposite wall by a side wall), and only rises while the wall it crawls on goes on above it (`AntNavigator.crawlAim`, `CLING_GAP`; it keeps facing the wall it is on, so the model stays leaning on it). Game tests `antHangingAtTheTopOfTheShaftCrawlsRoundToTheOtherWall` and `antHangingHalfWayUpTheShaftCrawlsRoundToTheOtherWall` (both failed with "floating while climbing" before the fix); the `watch` hover check now also accepts an ant crawling sideways along its wall. 55 tests pass.
- Ant queen founds her colony herself (user's design, CONFIRMED in-game by the user). Decisions (user): the queen first digs only a small **founding chamber** of her own, **2 blocks down** (a 2-block shaft, then a 1-block tunnel and a 3x3 room, 1 block of soil as its roof, leaving the ground below for the spiral); it is her home **for now**, and when the workers have dug her deep room (level 3 of the spiral, as before) she walks down to it (`Nest.getHomeChamber`: her deep room once finished, until then the founding chamber; the nursery-as-home rule is only kept for nests planned before this). She **seals herself in and raises 3 eggs on her own fat**, like the termite pair. The workers then open the entrance, dig the rest of the shaft and the spiral (seed store, nursery, queen's deep room, refuse chamber, in that order) and start the hill. Code: `NestPlan.foundingChamber` (kind `FOUNDING`, toward the opposite side from the rooms, so it never touches a level or a stair: unit test `theFoundingChamberStaysClearOfTheLevels`), profile `nest.founding_chamber` (true for the ant, default false; termites keep their one-level royal chamber), the ant profile now has `seal_while_founding: true` and `founding {eggs 3, egg_interval_ticks 400, feed_interval_ticks 200}`. `Nest.findDigTarget` digs the shaft 2 down and the founding chamber first, and while the colony is founding (no workers) nothing else. The founding batch moved from the termite queen into the shared `QueenCare.raiseFoundingBatch` (both queens call it; `nextFoundingEgg` saved with the colony now); the fat feeding stops once there are as many workers as batch eggs, or once she has laid eggs beyond the batch. The ant queen got `SealEntranceGoal`, and a founding queen does not starve (`queenStarves`: she lives on her fat). Workers placed with spawn eggs still join at once (then there is no sealing and no founding batch, but the founding chamber is still dug first and is her home). Saved per colony: `nestFounding` (old saves without it have no founding chamber). Game tests: `aLoneQueenFoundsHerColonyAndRaisesHerFirstWorkers` (replaces `aloneQueenDigsHerOwnRoom`: 12 blocks dug, sealed, 3 eggs, 3 workers, entrance open again; about 8000 ticks), `queenLivesInTheFoundingChamberUntilHerOwnRoomIsDug` (replaces the nursery version: she is fed in the founding chamber, her egg is carried down to the nursery), cell counts include the founding chamber (`NEST_CELLS` = 118). Also fixed a crash found by the tests: an ant that let its soil pellet go (no route to any hill spot) could still try to place it in the same tick (`CarryPelletGoal`, NullPointerException). 55 tests pass.
- Surface walking, the climbing rewrite (user's decision, CONFIRMED in-game by the user "works absolutely perfectly"; it replaces the old cell-by-cell climbing and its patches `chooseWall`, `crawlAim`, `holdOnToWall`). User: climbing must look natural (no sliding sideways, no 180-degree spins, no floating), it must keep the same intelligence and be a better base for later minds (marches, scouting, raids), and **ants climb down head first** instead of dropping. Design: an insect always holds a surface, a **foothold** (`nav/Foothold`: a cell plus the surface in it, the floor or one of the 4 walls; ceilings later). The planner (`AntPathFinder`) searches over footholds, and every step of a **route** (`nav/Route`, immutable, shareable) says which surface and what kind of move (`nav/StepKind`): WALK, ONTO_WALL / OFF_WALL (floor to wall and back at the foot of a wall), CLIMB_UP, CLIMB_DOWN (head first), CRAWL_SIDEWAYS, TURN_CORNER (inside corner), ROUND_EDGE (outside edge), OVER_TOP (up and over onto a block), OVER_EDGE (off a floor's edge and down the side), FALL (only where nothing can be climbed down, at most 12 blocks). No move crosses open air, so a route can never float. Costs about 1 per block (climbing 1.1 to 1.2, corners 0.5 to 0.6, over an edge 1.5, a fall 3 + its height). A route into a cell with a floor ends on the floor (never hanging at the foot of a wall); in a shaft it ends on a wall and the insect holds on there. The walker (`AntNavigator`) does nothing by guessing: walking on floors uses the normal walking with gravity; every move on a wall or round an edge is an exact motion along the surface, the body pressed against it (`moveAttached`, `wallPoint`, `pinned`; first it squares the body up inside its cell, because a body a hair over the cell edge caught on the next block and stuck for good: that was the queen's bug in the thin-soil test). Climbing speed = the speed attribute x 0.24 (a worker 0.06 blocks a tick). The legs check every tick that the step is still possible (the foothold it holds, the one it goes to and any cell it passes on the way, `AntPathFinder.passesThrough`) and plan again at once when a block was put in the way (the hill growing, a hole sealed, a player); a move that gets nowhere for 20 ticks counts as blocked and that foothold is avoided for 20 seconds. A mind can give its own costs for one walk (`moveTo(goal, speed, StepCosts)`, kept when the route is planned again; `StepCosts.forbid(cells)` rules cells out): the sealers use it so they never pass through the hole they close. Body (`AbstractAntEntity`, `AntRenderer`): the surface and the heading within a wall (0 up, 180 down, +90 to its left) are synced (`DATA_SURFACE`, `DATA_HEADING`); the client turns the drawn body smoothly toward them (30 degrees a tick, `turnBody`): it tips up onto a wall, turns within the wall to face where it goes (up, sideways, head first down), turns round corners, and tips back onto a floor facing the way it was going. For later minds: `AntNavigator.follow(route)` walks a route someone else planned (a trail, a marching column: plan once, many insects follow; the insect first finds its way onto the route's start), `AntPathFinder.find(.., Predicate<Foothold> isGoal, ..)` searches for any spot a mind accepts (any cell of a room, the nearest unexplored spot), and `StepCosts` lets a mind add its own cost to any move (scent trails, avoiding enemies or falls with brood); `Route.hasFall()`, `cost()`, `cells()` let a mind judge a route. Goals still only say `moveTo(cell)`. Tests: `AntPathFinderTest` rewritten (head first down the shaft, round the shaft walls, round a pillar's edge, falls only where nothing can be climbed, ends on the floor), new game test `antsGoRoundTheOutsideEdgeOfABlock` (8 ants, every direction and both ways, through `follow` with a hand-made route), all other game tests kept (their checks for floating, leaning and hanging still run on every walk). 56 tests pass. Not done: partial floors (farmland, paths, slabs are still not floors: ants avoid farms and village paths).
- Ceilings and holding on after a reload (user's request "fix the small flaw and add it so ants can crawl on blocks upside down", CONFIRMED in-game by the user "works perfectly"). (1) The small flaw: the wall an insect held was not saved, so an ant saved halfway up a shaft dropped to the bottom when its chunk loaded and climbed up again. Now the wall or ceiling it holds is saved with it (`HoldCell`, `HoldSurface`; `AntNavigator.getHold`, `restoreHold`) and it holds on from its first tick; the client draws it on the wall at once instead of tipping up (`turnBody`, first ticks). (2) Ceilings: a foothold can now be the ceiling (`Foothold.ceiling`, surface UP, the underside of the block above). New moves (`StepKind`): ONTO_CEILING (top of a wall onto the ceiling, 0.8), OFF_CEILING (ceiling onto a wall, head first down, 0.8), CRAWL_CEILING (upside down along it, 1.3 a block, `AntPathFinder.CEILING_COST`), UNDER_EDGE (down a block's face and round its bottom edge onto its underside, 2), OUT_FROM_UNDER (from an underside round the edge and up the face, 2), and a FALL from a ceiling (let go). Ceilings are a little dearer than floors and turning over costs extra, so routes stay on floors unless the ceiling is a real shortcut (the first try took a silly flip from the tunnel ceiling up into the shaft to save 0.1; the costs were raised for that). The mind decides when to go up there on purpose: `AntNavigator.moveTo(Foothold, speed)` walks to one exact foothold, and wandering (`AntWanderGoal.hangsFromCeilings`) sends a worker underground (ant and termite workers, 1 stroll in 4, my pick) to a spot on a room or tunnel ceiling, where it hangs upside down until its next job or stroll. Body: the synced surface byte also carries the wall a ceiling heading is measured from (`getCeilingRef`); the drawn body keeps tipping past upright to 180 degrees (upside down, feet on the ceiling at the top of the hitbox, `AntRenderer`), and turns smoothly between wall and ceiling. Tests: `AntPathFinderTest` (to the middle of a room ceiling and back down, floors beat ceilings, under an overhang and out), game tests `antCrawlsUpsideDownToTheMiddleOfTheRoomCeilingAndHangsThere`, `antsGoUnderAnOverhangAndOutTheOtherSide` (4 directions), `antOnTheShaftWallIsStillThereAfterAReload`, `antOnTheCeilingIsStillThereAfterAReload`; the hover check in `watch` counts a ceiling as holding on. All 60 game tests pass. Still open from the same talk: the hunger clocks run on while a chunk is unloaded, so a colony left alone for over an in-game day or two can starve the moment it loads (fix: pause the clocks for now, or "Colonies live offline" later).
- M7 step 3b, part 1: the mound blocks (user's design above, CONFIRMED in-game by the user "everything works"). **Termite Mound Soil** (`ModBlocks.TERMITE_MOUND`, `block/TermiteMoundBlock`): block state `layers` 1 to 5 and `age` 0 to 3. Layers 1 to 4 are 4, 8, 12, 16 pixels high (solid up to there), hardness 0.3, wet and cracked (fewer cracks each layer), and need a solid floor (a finished block stands on its own, so a mound is not undermined by one gap); layer 5 is finished. Placing the item on a block still being built adds a layer (like snow). A finished block ages on random ticks (`AGE_CHANCE` 1 in 20, 45, 130 per random tick, a block gets one about once a minute: setting after about 20 minutes, dry about 50 minutes later, old about 2.5 hours after that; my picks); hardness 0.6, 1.0, 1.5, 2.5, breaking like dirt while fresh or setting (a shovel is quick) and like stone once dry (a pickaxe is needed: `getDestroyProgress` works it out as for dirt or stone with this hardness); sounds mud, packed mud, mud bricks. Drops itself only when finished, dry and old soil only with a pickaxe; a half-built block drops nothing. For the termites' building job (next part): `addLayer(level, pos)` (only on something `canBuildOn` allows: a finished block that has set, or any solid top) and `canBuildOn`. **Termite Chimney** (`ModBlocks.TERMITE_HOLE`, `block/TermiteHoleBlock`): a block of setting-coloured soil with breathing pores and a crown of hollow spires on top (the model reaches 12 pixels above the block); solid for everyone except the creatures in the entity tag `antcolonies:passes_termite_holes` (termite worker, king, queen, nymph), who walk through it; the route planner does not use it as a doorway yet (next part). Both are in the Natural Blocks creative tab. Art: `tools/termite_blocks.py` draws all textures (soil pellets lit from above, side cracks and top mud cracks that fade layer by layer, the ages' colours, flutes on dry and old sides), writes the models and blockstates, and renders the block model checker `references/termites/preview/blocks.png` (every layer, every age, the hole block, and a small sample mound); it needs Python with Pillow. Game tests `TermiteMoundTests`: `aMoundBlockIsBuiltInLayersAndMustSetBeforeMoreIsStackedOnIt`, `onlyTermitesPassThroughTheHoleBlock`.
- M7 step 3b, part 2: termites build the mound (user's design in "Mound plan, step 3b", CONFIRMED in-game by the user). **Shape** (`colony/MoundPlan`, pure geometry, unit test `MoundPlanTest`): stage `h` is a mound `h` blocks tall (1 to the profile's `max_height` 22): a round body, wide at the foot (base radius grows 0.25 a stage up to `base_radius` 6.5) and narrowing into a spire (radius 1.5 + (base - 1.5) x (1 - height share)^1.5, my pick), a hollow chimney shaft up its middle (the nest shaft goes on up through it) capped by a chimney block at the top, and from stage 8, 11 and 14 the side chimneys (`side_chimneys` 3, 4 blocks out toward 3 of the 4 directions, chosen per colony; each rises up to `side_chimney_height` 9, 7, 5 blocks above the slope, with its own shaft and cap); a tunnel at ground level joins every side shaft to the middle one and goes on out to an entrance (a chimney block) at the foot. Full mound: 1211 blocks, 22 tall. Each stage is worked out on its own and the termites reconcile: build what is new, clear what is now hollow (the old cap when a chimney grows, a tunnel cut through the base), shape the new caps. The model checker is `references/termites/preview/mound.png` (stages 3, 9, 14, 22 and the full mound cut in half), drawn by `tools/termite_mound_preview.py` from files `MoundPlanTest` writes to `build/mound-plan/` (run `./gradlew test` first). **How big** (`colony/Mound`, part of the colony like the hill, `colony.mound()`): the colony earns blocks over time, `blocks_per_day` 2 plus `blocks_per_day_per_worker` 0.5 for each worker (my picks: a full mound in about 34 hours with 20 workers, slower with fewer; worked out from the game time, so it also grows while unloaded, saved as `moundAllowance`, `moundUpdated`); the stage is the tallest one whose blocks it has earned (always at least stage 1, an 8-block ring round the entrance with a chimney block in the entrance itself). Nothing is built while the colony is founding. **Soil:** each bite of a dug block gives a pellet, at most 5 per block (one dug block = one mound block, `Mound.takePellet`); the termites first dig the nest, then the mound's clear-out jobs, then galleries, only while more soil is wanted (more build spots than termites bringing soil): `MoundPlan.galleries`, a shaft straight down from the bottom of the nest shaft and `gallery_levels` 3 levels 2 blocks apart below the nest, each with straight galleries along the 4 directions and square rings at distance 3, 5, 7, ... up to `gallery_radius` 13 (enough soil for the full mound; dug nearest first, never stone, never next to water). **Building** (`BuildMoundGoal`, termite workers, priority 1): a termite with a pellet claims a spot (one termite to a spot; blocks being built first, then the lowest, then the nearest), walks to any foothold in the 26 cells round it (new `AntNavigator.moveTo(Predicate<Foothold>, towards, speed)`: from beside it, from the shaft under it or clinging to the outside) and adds a layer (`Mound.addLayer`: termites in the way are lifted onto the new layer, a player or other creature stops it). A wall at the foot over a dip is filled in from the ground up (at most 3 blocks). Over a tunnel (no floor) a block is built out from set soil beside it, like an arch (`TermiteMoundBlock.canStartAt`; a wet block there sticks to set soil beside it, `canSurvive`); on top of wet soil still never. **Chimney blocks** (`ShapeHoleGoal`, priority 2): a cap or an entrance is shaped (2 seconds of work, no soil) once the walls round it are finished and the shaft under it is cleared. **Clearing** goes through `DigGoal` (the old cap is bitten from the shaft under it; its soil goes back into the mound). **Doorway:** the route planner sees the world through the insect's eyes (`LevelCellMap(level, insect)`): a block that lets it through (the chimney block for termites) is an open cell and nothing to stand or cling on; for everyone else it is solid. Test command `/antcolonies mound <height>` (the nearest termite colony within 64 blocks has earned that stage, the termites build up to it) and `/antcolonies mound <height> instant` (the whole mound of that height is put up at once, oldest soil at the foot, to look at the shape); `/antcolonies list` shows `mound height h of 22, finished/blocks, earned`. Game tests in `TermiteMoundTests`: `termitesBuildTheFirstTwoStagesOfTheirMound` (stage 1, then stage 2 with the cap moved up; wet soil is set at once in the test), `aTermiteWalksThroughAChimneyBlockIntoAClosedRoom` (an ant finds no way in), `aBlockOverAGapIsBuiltOutFromSetSoilBesideIt`. Not done yet: the founding pair's royal chamber is still the only room (fungus garden rooms with step 5), termites do not repair a broken mound beyond rebuilding the stage's missing blocks (which they do: a knocked-out wall is simply a build spot again).
- Coming out of the chimney (user's idea, built while the user was away; the user tested it and did not like it: the termite looked like it was squeezing and struggling. Being redone, see the next bullet; this one describes the first version): a termite leaving a chimney block over its top no longer pops up through the block's flat top: it climbs up inside the tall middle spire of the crown until the front of its body is out of the mouth, stops for 1.5 seconds turning its head from side to side (looking round), climbs onto the rim, over it and head first down the outside of the spire, and steps off onto the mound. Going in is the same the other way round (onto the chimney block's top to the foot of the spire, up its outside, over the rim, head first down inside, hidden in the block). Species-neutral, in the legs: a block that implements `nav/Doorway` (its mouth's height and width; `TermiteHoleBlock`: rim 28 pixels up, 4 pixels wide) and lets the insect through changes how the OVER_TOP move out of it and the OVER_EDGE move into it are walked (`AntNavigator.comeOutOf`, `goInto`; such a step may take 400 ticks, `DOORWAY_STEP_TICKS`); the planner and the routes are unchanged. Side entrances at the foot of the mound are walked through as before (the termite vanishes into the block's side). In and on the spire the hitbox is moved 0.13 blocks away from the wall the body is drawn against (`DRAWN_INTO_WALL`, the renderer's push into a wall at the termites' 3/4 size), so the drawn body is in the middle of the mouth; the peek height (`PEEK_DEPTH`) and this offset are worked out from the numbers, not yet seen in-game. Game test `aTermiteComesOutOfTheChimneyMouthAndGoesBackIn` (it must come up the middle of the spire to the rim, look out for at least 20 ticks, reach the top, then go back in by the mouth and down the shaft).
- Coming out of the chimney, second version (user's request after testing the first: "no more squeezing or struggle", the termite pokes its head out, the head tips level and looks left, then right, fully animated, then it crawls out; waiting for the user to confirm in-game). The motion is now a list of points (`nav/DoorwayMotion`: `comeOut`, `goIn`; walked by `AntNavigator.walkDoorway`; unit test `DoorwayMotionTest`). Coming out: up the middle of the spire, slowly at the end, until the head, the front legs and the front of the thorax are out (`PEEK_DEPTH` 0.2 below the rim); then the termite holds completely still for `PEEK_TICKS` 56 (2.8 seconds) and only its head moves, the new `peek` animation (`tools/termite_models.py`, in the king's animation file the workers use): the head tips forward until it faces straight out, level, over the rim, turns 50 degrees to its left, stops, turns to its right, stops, and tips back up; the antennae follow the look and wave; the mandibles open and shut at each stop; every key frame is eased (GeckoLib's `easeInOutSine`). Then it grips the rim, tips forward over it (lying across the top), tips head first onto the outside turning round the rim's edge (the drawn body's middle stays at the edge, so it never cuts into the spire), climbs down and steps onto the mound. Going in: up the outside, rolls over the edge onto the top, across it, head first down the middle of the mouth, no look round. Both tips over an edge turn slowly (15 degrees a tick instead of 30). Body (`AbstractAntEntity`): a synced byte `DATA_POSE_FLAGS` (looking round, turning slowly); the `peek` animation plays while it looks round; climbing legs step slower when the body climbs slowly (they ran in place before, part of the "struggle"). The whole-body wiggle of the first version is gone. Fixed on the way (every insect): tipping from a top onto a wall facing the other way (over a thin wall) spun the body half round, because the body yaw had already turned toward the wall when the tip started, and a body still tipping off one wall turned its wall yaw toward the opposite one through 180 degrees; now the tip starts from how the body lay on the last tick (`floorYaw`), and onto a wall facing the other way it first lies flat, then tips up (`turnBody`). **Picture tool** `tools/chimney_preview.py` (run `./gradlew test` first; it reads `build/chimney/come_out.txt` and `go_in.txt` that `DoorwayMotionTest` writes from the game's own code): replays the server motion, the client's 3-tick updates and smoothing, and the renderer's wall pose, and draws `references/termites/preview/chimney.png` (side view cut open, every 3 ticks), `chimney.gif`, `chimney_closeup.gif` and the same for going in (`chimney_in*`). Unit test `TermiteAnimationsTest` reads the animation file with GeckoLib's own reader (the game tests never load it) and checks the look round is as long as the hold. The game test `aTermiteComesOutOfTheChimneyMouthAndGoesBackIn` checks the look round (on for the whole of it, at the right height, not moving, never on the way in). Not yet done: the user's realism choice that the chimney is only used about 1 trip in 10 (part 1 of the realism pass).
- Blockbench projects (user's folder `Blockbench/`, in git): `Termite.bbmodel` (termite worker, its idle, walk and peek animations) and `harvester_ant_worker.bbmodel` (the ant), both GeckoLib format, mod id `antcolonies`, project names `termite_worker` and `worker_ant` so exports get the file names the mod uses. Filled from the game's own files by `tools/bbmodel_import.py` (bones, cubes, texture embedded, animations with their easing; X mirrored like Blockbench's own importer; checked by turning them back and comparing). Close a file in Blockbench before Claude changes it. The user opened both, they work. Rerun `tools/bbmodel_import.py` after a model script changes (it overwrites both projects).
- Worker ant rebuilt (user's request "a proper rebuild", waiting for the user to confirm in-game; M2's model, made by Claude, the user can reshape it in Blockbench): `tools/ant_models.py` writes `worker_ant` geo, animations and texture, `queen_ant.png` (same layout, deeper red; the queen still uses the worker model, bigger) and preview pictures `references/ants/preview/` (front, front from above, side, top, walk). From photos of Pogonomyrmex barbatus: rusty red, darker shinier gaster, big square head with fine ridges, dark triangular mandibles, a golden beard under the head (psammophore), eyes at the sides, elbowed antennae (scape up and back, then a whip forward with a thicker tip), humped thorax with two spines at the back, a two-node waist (petiole, postpetiole), legs whose knees rise above the back. Per-face UV everywhere (no Blockbench warnings), cube rotations on all three axes for the leg and antenna segments (`rod`). Bones: the termites' layout (`body`, `forebody` with head, thorax, legs `leg_{l,r}{1,2,3}` with `_lower` and `_foot`, antennae `_1` scape and `_2` whip), plus `petiole` > `postpetiole` > `abdomen`. Animations: walk is a tripod gait (each leg pushes back, lifts with a bent knee, swings forward; antennae sweep, gaster sways), idle has antennae feeling about on their own rhythms, a small head turn, the gaster breathing and one mandible snap; all keys eased. New empty bone **`carry`** in the head of every ant and termite model: `PelletLayer` now draws a carried load (pellet, seed, forage, egg, dead ant) at that bone instead of at fixed numbers, so it sits in each model's own jaws (the termites' loads used to float in front of their heads). Unit test `ModelFilesTest` (every model has `carry` in its head; the ant's animations read with GeckoLib's reader and only move bones the ant has). Not yet seen in-game.
- M7 step 4, termites gather food (built while the user was away, every number is my pick, CONFIRMED in-game by the user). **Food store:** the termite nest gets a 5x5 room at the end of a 3-block tunnel off the foot of the shaft, straight across from the royal chamber (profile `nest.side_rooms: ["food"]`, `NestPlan.sideRooms`: up to three such side rooms, across, left and right; they never touch the royal chamber, each other or the mound's galleries, unit test). The founding pair do not dig it: the workers dig it right after opening the entrance, before any gallery. A direction where it fits in the soil is preferred when the nest is planned; otherwise it is dug as far as the soil goes. Termite colonies from older saves get it too. **What they gather** (profile `food`, now per species for both): items lying on the ground (`items`, tag `antcolonies:termite_food`: sticks, saplings, wheat and forage), and plants they cut to pieces where they grow (`plants`: short grass 4 pieces, tall grass 8, dead bush 6, dying leaves 2 (only leaves cut off from their tree, `only_decaying`; they rot away within a minute or so, so this is rare), hay bale 48, with up to 4 termites cutting at it at once (`foragers`); a used-up plant breaks and drops nothing). Each piece is the new **Forage** item (`piece`), carried in the mandibles into the store, where every piece is one layer of a **Forage Pile** (`pile`: the same layered block as the seed pile, dry-grass textures drawn by `tools/forage_textures.py`; breaking it drops Forage). The ant's profile now says the same things for its seeds (`items` the seeds tag, `plants` short grass 10, `piece` wheat seeds, `pile` the seed pile), so nothing changes for the ants, except that an ant now nibbles a tuft from beside it too (needed for solid plants like a hay bale). Termites mill no bread (`mill_ticks` and `bread_stock` left out, `FoodNumbers.makesBread`). **Jobs:** termite workers now get roles like the ants (the role code moved from `WorkerAntEntity` to `AbstractAntEntity`, `hasColonyRole`): half builders (dig, build the mound), half foragers (the ants' `GatherSeedGoal`, `ClipGrassGoal` and `StoreSeedGoal`, now species-neutral); one with nothing to do for 5 seconds helps with the other job. No termite nurses until the fungus garden (`nurses_from_workers` is empty). **Soil:** a bite only gives a soil pellet while the mound wants soil (`Mound.takePellet`), and a termite that finds no spot for its soil for about 15 seconds leaves it behind (`BuildMoundGoal`); otherwise digging the store left termites holding soil with nowhere to put it. The food is only stored for now; the fungus garden (step 5) will turn it into colony food. `/antcolonies list` shows `food n pieces in the store` for termites. Game test `termitesGatherDeadPlantsIntoTheirFoodStore` (every piece cut is in the store, in a termite's mandibles or on the ground; all heaps lie in the store). In it 6 termites dig the store and gather a dead bush, tall and short grass, a whole hay bale, a stick, a sapling and wheat (73 pieces) in about 14500 ticks (12 minutes at normal speed). All 67 game tests pass.
- Realism pass, part 1: exits and the daily schedule (user's decisions in "Realism pass" above; the details are my picks; CONFIRMED in-game by the user "works great").
  - **Foraging tunnels and holes** (`NestPlan.foragingTunnels`, profile `nest.foraging_holes` 2 and `nest.foraging_hole_distance` 8): two shallow tunnels leave the entrance shaft 2 blocks under the entrance cell and run straight out just under the surface, one to each side the royal chamber and the food store leave free, 8 blocks (beyond the foot of a full-grown mound), and a hole opens from each tunnel's end up through the top block of the ground. The workers dig the tunnels after the food store (`Nest.findDigTarget`). The holes (`colony/ForagingHoles`) are opened (dug from the tunnel end) at foraging time and plugged with Burrowed Dirt from inside when it is over (`PlugHoleGoal`, builders or idle workers: they walk to the tunnel end without passing through the hole, pack for 2 seconds, and wait while anyone is in the hole), once every member of the colony is back inside or 30 seconds after (`PLUG_GRACE_TICKS`; a straggler finds another way in, such as the chimney).
  - **Schedule** (profile `food.forage_at`, termites `["night", "rain"]`, default all three, so the harvester ant is unchanged): `Colony.isForagingTime` = by night (the sky is dark, `Level.isDay`), or while it rains in the world (in the savanna no rain falls but the sky clouds over: the user's "overcast day"). Foragers only gather then (`GatherSeedGoal`, `ClipGrassGoal`; one on its way out turns back when it ends). Outside it a colony that keeps a schedule stays in, out of the sun (`AbstractAntEntity.mayGoOut`, `mayBeAt`): builders only build spots they can reach from inside (`Mound.isWorkableNow`, counted in `wantsSoil` too, so no soil is dug for spots out of reach), diggers and chimney shapers only work from inside, wandering stays inside, and a termite still outside goes back in (`GoInsideGoal`). "Inside" = the nest's cells (shaft, rooms, galleries, foraging tunnels), the entrance cell and the mound's hollow parts and chimney blocks (`Nest.isInside`); a foraging hole itself is the way out, not inside. Idle foragers help the builders by day, as before.
  - **The chimney now and then** (profile `mound.chimney_trip_chance` 0.1): every new walk asks the insect for its habits (`AbstractAntEntity.walkHabits`, `AntNavigator.habits`): on 9 walks in 10 a termite keeps away from chimney mouths (`Doorway.avoidMouths`: no coming out of a doorway block over its top, no going in over its edge; walking sideways through an entrance at the foot is not affected).
