Habits give way: if no route keeps to them, the route is planned without them, so the chimney is still used when it is the only way (before the holes are dug or opened, a straggler at dawn). On the 10th walk the cheapest way is taken, which may or may not be the chimney.
  - `/antcolonies list` shows `foraging now` or `staying in` (out at: night, rain) and `holes open n of 2`. To test by hand use `/time set night`, `/time set day` and `/weather rain`.
  - Tests: unit tests `NestPlanTest.foragingTunnelsRunOutUnderTheSurfaceClearOfTheRooms`, `SpeciesProfileTest` (forage times, the termite numbers); game tests `TermiteForagingTests.termitesOpenTheirForagingHolesAtNightAndPlugThemByDay` (both holes opened at "night", plugged by "day" with everybody inside and a dead bush left alone, opened again the next night; the clock is forced per colony with `Colony.setForagingTimeForTests`) and `TermiteMoundTests.aTermiteOnlyUsesTheChimneyMouthWhenThereIsNoOtherWay` (habits forced with `setWalkHabitsForTests`). The older termite tests force "night".
  - Not done (later parts of the realism pass): soil sheeting over the runways, new holes where food is, food sources (dry grass, fallen branches, logs), the fungus garden, jobs by age.
- Cathedral mound, step 1: the new blocks (user's design in "Cathedral mound look" above; CONFIRMED in-game by the user "they work great"; the user then chose how termites get round them: "Built from inside" in "Cathedral mound look" above). Placeable by hand from the Natural Blocks tab; the termites do not use them yet (step 2: the new shape and the termites shaping finished soil into these pieces).
  - **Termite Spire** (`block/TermiteSpireBlock`, state `thickness` 0 to 4: base 16 to 12 pixels wide, thick 12 to 10, middle 10 to 8, thin 8 to 6, tip 6 to a point): like pointed dripstone. Placed on a spire piece it becomes the next thinner one, so 5 in a column make a spire top.
  - **Termite Chimney Mouth** (`block/TermiteMouthBlock`, implements `Doorway`): a 6-pixel tube on top of the block with its opening at the rim, 28 pixels up (like the old chimney crown's middle spire, 2 pixels wider). Solid for everyone but termites (same entity tag as the chimney crown). State `slim`: alone on a spire its lower part narrows from 10 to 6 pixels (continuing a spire: base, thick, mouth), with a block with a solid side beside it it is a full block. A termite comes out of it with the same look round as the chimney crown (game test). Coming out of a slim mouth on a lone spire has no route yet (nothing to climb down beside it): step 2.
  - **Termite Mound Ramp** (`block/TermiteRampBlock`, `facing` = the high side, `shape` straight / outer / inner corners): 8 small 2-pixel steps up to a full block; ramps side by side join into corners like stairs; a player walks up them. The termites' route planner sees spire pieces and ramps as neither floor nor wall (not full faces), so termites walk round them; real termites build the mound from inside, so step 2 can keep it that way.
  - All three are finished soil: no layers, they dry with age like the mound block (`block/MoundSoil`: the ages, hardness, breaking speed and sounds now shared by `TermiteMoundBlock` and `MoundPieceBlock`); drops like the mound block (dry and old ones need a pickaxe).
  - **Textures:** the finished mound block's sides now carry rain streaks that run down from block to block (the same streak columns on every side), stronger the older the soil, and a finished block shows one of three looks picked by its position. All drawn by `tools/termite_blocks.py`, which also writes the pieces' models (built from 2-pixel layers with the same sums as the game's code) and `references/termites/preview/cathedral_blocks.png` (every piece and a small piece of a cathedral mound).
  - Test command `/antcolonies age <0-3> [radius]`: sets every finished mound block and piece within the radius (default 8) to fresh, setting, dry or old.
  - Tests: unit test `MoundPieceModelsTest` (the ramp and spire model files match the game's shapes, every block state has a model and its textures); game tests `cathedralPiecesArePlacedByHand` (spire stacking, ramp corner, slim and full mouth, termites pass through the mouth and players not, ageing) and `aTermiteComesOutOfTheSpireMouthAndGoesBackIn`.
- Cathedral mound, step 2: the shape (user's decisions in "Cathedral mound look" above; CONFIRMED in-game by the user "it looks so cool I love it"). It REPLACES the 22-tall tower with side chimneys of step 3b part 2.
  - **Shape** (`colony/MoundPlan`, a Java port of `tools/mound_concept.py`): a dome with up to 9 buttresses, a fluted main spire that leans a little, and up to 6 side spires, all growing with the stage; 32 stages (profile `max_height` 32; `base_radius`, `side_chimneys` and `side_chimney_height` are gone), stage `h` = the mound grown to h/32, about 31 tall and about 1,050 blocks when full-grown (as many as the old mound, so it grows at the same speed), the young mound (stage 5) about 6 tall with a small spire. Every spire ends in a column of spire pieces narrowing to a point (thickness picked to follow the concept's taper); the main spire and the 2 tallest side spires end in a chimney mouth instead (base, thick, mouth). Ramps over the steps (facing the soil beside them; corners by the same rule as the block, `TermiteRampBlock.shapeFor`, shared); a dent with soil on 3 sides is filled. A hollow shaft runs from the entrance up the middle of the main spire, following its lean, closed at the top (a roof built out from the walls); no chimney caps and no entrances at the foot. Soil that would hang in the air is dropped. Ages for the instant build follow the concept (old low down, streaks running down, patches of fresh soil). **Every colony's mound is different** (seed from the colony id: the layout is turned, spires lean, buttresses vary); the shape stays the same as it grows.
  - `MoundPlan.stage(h, numbers, seed)` returns a `Shape` (parts WALL, OPEN, MOUTH, SPIRE, RAMP plus each piece's thickness, a ramp's high side and corner, and the age).
  - `/antcolonies mound <stage> instant` puts the whole mound up (pieces, ramps, mouths, ages) and first clears the blocks of an earlier mound outside the new shape (so trying a smaller stage after a bigger one works). Full-grown: `/antcolonies mound 32 instant`.
  - **Termites building it** (left for after the tunnel block): for now they build only the soil (WALL); spire pieces and ramps are not built by them, and a mouth is only shaped once the spire under it stands (so on a termite-built mound the mouths wait). `Nest.isInside` counts only the shaft and the mouths as inside.
  - Picture tool `tools/termite_mound_preview.py` (run `./gradlew test` first; `MoundPlanTest` writes `build/mound-plan/seed_<seed>_stage_<h>.txt`) draws `references/termites/preview/mound.png` with the concept's renderer and the game's real piece shapes: front, back, cut in half, another colony's mound, and stages 5, 13, 22, 32.
  - Tests: `MoundPlanTest` (6 seeds, every stage: everything stands on something, pieces on soil or pieces, ramps on soil, the shaft closed in and joined to the entrance, growth, 28 to 36 tall, 3 mouths full-grown and 1 young, mounds differ). Game test `termitesBuildTheFirstTwoStagesOfTheirMound` now checks only the soil and the open entrance.
- **Next steps (user's order, after the cathedral shape):** (1) the tunnel block, placed by hand; (2) termites climb the outside pieces (ramps, spire pieces, the mouth) so they can roam the mound at night and use the full chimney trip again; (3) termites crawl through tunnels; (4) termites build the cathedral (tunnel plan, windows, the "can it be built" test).
- Tunnel step 1: the Termite Tunnel block (user's design in "Termite tunnel block" above; CONFIRMED in-game by the user "works perfectly"). `block/TermiteTunnelBlock` (a `MoundPieceBlock`, so it ages, hardens and drops like the other pieces; in the Natural Blocks tab): states `north`, `south`, `east`, `west`, `up`, `down` (the channel opens out of that side) and `age`. The channel is 6 pixels (`CHANNEL_FROM` 5 to `CHANNEL_TO` 11) through the middle. **Joins up on its own:** a side next to another tunnel block opens (both open toward each other), a side against any other solid block closes, a side toward open air keeps what it was (`opensToward`), so it is never opened by itself; a neighbour broken away leaves the side open (a broken-into tunnel). **Windows:** `TermiteTunnelBlock.setWindow` opens or closes a side toward open air (for the termites later); a player right-clicking a side with a **shovel** does the same (for testing). Solid for everyone for now, termites too (crawling through is step 3). Its soil (`getOcclusionShape`, the block minus the open channel) hides the faces of the blocks beside it, so a channel can be seen into through an opening; light seeps in through an open channel (light block 4; a fully closed one stops it). Art (`tools/termite_blocks.py`): the inside of the channel is a darker, smoother soil texture (`termite_tunnel_<age>_inside`); the model is a multipart blockstate, a frame (20 boxes, always there) and a plug for each closed side; the item is a straight tunnel; preview `references/termites/preview/tunnel_blocks.png` (the shapes, and a small network seen whole and cut open). Tests: unit test `MoundPieceModelsTest.tunnelModelsLeaveExactlyTheChannelOpen` (all 64 sets of open sides x 4 ages fill exactly the block without the channel); game test `tunnelBlocksJoinUpOnTheirOwn`.
- Tunnel plan step 2: termites climb the outside pieces (user's request "climb on their mound at night ... no matter what"; CONFIRMED in-game by the user "everything works"). Species-neutral, in the legs (`nav`), so ants can use it too.
  - **Slopes** (`nav/Slope`, the ramp implements it: `surfaceHeight` in pixels per 2-pixel column): the route planner treats a ramp's own cell as a floor (`Foothold.isValid`, `CellMap.slopeHeight`/`isSlope`; walls and ceilings in it are not used). A walk onto, along or off a slope is allowed wherever the two surfaces meet without a step of more than half a block anywhere along the edge between them (`AntPathFinder.floorEdge`, `edgesMeet`, `MAX_STEP_PIXELS` 8): up its high side onto the block beside it, down onto it, across onto a ramp beside it, off its low side; not onto its steep sides. Where a wall goes on up from its high side the insect steps onto it and back (`ONTO_WALL` from the slope, walking up to the wall first; `OFF_WALL` onto the slope). It climbs over a wall onto a slope's low side (over its lip), goes over a slope's low side down the block under it, never over a high side. Something dropped onto a ramp lands on it. The walker walks slopes with the normal walking (the 2-pixel steps are climbed like stairs) and counts a body standing on a ramp's top edge (in the cell above it) as on the ramp (`inFloorCell`, `floorUnder`).
  - **Climbable columns** (`nav/ClimbShape`: spire pieces base to thin, not the needle tip; the chimney mouth's lower part, slim or full): their sides count as walls for the planner (`LevelCellMap.isSolidFace`, even for the mouth that termites pass through); their tops are nothing to stand on. The legs hug the real column: every move is worked out as for a full block (the "plan position", the cells the planner sees) and then drawn in onto the column's narrower sides and edges at that height (`AntNavigator.toBody`, `pos`, `squeeze`/`unsqueeze`, `columnWidth` = the widest part of the column level with the body, so it never cuts into a wider step). Doorway motions are left exact.
  - Along the foot of a wall (a cell with a floor) crawling sideways, turning a corner or rounding an edge costs 2 more (`FOOT_OF_WALL`), so an insect holding a wall low down steps off and walks instead of leaning on it while standing (found by the cathedral roam test).
  - **Body:** on a ramp the drawn body tilts with the slope, head up uphill, leaning across it on a corner (`AbstractAntEntity.tiltOnSlope`, read from the ramp's own surface under it; `AntRenderer`).
  - The chimney trip from a spire's mouth still needs a way up inside the spire (tunnels, steps 3 and 4): termites can now climb the outside of a mouth's lower part, but nothing leads into a mouth from inside yet.
  - Tests: `AntPathFinderTest` (up a ramp onto a step and back down by walking only, not onto a ramp's steep side, from a ramp onto the wall above its high side and back, over a wall onto a ramp's low side but never its high side, landing on a ramp); game tests in `TermiteClimbingTests`: `termitesWalkUpAndDownRampsWithoutClimbing`, `termitesClimbASpireHuggingItAndGoRoundIt` (drawn in past where a full block's face would be, touching the piece, not inside it; the tip is nothing to hold), `termitesRoamAllOverACathedralMound` (stage 13 put up at once, 8 termites each walk to 4 random places on ramps, spire sides and soil tops and back; never stuck). Test helpers: `TestAnts.loneTermite`, `TermiteWorkerEntity.keepOutOfColonies`, `Mound.blockFor` (a planned cell's block, shared with the instant build); the "leans on a wall" check in `NavigationTests.watch` now says where and on which route.
  - Fixed during the last test runs: going down from beside a wider block onto a narrower piece below (from beside a full chimney mouth onto the thick spire piece under it) the legs first crept sideways in ever smaller steps instead of going down (`moveAttached` now only steps away first when the column widens below, `outFrom`). The "floating" check in `watch` now looks at the blocks' drawn shapes, not their collision (a termite climbs the outside of a mouth it could also pass through), and the floating and hovering checks skip a chimney mouth's exact motion (`AntNavigator.isWalkingADoorway`). All failure messages of `watch` say where, on which foothold and on which route.
  - Known flaw, fixed by step 3: a full (not slim) chimney mouth was an open cell for termites, so a route could go in by its tube and out sideways through the block's wall (walking through it). Step 3 makes the mouth a channel block.
  - `workersCarrySeedsToTheFoodStore` failed again (7 seeds instead of 5, a neighbouring test's seeds or grass): it now runs in a test batch of its own (`batch = "seeds_alone"`).
  - All 75 game tests pass. Merged into master after the user confirmed it.
- Tunnel plan step 3: termites crawl through tunnels (user's plan; built overnight while the user slept; CONFIRMED in-game by the user "everything works"). Species-neutral, in the legs (`nav`).
  - **Channels** (`nav/Channel`): a block an insect can crawl through by a narrow channel; who fits is up to the block (`fits`), which sides can ever open (`canJoin`) and which are open now (`openSides`). The termite tunnel block (all 6 sides, as its state says), every spire piece but the tip (a channel up its middle, open down and up only toward another channel block: a tunnel under the spire, the next piece, a mouth) and the chimney mouth (open on top by its tube, down toward a channel block under it) are channel blocks. Who fits: the new entity tag `antcolonies:fits_termite_tunnels` (termite workers only; soldiers later will not be in it, the user's decision). For them a tunnel block's collision is its soil only (the block without the channel), a spire piece's the piece without the channel; for everyone else they stay solid. A tunnel block now opens toward any channel block that can open back (a hollow spire piece over it), not only toward tunnel blocks. Tunnel blocks no longer suffocate (a termite inside one used to choke to death: the game counts it as inside a wall).
  - **Planner** (`AntPathFinder`): a cell with a channel the insect fits is never an open cell (`LevelCellMap`), it is a channel foothold (`Foothold.channel`, `inChannel`). New moves (`StepKind`): `CRAWL_CHANNEL` to the next block's channel where both open toward each other (1, up or down 1.1); `INTO_CHANNEL` / `OUT_OF_CHANNEL` (1) by an opening: a hole in a block's side from the floor in front of it or the wall it is in, a hole in a floor from above, a hole in a ceiling from below; `OUT_OF_MOUTH` / `INTO_MOUTH` (3): by a chimney mouth's tube on top, onto (or from) the top of a block beside the mouth or the outside of the mouth's own column (a mouth alone on a spire). A mouth is no longer an open cell, so nobody walks through its sides (the flaw of step 2). The habit of avoiding mouths (9 walks in 10) covers the new mouth moves. A channel cell can be a goal and a start; `AntPathFinder.whatChanged` also notices a channel that closed.
  - **Legs** (`AntNavigator`): in a channel the body moves exactly along the channel's middle, its feet on the channel's floor (5 pixels up, `Channel.FLOOR`): sideways lying on the floor, up or down holding the channel's wall (`crawlChannel`, `intoChannel`, `outOfChannel`); out of a side hole it drops onto the floor in front or goes head first down the wall; out of a hole on top it steps off the hole so it does not fall back in (`OFF_THE_HOLE`); out of a mouth it walks the doorway motion (up the tube, the look round, over the rim) and then onto the block beside or on down the outside of the mouth's column (`outOfMouth`, `intoMouth`). It rests in a channel when its walk ends there and is still there after a reload (`HoldChannel` saved). A body only counts as in a channel when it is on the channel's middle line (a termite hugging the outside of a narrow hollow piece has its feet in the piece's block). Body: a pose flag `IN_CHANNEL` (synced); in a channel the drawn body is pushed less toward the wall (`AntRenderer.CHANNEL_INTO_WALL`), so it stays in the middle.
  - **Hugging narrow columns, reworked** (found by the new tests): on a column narrower than a block the legs now move along the plan one step at a time, each step drawn in at the height it goes to (`hugAlong`): sideways along the face by the column's width there, out from the face by the widest part within a step above or below (so the body is clear of a wider part before it passes it, and slides smoothly down a taper); away-first only when the body is not yet clear of the part it sweeps past. The plan position of a hugging body is worked out from how far out it is (`pos`), so it never drifts. On full blocks the legs move exactly as before.
  - Tests: `AntPathFinderTest` (through a tunnel instead of round a wall, an insect that does not fit never uses one, a channel cell as goal and start, up inside a spire and out of its mouth down any side, never out of a mouth sideways, in and out of the shaft wall, a floor hole and a ceiling hole, a closed channel); new game tests `TermiteTunnelTests`: `aTermiteCrawlsUpInsideASpireAndComesOutOfItsMouth` (in at a window, along the tunnel, up the hollow pieces into the mouth; with the window shut the only way out is the mouth: the look round, down the outside, to the ground), `antsFindNoWayIntoTermiteTunnels`, `aTermiteInATunnelIsStillThereAfterAReload`; `aTermiteComesOutOfTheSpireMouthAndGoesBackIn` now has a tunnel block between the shaft and the mouth (in from the shaft's ceiling hole). The watch checks skip a channel's exact motion like a doorway's.
- Tunnel plan step 4: termites build the cathedral from inside (built overnight; the user saw it working in-game but cannot yet tell whether the termites can build the whole cathedral, because a colony cannot grow big without the fungus garden and the queen laying eggs; all 79 game tests and all unit tests pass).
  - **The rules and the tunnel plan** (`colony/MoundGrowth`, pure geometry): termites work from the shaft (26 cells round) or from a channel (the block right beside it through a window; or diagonally past an empty cell outside the plan). The mound is grown in a pretend world stage by stage and a tunnel is carved only where they would get stuck, plus enough that every outside block can be mended from inside (the user's "every outside block is next to a channel"). Result: `MoundPlan.Part.TUNNEL` (about 550 to 620 tunnel blocks of ~1,050 in a full mound). `MoundPlan.stage` now includes tunnels (cached per colony, `MoundPlan.growth`); `shapeOf` is the bare shape.
  - **Shape fix:** soil blocks touching only at an edge (diagonal buttresses) get one gap filled (`fillDiagonalGaps`), else no tunnel could ever reach them.
  - **"Can it be built" test** (`MoundGrowthTest`, 20 mounds): every stage grown, then rebuilt by the rules alone stage after stage, in random order, with surprises (1% and 5% of every stage blocked), every outside block mendable, tunnels stay. All pass. Jumping straight to a much later stage is NOT proven, so the game builds one stage at a time.
  - **In the game** (`Mound`, `MoundReach`, `BuildMoundGoal`, new `ShapeMoundGoal` replacing `ShapeHoleGoal`): soil, then carving tunnels (opening into the shaft); ramps, spire pieces and mouths cost 1 pellet each (my pick, to confirm); pieces that change between stages are reshaped, pieces where soil goes are cleared; pieces of earlier stages where nothing goes any more crumble when a new stage begins (my pick, to confirm). Windows: a termite in a tunnel bites a window, builds, plugs it; only at night or in the rain. The termites finish a stage before starting the next (`Mound.getStage` = work stage, saved as `moundWorkStage`; `getEarnedStage` = earned); if nothing is built for 5 minutes they move on anyway (my pick). Fallbacks (outside at night, then 2-block reach) only after 1 minute without progress (my pick). Tunnel blocks count as set soil for arches; arches may be built over pieces.
  - Game test `TermiteCathedralTests.termitesBuildTheYoungCathedralFromInside`: a colony builds stages 1 to 5 by itself (soil, tunnels, ramps, spire, mouth), no window left open; passed 4 runs in a row, no fallbacks used.
  - Not done yet: the termites' body does not visibly poke out of a window; `tools/termite_mound_preview.py` does not draw tunnels yet (it may not read the new TUNNEL part); a day-time test (no windows by day).
- M7 step 5a: the fungus garden and the queen's eggs (user's decisions in "Fungus garden decisions" above; CONFIRMED in-game by the user "it works great"; every number and the room layout are my picks, the user has not commented on them).
  - **Garden rooms** (`NestPlan.gardens`, kind `GARDEN`): 3x3 rooms on the royal chamber's level, round it in a grid (beside it left and right, beyond it, and further out), each joined to the royal chamber or to the garden next to it by a 1-block doorway, a wall of soil to the next room (unit test `gardenRoomsLieRoundTheRoyalChamberAndOnlyOpenThroughTheirDoorways`: no room touches another, the shaft, a gallery or a foraging tunnel). Profile `garden`: `rooms` 6 at most, one more for every `workers_per_room` 4 workers (the first with the first worker, `GardenNumbers.roomsFor`). Dug by the workers: the first right after the entrance plug (before the foraging tunnels), the others as the colony grows (`Nest.findDigTarget`, `wantedGardens`). The food store side room is gone from the termite profile (`side_rooms` removed): new colonies dig gardens instead; old colonies keep their dug store as an unused room.
  - **Fungus Comb** (`block/FungusCombBlock`, a `SeedPileBlock` with collision, in the Natural Blocks tab): states `layers` 1 to 4 used (2 pixels each, half a block at most; `maxLayers()`), `nodules` 0 to 3. The termites' food pile is now the comb (profile `food.pile`): every piece a forager brings is one layer, built onto comb that has room before a new one is started (`FoodStore.pickFoodSpot`), never under an egg or a nymph (`depositSeed` refuses). Termites walk on it (a flat `Slope`; the legs now count a slope no higher than half a block as a floor even under a ceiling, `Foothold.LOW_SLOPE_PIXELS`, unit test `acrossALowFlatSlopeUnderACeilingButNotAHighOne`), and a digger may stand on it (`Nest.findStandSpots`). Breaking it drops itself and its nodules. Art by `tools/fungus_garden_art.py` (light brown brain-like comb, white nodules, the Fungus Nodule item).
  - **Nodules** (`colony/FungusGarden`, `colony.garden()`): once a second (called by the queen) every comb block with a layer to spare (the bottom layer stays) and fewer than 3 nodules grows one with a chance of 20 / `nodule_ticks` 1200 (about one a minute); it uses up a layer (the colony's food count goes down).
  - **Nurses' food** (`colony/NurseFood`, `colony.nurseFood()`: bread for ants, `Bakery`; nodules for termites, `FungusGarden`): `FeedQueenGoal` and `FeedLarvaGoal` now work for both. Termite workers now have nurses (profile `nurses_from_workers` 3, 8, 12, 20, like the ants) and the goals `CarryEggGoal`, `FeedLarvaGoal`, `FeedQueenGoal`, `StandByQueenGoal`. The carried nodule is the new **Fungus Nodule** item (`ModItems.FUNGUS_NODULE`, counts as `isCarryingBread`).
  - **The queen's steady stream** (profile `laying`, `QueenCare.layInStream`, `layStreamEgg`): she holds up to `queen.meal` 6 nodules; while she lies in her room with at least `food_per_egg` 2 in her and fewer than `max_young` 10 young, an egg comes out behind her every `egg_interval_ticks` 2400 (2 minutes) when just founded, down to `egg_interval_ticks_full_size` 300 (15 seconds) at full size (`Laying.intervalFor`). Not while founding (the founding batch is unchanged). Saved: `nextStreamEgg`. Her starving is not switched on for termites (only ant queens starve).
  - **Eggs into the gardens** (`Brood.pickEggSpot`): nurse, then garden (on the comb first, then a free floor cell), then her room; an egg lies on top of the comb (`Brood.restingHeight`, `FungusCombBlock.topOf`), hatches there into a nymph, and the nurses feed it `larva_meal` 5 nodules until it grows into a worker.
  - `/antcolonies list` shows for termites `gardens n dug (planned 6), comb x layers on y blocks, nodules z` and `queen holds n/6 nodules, next egg in m:ss` (or `not laying`).
  - Game tests: new `TermiteGardenTests.termitesGrowAFungusGardenAndRaiseTheQueensEggsOnIt` (6 workers dig 2 gardens, build hay into comb, nodules grow, the full-grown queen is fed and lays, eggs go onto the comb, hatch, nymphs are fed, a new worker grows up; no egg or nymph ever inside the comb; about 13500 ticks); `TermiteFoodTests.termitesGatherDeadPlantsIntoTheirFungusGarden` (was ...FoodStore: every piece is in the comb, grown into a nodule, carried or lying; all comb lies in the gardens).
  - Not done (later parts): jobs by age (5b), Termitomyces mushrooms (5c); old comb is not eaten and rebuilt beyond the nodules using it up; termite workers do not eat; the termite queen does not starve.
- Three mound fixes (user's requests after testing stage 13 to 14, 2026-10-13; CONFIRMED in-game by the user, together with bugs 6e and 6f: the termites built it all without going outside):
  - **Spires are reshaped where they stand:** when the mound grows, a piece (spire piece, mouth, ramp) where the plan now has soil or a tunnel is no longer cleared and built again: it is filled up into that soil or tunnel block where it stands (`Mound.jobAt`/`shape`, the same rule in the pretend mound `MoundGrowth`, so the "can it be built" proof covers it). So the bottom of a spire that moves up a block becomes a tunnel block at once, nothing above it is left hanging in the air, and the channel up the spire is never cut. No soil pellet is needed for it (the piece already holds its soil; my pick). Game test `termitesGrowStageThirteenIntoFourteen` now fails if a spire piece or mouth ever hangs over air.
  - **The middle hole is plugged by day:** on a young mound (about stages 1 to 3) the entrance in its middle is open to the sky (the plan has nothing over it yet). A colony that keeps a schedule (termites) now plugs it with soil from the top of the shaft by day, like the foraging holes (`ForagingHoles.middleHole`, same plugging goal and the same "everybody inside, or 30 seconds" rule), and bites it open again at foraging time (`Nest.mayOpenEntrance`). Once anything stands over it higher up (`Nest.isEntranceCovered`: the mound's roof over its shaft) it is no longer plugged, and a plug left under a roof is opened. The ants are unchanged (they keep no schedule). Game test `termitesOpenTheirForagingHolesAtNightAndPlugThemByDay` checks the middle hole too.
  - **Heading in before dawn:** from time of day 22000 (about an in-game hour before sunrise; `Colony.HEAD_IN_FROM`, my pick) the night counts as over for species that go out at night (`Colony.isBeforeDawn`, used by `isForagingTime`): foragers turn back, no new windows, holes and windows are plugged and everybody is in before the sun is up. Rain still counts as foraging time. Game test `theNightEndsForTermitesBeforeDawn`.
- Termites keep their nest shut, with their own soil (user's request, 2026-10-13; CONFIRMED in-game by the user):
  - **Sealing:** termite workers now have the ants' `SealGoal` (builders, or idle workers): any opening in the walls of the dug-out nest (a player digging down into a garden or the royal chamber, a cave, a cut wall) is packed shut from inside, any time of day. The foraging holes are not openings (they open and close on their own schedule), and neither is the middle hole.
  - **Only the nest that is really there:** an opening only counts next to a dug-out nest cell joined to the top of the shaft through other dug-out cells (`NestWalls.joinedCells`; ants too). Before, a planned cell that happened to be open (a cave crossing a gallery nobody had dug yet, or in the test arena the galleries running past the edge of the ground) counted as nest, and the termites went off sealing round it.
  - **Their own blocks:** what a species packs into a hole is in its profile, `nest.plug_block` (default Burrowed Dirt, so the ants are unchanged; termites `antcolonies:termite_mound`, put in finished and fresh, so it sets and hardens with age like the mound). Used for the openings, the foraging holes, the middle hole by day and the founding pair's sealed entrance (`NestNumbers.plugState`, `isPlug`; Burrowed Dirt plugs from older saves still count as plugs and are opened as before). The mound leaves a plug in the entrance or a foraging hole alone (it is no soil to clear, `Mound.isClearJob`).
  - The mound itself was already mended: a broken mound block is a build spot again, and by day it is rebuilt from the tunnel beside it. A pit a player digs down to the nest is closed only where it meets the nest (the block touching the room); the pit above stays open (my pick, to confirm).
  - Game test `TermiteRepairTests.termitesSealOpeningsInTheirNestWithMoundSoil` (by day, the royal chamber and a garden opened to the sky and a garden wall cut: all three closed with finished mound soil, no openings left); the termite founding and foraging tests now expect mound soil plugs.
- M7 step 5b, jobs by age (user's decisions in "Jobs by age decisions" above, 2026-10-13; CONFIRMED in-game by the user; ants and termites alike):
  - **Age:** every worker remembers when it grew up (`AbstractAntEntity.grownUpAt`, saved as `GrownUpAt`): a worker hatching from the brood is 0 days old. A worker of unknown age (spawn egg, older save) is made a grown-up of middle age by its colony, two builders to one forager (`Colony.ageForUnknownWorker`, my pick), so a handful from spawn eggs shares the jobs as before (6 give 1 nurse, 3 builders, 2 foragers).
  - **Stages** (profile `worker.nurse_until_ticks` 72000 = 3 days, `builder_until_ticks` 192000 = 3 + 5 days, both species; `WorkerNumbers.roleForAge`): nurse, then builder (digging, sealing, building the mound, windows too), then forager. Workers do not die of old age (not planned yet).
  - **Flexible** (`Colony.roleOf`, `workOutRoles`, `shareOut`, worked out once a second): at least as many nurses as `nurses_from_workers` asks for, at least a third of the rest builders and a third foragers (rounded up; my pick); a short job takes workers from the job with most to spare, and the jobs always stay in age order (youngest nurse, oldest forage). So a founding batch that grew up together is spread out at once (termites 5: 1 nurse, 2 builders, 2 foragers; ants 3: one of each), and an old colony keeps its nurses and builders. Spare young workers stay nurses (they help others when idle, as before).
  - **Paler young:** the pale copy of the worker texture (`<texture>_young.png`, drawn by `tools/young_textures.py`; rerun it after a model script redraws a worker texture) is drawn over the body by `client/YoungPaleLayer`, fully on the day it grows up, fading out by the end of its nurse years (synced `DATA_YOUTH`).
  - `/antcolonies list` shows `ages a young, b middle, c old, jobs x nurses, y builders, z foragers`.
  - Tests: unit test `JobsByAgeTest` (the sharing out); game test `JobsByAgeTests.termiteWorkersTakeTheJobOfTheirAge` (six termites of three ages get their age's jobs, the youngest pale, the age saved).
  - Fixed in the 5c session (found by that game test failing once): the colony kept its own copy of each worker's age and refreshed it only when that worker asked for its job, so a changed age was shared out stale for a moment. Now `Colony.workOutRoles` reads every worker's age afresh each time.
- M7 step 5c, Termitomyces mushrooms (user's decisions in "Termitomyces decisions" above, 2026-10-13; CONFIRMED in-game by the user "everything works really well"; the numbers are my picks and the user kept them):
  - **When:** the moment a rain ends (in the savanna no rain falls, but the sky clouds over and clears: the same), a colony whose gardens hold at least `mushroom_comb_layers` 8 layers of comb (a healthy garden) sends up one mushroom per `comb_layers_per_mushroom` 6 layers, at most `max_mushrooms` 8 (profile `garden`; `colony/Mushrooms`, called once a second by the termite queen; the weather is watched only while the colony is loaded, not saved).
  - **Where:** random spots within `mushroom_radius` 12 of the entrance, on the top of the ground (earth, `#minecraft:dirt`) or of finished mound soil, in the open, at most 4 blocks above or below the entrance's ground (so on a mound only the lower slopes); never in the mound's plan, the nest, a foraging hole or the entrance; grass in the spot is pushed aside.
  - **The small mushroom** (`block/TermitomycesBlock`, `stage` 0 to 3): a white button, a young mushroom with a pointed cap, the open cap (pale cream rim, light brown, dark brown point in the middle, radial cracks, white gills, spores drifting off), and wilted (brown, drooping), then gone. On random ticks: button and closed cap about 3 to 4 minutes each, open about 45 minutes (2 days), wilted about 18 minutes (a day). Nothing collides with it; anything placed in its cell replaces it like grass (termites building there build over it). Picked by hand it gives 1 mushroom (button, closed) or 2 (open), wilted nothing. Bone meal brings a button or closed cap on.
  - **The giant** (`block/GiantTermitomycesBlock`, `part` foot/middle/top, `stage` closed/open/wilted): a mushroom button marked `giant` (each mushroom has the chance `giant_mushroom_chance` 0.04, only on earth, not on the mound, and only with room for 3 blocks clear of the termites' plan) grows into a white stalk 3 blocks tall with a tall pointed spike, which opens into a cap 40 pixels (2.5 blocks) across overhanging the blocks round it; it wilts and goes like the small one. A giant button with no room above grows as an ordinary mushroom. Breaking any part breaks the whole giant; 6 mushrooms closed, 10 open, nothing wilted. A thin stalk collides (and the cap over its own block); the route planner walks round it.
  - **Food:** Termitomyces (raw: 1 drumstick, saturation 0.3) and Cooked Termitomyces (3 drumsticks, saturation 0.6), from a furnace, smoker or campfire; in the Food and Drinks tab. The mushroom is never planted (no block item: they only come from the gardens; pick block gives the food).
  - **Art:** `tools/termitomyces_art.py` draws the textures (a 48-pixel cap top for the giant so its cap is as sharp as the blocks), writes the models, blockstates, item models, loot tables and cooking recipes, and a preview `references/termites/preview/termitomyces.png` (every stage side by side) and `termitomyces_textures.png`.
  - **Commands:** `/antcolonies mushrooms` (as if a rain had just ended: the garden's number, none if not healthy), `/antcolonies mushrooms <count>` (that many whatever the garden), `/antcolonies mushrooms <count> giant` (giants where they fit), `/antcolonies mushrooms grow` (every mushroom within 16 blocks one stage on). `/antcolonies list` adds `mushrooms after rain: n (last rain m came up)` or `no mushrooms yet (comb x of 8 layers)`.
  - Tests: game tests `TermitomycesTests.aTermitomycesGrowsOpensWiltsAndIsGone` (stages, drops, bone meal, the giant, cooking) and `aHealthyGardenSendsUpMushroomsWhenTheRainEnds` (no comb: none; 35 layers: 5 or so round the mound, all on good ground, in the open, never where the termites build or live; giants sent up grow into giants; the rain is set per colony with `Mushrooms.setRainForTests`).
  - Not done: mushrooms on old natural logs or in other biomes; the termites do nothing with the mushrooms (real termites do not either); a rain that ends while the colony is unloaded sends up nothing.
- M7 step 6a, termite soldiers (user's decisions in "Soldier decisions" above, 2026-10-13; built in one go at the user's request "do the entirety of 6a now, then I'll bug test after"; CONFIRMED in-game by the user; every number below is my pick, the user kept them):
  - **Two mobs** (`entity/TermiteSoldierEntity`, one class, `Rank` MINOR / MAJOR; entity types `termite_minor_soldier` (hitbox 0.3 x 0.22, health 6, speed 0.24) and `termite_major_soldier` (0.45 x 0.3, health 12, speed 0.17); spawn eggs for both; counted by the colony under the castes `minor_soldier` and `major_soldier`). A soldier put down with a spawn egg joins the nearest termite colony within 32 blocks. No colony role (no jobs); they dig and carry nothing. They do not fit the termite tunnel blocks (not in `fits_termite_tunnels`) and do not pass the chimney mouths.
  - **Models** (`tools/termite_models.py`, `soldier_model`): the worker's pale body (no eyes, no wing stubs) with a big rounded, shiny orange-brown head, darker at the front, and long black jaws that run forward and curve in, crossing at the tips; beaded antennae from the sides of the head. Minor: the worker's body size (0.85) with a bigger head, about 0.47 block long; major: body 1.2 with a huge head and longer jaws, about 0.74 block long (about twice a worker). The model is moved back so the middle of its length sits over the hitbox. Files `termite_minor_soldier` / `termite_major_soldier` (geo, texture; the soldiers' colours are only in their own texture, the other textures are unchanged) and `termite_soldier.animation.json`: idle (with a slow jaw twitch), the worker's walk, and **`head_bang`** (a 1-second loop: 6 quick strikes 0.1 s apart, the head tipping down 10 degrees so the jaws knock the floor, the forebody jerking with it, the jaws gaping and the antennae pulled back, then a rest). Previews `references/termites/preview/` (front, side, top... now with both soldiers) and `head_bang.png`. The major climbing a wall is drawn pressed farther into it and lifted more (`AntRenderer.withClimbPose`).
  - **Soldiers on demand** (`colony/Soldiers`, `colony.soldiers()`; profile `soldiers`: `per_worker` 0.1, `minors_per_major` 3, `minor_meal` 6, `major_meal` 10 nodules, `meal_interval_ticks` 24000, `slow_after_ticks` 48000): the colony wants `workers x 0.1 x 3/4` minors and `workers x 0.1 x 1/4` majors, rounded (0.5 rounds up): none for the founding batch, the first minor from 7 workers, 2 minors and 1 major at 20 workers (today's worker limit), 75 and 25 at 1000. When an egg hatches (`Brood.hatchEgg`) the nymph grows into a soldier if the colony has fewer (soldiers plus nymphs already growing into them) than it wants, minors first; it then eats the soldier's meal and grows into that soldier (`Brood.hatchSoldier`; soldiers are not held back by the 20-worker limit). A worker nymph that has eaten its fill while the colony has all its workers turns into a soldier nymph if the colony is short of one, so the brood never jams. The nymph's kind is saved (`soldier`) and synced: from half its meal on it is drawn as a "presoldier" with a big pale head and soft jaws, a future major grows bigger (`TermiteNymphRenderer`).
  - **Fed by nurses** (`entity/FedByNurses`, implemented by the nymph and the soldier): the nurses' `FeedLarvaGoal` now picks at random among hungry nymphs and hungry soldiers and follows a soldier that moves; a soldier a nurse is on her way to holds still. A soldier wants a nodule once a day (only in a colony whose hunger is on); after 2 days without it moves at half speed; it does not starve to death (termite workers do not eat at all yet).
  - **Guarding inside** (`entity/goal/GuardPostGoal`, `Soldiers.posts`): one soldier to a post, most important first, only posts dug out with a floor. Majors: the royal chamber's doorway (the end of its tunnel, facing back toward the shaft), the way in from the shaft (the start of that tunnel, facing the shaft; not the foot of the shaft itself, because the workers dig on down from there to their galleries), then each garden doorway (facing the room it opens into). Minors by day: in each foraging tunnel one block before its end, facing the hole's end (at foraging time they go out, see step 6b). A soldier with no post strolls about inside. The majors always stay inside. One that finds no way to its post leaves it alone for a minute.
  - **Drumming:** `TermiteSoldierEntity.drum(ticks)`: it stands still, plays `head_bang` (synced) and a quiet dry tapping (note block hat, in bursts). For now only by command: **`/antcolonies drum [seconds]`** (every soldier within 16 blocks, default 3 seconds). The alarm (6c) will start it by itself.
  - `/antcolonies list` adds `soldiers 1 minor (wants 2), 0 major (wants 1), nymphs growing into soldiers 1, posts held 1`.
  - Tests: unit tests `SpeciesProfileTest.aColonyWantsAboutOneSoldierInTenThreeMinorsToAMajor`, `ModelFilesTest` (both soldier models hold the carry bone; their animations read and only move bones they have; the head bang loops); game tests `TermiteSoldierTests`: `nymphsGrowIntoSoldiersWhenTheColonyNeedsThem` (minor, worker; at 20 workers minor, major, worker; the kind kept after a reload; fed nymphs grow into the right soldiers; a full worker nymph at the limit turns into a minor when one is missing), `soldiersStandGuardAndANurseFeedsAHungrySoldier` (two majors at both ends of the royal tunnel, the minor in a foraging tunnel, each facing out; a nurse feeds the hungry major; done at about tick 330), `aSoldierDrumsStandingStill`, `soldiersFindNoWayIntoTermiteTunnels`.
  - Not done (6c): the alarm ladder; soldiers fight nothing (M8); no soldier bodies when they die.
- M7 step 6b, the minors out with the foragers (user's decisions in "Soldier decisions" above; built straight after 6a at the user's request "start the next big step but keep the commits separate"; CONFIRMED in-game by the user "it works"; the details below are my picks, the user kept them):
  - **Out at foraging time** (`Soldiers.minorsOut`, `TermiteSoldierEntity.mayGoOut`): at night and in the rain the minors go out; the majors always stay in.
  - **Where they stand** (`Soldiers.outsidePosts`, worked out once a second, most important first): for each open way out (`ForagingHoles.waysOut`: an open foraging hole, and the entrance in the middle while it is open to the sky on a young mound), the busiest first: one minor on the ground right beside it, facing out from the nest; then the **sentry line** along that way out's trail (`colony/SentryLine`): one every `soldiers.sentry_spacing` 3 blocks on alternate sides, about a block to the side of the trail, each facing away from it, stopping a block and a half short of the food. A way out no forager used lately gets only its guard, no line. Posts must be on solid ground (not leaves), in the open, outside the nest and the mound, not where the mound will grow. Minors left over wait at the inside posts in the foraging tunnels. A soldier moves up to a more important post when one comes free (`Soldiers.claimPost`, `holds`), so the minors waiting inside come out as soon as a hole is opened.
  - **The trails** (`colony/ForagingTrails`, `colony.trails()`, species-neutral, a first piece of the "food map"): every time a forager sets out for food or takes a piece, the spot is remembered for 2 minutes (`MEMORY_TICKS`), with the way out that forager last came out by (it is seen outside within 4 blocks of it, looked at once a second). A trail runs from a way out to the patch where most of its spots lie (spots within 4 blocks belong together; the trail keeps its patch while that still has half as many spots as the busiest, and the busiest trail keeps first place until another has 3 more spots, so the sentries do not keep moving). Not saved.
  - **In last:** when the foraging time ends, the minors stay at their posts while any worker is still outside (counted once a second, out to the food radius), at most `soldiers.escort_home_ticks` 300 (15 seconds; the holes are plugged at the latest 30 seconds after the end), then they go in by the holes and take their day posts inside.
  - **Bug fixed on the way (all foraging holes, since realism pass part 1):** the check "is anyone of the colony still outside" (`ForagingHoles.isAnyoneOutside`) never ran: its once-a-second timer started at `Long.MIN_VALUE`, and `now - Long.MIN_VALUE` overflows to a negative number, so it always said "nobody". The holes were therefore plugged as soon as the foraging time ended, without waiting for the workers still out (they then came in by the chimney or waited outside). Now they wait for everybody (or 30 seconds) as designed. See bug 16b.
  - `/antcolonies list` adds `(1 beside ways out, 3 on trails)` while the minors are out (`, waiting for the workers to come in` after the foraging), the trails (`trails: from x,y,z to x,y,z (n spots)`), and `minors in` by day.
  - Also fixed: the game test `termitesSealOpeningsInTheirNestWithMoundSoil` could cut a wall that a later garden's doorway goes through (bug 18c).
  - Tests: unit tests `SentryLineTest` (a straight trail lined on both sides facing out, no line for a short trail, a slanting trail in 24 directions lined beside it never on it, the patch is where most spots lie), `SpeciesProfileTest` (the new numbers); game test `TermiteSoldierTests.minorsGuardTheHolesAndLineTheForagersTrailThenGoInLast` (5 minors, 5 old workers, a hay bale 11 blocks from a hole: a minor beside each hole, 3 on the trail on both sides, all facing out; at "day" the minors go in only after the last worker, and both holes are plugged once everybody is in).
- M7 step 6c, the alarm ladder, chickens and a simple bite (user's decisions in "Soldier decisions" and "Chickens and a simple bite in 6c" above; built at the user's request "whatever is easiest for that add on, just for a fun test, start 6c"; waiting for the user to test it in-game; the details are my picks, to confirm):
  - **The alarm** (`colony/Alarm`, `colony.alarm()`, species-neutral; only species with an `alarm` group in their profile raise one: termites, not the harvester ant). Levels: **1 footsteps** (a player sprinting or jumping on the mound, on a block of its plan or within 3 blocks of the entrance; sneaking does not count; jumps come from the game's jump event, sprinting is looked for once a second): the 2 minors nearest come to the nest cells nearest the spot and drum once. **2 an enemy at the foragers** (a member of the colony hurt by anything not of the colony, `AbstractAntEntity.hurt`; or a chicken, an ant or a termite of another colony within 4 blocks of a forager outside or 3 of an open way out, looked for once a second): no foraging while the alarm is at 2 or more (`Colony.isForagingTime`), so the foragers go in and the holes are plugged after them; the minors that feel it go at the enemy (outside, or within 8 blocks of it) and bite it if it is a chicken; they chase it only within the colony's ground (food radius + 8). **3 a break-in** (a player breaks a block of the mound's plan or one touching the dug-out nest, `BlockEvent.BreakEvent`): every soldier goes to a spot near the breach, the minors nearest (one into the hole itself if it has a floor), the majors behind them; the workers seal it as always (`SealGoal`); a soldier standing in the hole is pushed out the far side (shut out; if the far side is soil, back into the nest) instead of stopping the sealing (`NestWalls.plugLeak`). **4 repeated break-ins** (3 within 5 minutes): as 3, everybody stays in for a whole day, and the colony wants twice as many soldiers for 3 days (`Soldiers.wanted`, saved as `soldierBoostUntil`).
  - **Calming down** (profile `alarm.calm_ticks` 600, 2400, 3600, 24000): each level drops one level once its time has passed since the last thing that raised it (30 seconds, 2 minutes, 3 minutes, a day). A lower-level event during a higher alarm changes nothing. Saved: the level and when it was raised (`alarmLevel`, `alarmRaisedAt`).
  - **Drumming spreads** (`Alarm.listen`, asked by each soldier every 5 ticks): at levels 2 to 4 the soldier nearest the trouble feels it first (at 2 also the minors within 6 blocks, who see it); it drums 1.2 seconds (head bang) and then acts; a soldier within `feel_radius` 6 blocks of one that started drumming at least 6 ticks before feels it in turn (at 2 only minors). At a break-in everyone has felt it after 5 seconds even if the chain is broken. `/antcolonies drum` still works.
  - **Soldiers** (`entity/goal/DefendGoal`, priority 3, before going in and the guard posts): the alarm gives each soldier a task (`Alarm.taskFor`, worked out every half second), at an enemy or to a spot; a soldier with a task may be outside (`mayGoOut`). A soldier waiting for a nurse no longer holds still during an alarm.
  - **The bite** (`TermiteSoldierEntity.bite`): the minor's attack damage is now 3 (the user's number; majors keep 4 but bite nothing yet), once a second while its jaws touch the enemy, with a new `bite` animation (0.35 seconds: head back with the jaws wide open, then a lunge as they snap shut; `tools/termite_models.py`; the user asked to reuse the breaking animation, but there is none, digging only shows cracks on the block) and a small cracking sound. The user wants the bite refined later (a placeholder for now). Only chickens are bitten for now; players and ants are only faced (M8).
  - **Chickens hunt termites** (`entity/goal/HuntTermitesGoal`, added to every vanilla chicken when it joins the world): a grown chicken goes for the nearest termite worker it can reach within 8 blocks that is out in the open (not in a tunnel block, not on a wall), any time of day, and pecks it (1 damage a second; a worker has 3 health). One that has eaten a termite is full for 30 seconds. A bitten chicken panics and runs (vanilla), then may come back. Termites only (ants with M8). A worker killed by a chicken leaves no body (no termite bodies yet).
  - Test command `/antcolonies alarm <0-4>` (the nearest termite colony's alarm at that level where the player stands; 0 calms it). `/antcolonies list` adds ` | calm` or ` | alarm 3 (break-in at x,y,z, 5 soldiers alarmed)` and the soldier boost.
  - Tests: unit `SpeciesProfileTest` (the alarm numbers; the ant has none); game tests `TermiteAlarmTests`: `aChickenHuntsATermiteWorker`, `minorsBiteAChickenThatHuntsTheForagersWhoHurryHome` (at "night" a chicken at the hay: alarm 2 within a second, no foraging, every minor feels it, a minor bites the chicken to death, the workers all go in), `aBreakInSpreadsTheDrummingAndSendsTheSoldiersToTheBreach` (by day a garden wall broken: the drumming spreads, a minor stands in the hole, the workers seal it behind it, the minors stand nearest the breach and the majors behind), `repeatedBreakInsMakeItLevelFourAndTheColonyWantsMoreSoldiers` (3 break-ins: level 4, no foraging, 3 minors wanted instead of 2, kept after a reload), `footstepsBringTwoMinorsToLookAndDrum`.
  - Not done: soldiers swarming the outside of the mound when a block high up is broken (they go to the nest cells nearest it); shut-out soldiers simply try to get in (by a foraging hole once it opens at night); no fighting players or ants (M8).
- Finding food (user's request after 6c, 2026-10-13; built on branch `m7-food-finding`, made from `m7-alarm`; waiting for the user to test it in-game; the details are my picks, to confirm):
  - **The food map** (`colony/FoodMap`, `colony.foodMap()`, species-neutral; only for species with `food.find_radius` in the profile: termites 5, the harvester ant has none and knows every plant round its nest as before). Once a second (called by the queen) every worker outside the nest **finds** the food plants within 5 blocks of it; a worker inside **tells** the colony of the plants it found, and from then on the colony **knows** them. The finder itself may go for its find before it has told anyone. Plants used up or gone are forgotten. Known plants are saved (`knownFood`); found-but-untold ones are not. Dropped food items (sticks, wheat) are still seen anywhere in the food radius, as before.
  - **Worth** (`FoodStore.findGrass`, `worth`): a forager takes the known plant worth the most: the pieces left on it to the power 0.75, divided by its distance from the nearest open way out plus 4. A full hay bale (48 pieces) beats a tuft of grass (4 pieces) right by a hole anywhere within the food radius (24); a hay bale nearly used up is worth no more than grass. At most 4 cut at a hay bale at once (as before), the rest take the next best. The trails and the sentry line follow on their own.
  - **Scouts** (`entity/goal/ScoutGoal`, foragers, at foraging time, while the colony wants food): a forager with no known plant to go for walks to a random open spot on the ground 8 to 24 blocks from the entrance (outside the nest and the mound) and looks round for 2 seconds; with known food still about 1 trip in 6 is a scouting trip, so a richer find farther out is made.
  - `/antcolonies list` adds `knows n food plants (m found, not yet told)`.
  - Tests: unit `FoodWorthTest`, `SpeciesProfileTest` (find radius); game test `TermiteFoodTests.aWorkerFindsAHayBaleAndTheForagersGoForItRatherThanTheGrass` (a worker put down by the hay by day finds it, the colony knows it only once that worker is inside; at night the foragers cut the hay and leave the grass by the holes). In the game tests scouts and foragers stay in their arena (`FoodMap.setTestArea`, set by `TermiteMoundTests.colony`): a neighbouring test's termites used to cut another test's grass, and walking past its holes set off its alarm. The other termite tests that are not about finding start with their food known (`FoodMap.knowForTests`).
  - Not done (the big "Smart foraging" later): scouts remember where they have looked, trails that fade or get stronger, far foraging beyond the food radius, food in unloaded land, dead wood worth more.
  - **Online backup (2026-10-13):** the project is on GitHub as a private repository, `https://github.com/urbad150-hub/ant-colonies-mod` (remote `origin`, every branch). After each commit, push it (`git push`) so the backup stays up to date.
  - **Git state (2026-10-13):** committed on branch `m7-food-finding` (commit c111c5d, with the arena spacing and the test fixes), made from `m7-alarm` (6c, committed but untested in-game). Not merged into master; the user tests 6c first, then food finding.
- **Overnight handover (user going to bed, 2026-10-12):** the user asked for tunnel plan steps 3 (termites crawl through tunnels) and 4 (termites build the cathedral: tunnel plan, windows, the "can it be built" test, 2-block reach only as a fallback) to be done together while they sleep; they will review everything in the morning. Step 2 (climbing the outside) is NOT confirmed in-game yet, so steps 2 to 4 stay uncommitted, or go in separate commits on a branch, never squashed into master, so the user can test them one at a time. Every pick made without the user goes into this file, marked "my pick, to confirm". When step 2's game tests were last run: the ramp and spire tests passed; `termitesRoamAllOverACathedralMound` had just been fixed twice (stepping away from a widening column before going down; lifting high enough to step over onto a ramp) and was being re-run; `workersCarrySeedsToTheFoodStore` failed once with 8 seeds instead of 5, most likely a neighbouring test's grass being clipped (the new tests moved the arenas; see bug 17): if it repeats, give it its own test batch.
- Code review notes (new session): pathing: the planner knew each step's kind but passed on only positions, so the walker guessed it every tick; most pathing bugs came from that guessing. DONE by the surface walking rewrite above. Still open: farmland and dirt paths (15/16 tall) are not floors for the planner, so ants likely avoid farms and village paths.
- Plan change (user): after the worker hunger comes a starvation mechanic for the whole colony (now designed and built, see above), and THEN M7 termites. The rest of the M6.5 backlog (age-based jobs, cleared circle, seed kinds beyond rich, day/night schedule, pebble cap, seed husks, nursery moves, fights, predators, rain, disease/fungus idea) is parked, not dropped.

- **Performance step 1, the stopwatch and the stress test (2026-10-14, branch `perf-measure` from `termite-holes`):** `debug/Perf` times each server tick's share of our mod in parts (insects: vanilla body and goals; walking: the navigator; route searches, with how many, how many failed and how many search steps; the colony brain), each part on its own, server thread only, and reports every 5 seconds to the log (`[perf]`) and to `/antcolonies perf`, with the whole server's tick time beside it. `/antcolonies stress <count>` makes the nearest colony keep that many real mobs out (workers added to its numbers if it has too few, 10 a second come out where no player can see; not saved), `/antcolonies stress off` ends it (`Population.setStress`). Game test `StressTests.stressTermiteColony` (only runs when named, `ANTCOLONIES_TESTS=stresstests`; `TestFilter` leaves every test starting with `stress` out of full runs): a termite colony with 400 workers in its numbers at 60 then 100 real termites, building and at foraging time. **First results** (flat test arena, young mound, no player, no food): 60 termites about **0.8 ms a tick** building, **2.5 ms** at foraging time (about 45 route searches a second, most failing after about 300 search steps: foragers with nothing to find); 100 termites about **1.3 ms** a tick, worst single tick 23 ms (one 21.5 ms route search). A tick has 50 ms. Waiting for the user's in-game numbers (real world, cathedral mound, food, the client's frame rate).
- **The animation engine (built overnight 2026-10-16 on branch `anim-engine` in the worktree `C:/Ant Mod film`, made from `anim-camera` with `brain-offline` merged in; NOT merged into `brain-offline`, waiting for the user's OK; nothing of it confirmed in-game yet; every number my pick, to confirm, also listed in `docs/DESIGN.md`):**
  - **Motion library** (`tools/ant_motion.py`, used by `tools/ant_models.py`): each leg worked out backwards from where its foot must be (hip swing and lift, knee, ankle, and a hip roll for a leg lifted clear of the body), so planted feet never slide; `stand` (every foot where it stood, or where it is told), `walk` (the tripod gait from sweep, duty and lift), `Track` (key frames, simplified). **Walk and hot walk rebuilt** with it: sweep 3.2 units, duty 0.55, lift 0.9; stride 0.48 body lengths a step (real ants 0.5 to 0.7); the hot walk lifts the body on straighter knees and the gaster at the waist, not by hip stilts. Each walking clip carries a stride note so the game plays it at the speed that keeps the feet planted (commits 03e756e, 2bf8e64).
  - **Three animation players** per insect (`entity/body/AntAnimationController`): body and legs (at the pace of the ground walked), head and antennae (at the insect's own pace), extras; each insect has its own clock and pace, so ants standing about never move in step (02d358f).
  - **Naps** (`NapGoal`): an idle ant inside the nest falls asleep now and then (chance 0.033 a second, 800 to 1,600 ticks; queens 3,600 to 7,200), sleep and sleep with antenna twitches by turns, settling in over 20 ticks; any job, being hurt or the alarm wakes it. **Hot ground**: in the hottest hours of hot biomes ants stand and walk tall with the gaster up (`idle_hot`, `walk_hot`). Both in the species file's new `body_language` section; game test category `body` (036207d).
  - **Personality and the curious look** (`NoticeGoal`, step 6): each real insect draws boldness, curiosity and restlessness once (older ones bolder), saved; an idle harvester ant that sees a player move within 6 blocks may stop and look (`look`: forebody up, antennae pointed and flicking, head tilting and turned toward the player); bold ones walk up to about a block off, shy ones back off 2 blocks with jaws open (`look_wary`); Macrotermes workers are blind and freeze with antennae quivering when a player walks within 3 blocks or jumps, lands or sprints within 6 (e349831).
  - **Antenna cleaning** (step 7, `GroomGoal`; clips `groom_antenna_l`, `_r`, `_both`): an ant standing about with nothing to do now and then stops, lifts a front leg straight up, combs the antenna of that side through the strigil 3 times (head down and to that side, the antenna swung out), then draws the leg through its open jaws twice (forebody and head raised, the jaws closing on each draw) and sets the foot down where it stood. Chance 0.05 a second times 0.5 to 1.5 by restlessness, both antennae 40% of the time (`body_language.groom`); never with something in its jaws, asleep, noticing, hurt, wet or in an alarm. Queens too. Termites have no cleaning clips yet. The leg moves between holds on a bowed path (out, up and back) so it never passes through the body or head; every lab check passes.
  - **Digging and carrying** (step 8): while a worker bites soil loose (`DigGoal`, within reach of its block) it plays `dig`: forebody 4 and head 12 degrees down nodding with each bite, jaws snapping 3 times a second, the front legs raking the loose soil back by turns, antennae tapping the face. An ant with anything in its jaws (seed, pellet, egg, body) holds its head up 12 degrees with the jaws shut and the antennae high (`carry`, played by the head player over the walk). Ant models only (`hasWorkClips`); the termites keep their usual clips. The lab now counts a clip as walking only when most feet travel, so the raking front feet are not taken for a walk.
  - **The edge curve** (step 9, `client/BodyLanguage`, from `InsectModel`): the front's turns (up or down a wall, a ceiling, a ramp, round a turn) are added up each tick and the waist bends by how far the front has turned in the last 4 ticks, shared 40/35/25 by the petiole, postpetiole and gaster joints, at most 60 degrees up or down and 25 to the side; so going over an edge the front tips first and the gaster follows. The live layer (head look and edge curve) is skipped beyond 32 blocks from the camera. The ant queen now uses `InsectModel` too (head look and waist).
  - **Steam off hot ants** (the user's one knowingly unrealistic touch): an ant on hot ground now and then gives off a small wisp of white smoke off its back (client side, about one every 30 ticks, `tickSteam`); none off a sleeping ant.
  - Tests: `BodyTests` (category `body`): naps, noticing and personality as above, plus cleaning (an idle worker cleans and stands again; one holding a seed and a termite never clean); unit test `WorkerAnimationsTest` (every worker clip reads as GeckoLib reads it, and the cleaning clips last exactly as long as the ant holds still for them). Checks: `python tools/anim_lab.py check worker_ant` passes every clip (0 problems).
  - Found while building: the game only looks for new goals on every other tick (`Mob.serverAiStep`), so a goal that asks "once a second" must use an even offset from the mob's id (the first `GroomGoal` never ran).
- **The review's top three (2026-10-16, branch `anim-engine`, asked for by the user after `docs/plans/animation-engine-review.md`; every number my pick, to confirm, also in `docs/DESIGN.md`; walks, climbing and turning confirmed in-game by the user 2026-10-16, "they look amazing"):**
  - **The stride grows with speed** (review item 1): real ants walk faster mostly by longer strides, with less of each step on the ground (Wahl et al. 2015). Three walk clips, picked by the speed in body lengths a second: `walk_slow` (below 1.6: stride 0.26 body lengths, duty 0.68, middle legs 0.72, a looser tripod `LOOSE_TRIPOD` whose three legs touch down 0.08 of a step apart), `walk` (1.6 to 3.7: 0.37, duty 0.62, middle legs 0.67), `walk_fast` (above: 0.48, duty 0.55). Each carries its band in a `speeds` note and the moment of its step where the two tripods swap in a `swap` note. `AntAnimationController.gearFor` picks the clip (one gear up or down only 10% past a band's edge); a change from one walk to another (or to a turn) waits for the old clip's swap moment and goes on from the new clip's own (no blend, so the feet never pop), at most 5 ticks. In the game: workers on the floor (5.5 body lengths a second) use `walk_fast`, climbing workers (2.4) `walk`, slow walkers `walk_slow`. 0.48 body lengths is the most the placeholder model's legs reach (longer, the feet miss the ground or the front legs go through the head), so at full speed ours still step about twice as often as a real ant would. Motion library: `walk(middle_duty=...)`, `walk_swap`, `LOOSE_TRIPOD` (`tools/ant_motion.py`); the lab judges a clip with a band at the band's middle speed.
  - **The waist bends by distance** (review item 2, `AbstractAntEntity.tickWaist`): "follow the leader": a ring of the front's pitch and yaw at each bit of path walked (64 marks); the gaster lies the way the front lay, on average, over the stretch of path it covers (`GASTER_START` 2.4 to `GASTER_END` 5.2 model units behind the middle), so an ant stopped halfway over an edge keeps its bend, and slow and fast climbers bend alike. Over an outside edge it hangs down only while the waist joints (`WAIST_AT` 0.64 to 2.4 units behind) are still over the edge: `bend = max(front − gaster, min(front − waist joints, 0))`. (The first version took the front's pose 5 units back at one point; on top of a wall the gaster then stayed bent down into the stone for several ticks, seen by the user in game.) A curve tighter than 3 units across is the body turning where it stands, which turns the gaster with it (no bend); standing still more than 20 ticks, a side bend slowly eases out.
  - **Legs step when turning on the spot** (review item 3): clips `turn_r` and `turn_l` (`ant_motion.turn`: each foot steps round an arc about the body's middle, 40 degrees a cycle, front feet down 0.4 of the cycle and set wider so they never pass through the head); played when the body turns faster than 2 degrees a tick (until below 1) and turning moves the outer feet more than walking does, at the speed of the turn. Not on hot ground; ants only. The lab checks a turning clip's planted feet against the turn (0 problems).
  - **The in-game camera learned to send an insect about** (`client/FilmDirector`): `walker` (an insect with its goals taken away), `go` (it walks its own route there), `look` (it keeps looking at a point, so it turns as in the game), `follow` (the camera rides along with it) and `log` (one line a tick about its drawn body: surface, tilt, heading, walk and turn speeds, waist bend, clip). New scene `tools/film/climb.film`: up a 2-block wall, over the top, down the far side, then turning round where it stands.
  - **What the climbing film showed:** the waist bends the right way at all four corners (at the foot of the wall going up the gaster stays along the floor; over the top edge it hangs down the wall; going over the far edge the front tips down first; at the far foot the gaster stays up the wall), and turning on the spot plays `turn_l` with the waist straight. It also showed an **older bug, fixed**: on a wall the game's yaw faces the wall, so an ant coming down a wall head first landed facing back into the wall and its body swung round about 180 degrees as it walked off (a sideways bend of the gaster too). Now the drawn body goes on the way its head pointed on the wall (`turnBody`, client side only).
  - **The user's report: "the abdomen is all messed up when climbing and phases into the stone on top of the wall"** (2026-10-16). Three causes, all fixed:
    - **The bend piled up frame after frame** (`client/BodyLanguage.turnBy`): GeckoLib clears every bone's "moved" mark after playing the clips and only puts an unmarked bone back to its clip (or resting) pose next frame; setting a turn marks it. So a bone the playing clip does not move (the postpetiole in every walk, all three waist joints in the dig, carry and cleaning clips) kept this frame's bend and got the next one added on top, every frame (at 60 frames a second the gaster spun round), and as the model's bones are shared, the next ant drawn got it too. Now the marks are put back as they were after the live layer turns a bone. The film draws about one frame a tick, so it only showed the bend about twice too big.
    - **The gaster stayed bent too long on top** (the waist rule above).
    - **The body tipped over an outside edge too late** (`entity/body/EdgeOver`, `AbstractAntEntity.turnBody`, `AntRenderer.applyRotations`; client, the body only): the navigator switches surface only when the body's middle reaches the edge, so the front half stood up in the air above the top of a wall, or stuck out level over a drop. Now near a full block's top edge (the body within 25 degrees of straight over it, floor to wall or wall to floor) the drawn body is put across the edge from the insect's position and the blocks: the tip starts when the front feet (3.6 model units ahead of the middle) reach the edge and ends when the hind feet (2.85 behind) are over it, the body lying along the line from the hind feet to the front feet, through the edge, as far off the surface as the usual poses draw it. At both ends of the stretch it agrees with the usual pose, so it never jumps; leaving it part way, what is left of its shift halves each tick. No route needed, so the legs layer is untouched. Known limit: the clip's legs do not reach for the surfaces, so mid-tip the front feet hang a little above the top (needs leg IK). Film: up the side and over the top the tilt goes 90, 87, 80, 71, 62, 51, 40, 29, 18, 10, 3, 0; over the far edge 0, 7, 16, 25, 36, 48, 59, 70, 79, 86, 90; the body's middle never drawn in the stone.
  - **Cleaning, naps and noticing lasted twice as long as their numbers** (found by the `body` game test failing now and then: "the cleaning ant went on cleaning for 125 ticks"; `docs/BUGS.md` 35): the game ticks a running goal only every other tick unless it asks for every tick (`Mob.serverAiStep`), and `GroomGoal`, `NapGoal` and `NoticeGoal` count their time in ticks, so a both-antennae cleaning (104 ticks) took about 220 and outlasted its clip, and naps and looks were twice the picks in `docs/DESIGN.md`. Now they ask for every tick (`requiresUpdateEveryTick`). Many older goals count ticks the same way (milling, eating, carrying, patrolling and more); not checked or changed here.
  - **Film tools:** `times.txt` gives each picture's tick (`drawn`) and the waist bend; the climbing scene's wall is now a one-block pillar (a long wall's near end looked lower than where the ant was, by perspective); `log` prints the edge pose's shift.
- **Faster, surer filming (2026-10-16, branch `anim-engine`; the user asked for a quicker way to keep the animations' quality: "build the automatic film checks and filming many scenes in one launch"):**
  - **Many scenes in one launch:** `./gradlew runFilm -PfilmScene=tools/film/climb.film,tools/film/work.film` (or a folder, or `-PfilmScene=all` for every scene in `tools/film`). Between scenes the camera clears every insect, makes the floor new round the middle and sets itself back; each scene's pictures, logs and `times.txt` go into `run-film/screenshots/<scene>/` (`quit` now ends only its own scene). The game starts once instead of once a scene: climb and work together took 107 seconds.
  - **Automatic checks** (`client/FilmChecks`): for every picture, where each box of each filmed insect was really drawn in the world. It flags **inside** (a part drawn more than 0.01 blocks inside a solid block, with the block) and **jump** (a part moving more than 0.03 blocks a tick and 3 times its usual move in the two pictures before and after: a pop, measured against the insect itself so walking about does not count). Results in each scene's `checks.txt`; one summary line per scene in `run-film/screenshots/checks.txt`, so only the named pictures need looking at. Thresholds my pick, to confirm. Found while building it: GeckoLib's "local space" bone matrix has the do-nothing matrix added to it (its `translateMatrix` adds a whole matrix), so the checks take it back off.
  - **Several frames per tick:** the `frames N` step draws each burst tick N times, at 0, 1/N, ... of the way to the next tick (as a fast screen shows it between ticks), the insect's place included; the climbing scene films its three-quarter turn at 3 frames a tick.
  - **First findings (both fixed the same day, next bullet):** coming down the far side of the pillar the body goes from upright on the wall to flat on the floor between two pictures (tilt 90, 60, 30, 0 in three ticks) and the gaster pokes 0.02 blocks into the stone as it lands; turning on the spot the body turns in 20-degree steps every other tick (yaw 249.8, 249.8, 229.9, 229.9, ...), so the turn stutters; going up, a hind foot dips 0.02 into the grass for a picture. Nothing in the work scene.
- **The foot of a wall and turning on the spot, smoothed (2026-10-16, branch `anim-engine`; the user: "fix the wall-to-floor drop at the foot of a climb down and the stuttering turn on the spot"; the two first findings of the film checks):**
  - **Round an inside corner** (`EdgeOver.findCorner`, `EdgeOver.Body.round`): where a wall meets the floor, a real ant bridges the corner: its front feet are already on the new surface while its hind feet are still on the old one, and the body tips across bit by bit as it walks. Now the drawn body does the same, coming down a wall onto the floor and going from the floor up a wall: the tilt follows how far the body has gone round the corner (eased, so it starts and ends softly). Climb film: coming down, tilt 90, 82, 68, 56, 47, 30, 8, 0 (was 90, 60, 30, 0 with the gaster 0.02 blocks in the stone); going up 0, 7, 30, 56, 80, 90; no part inside a block. Confirmed in-game by the user 2026-10-17 ("everything works").
  - **Turning on the spot** (`entity/body/BodyTurn`, used by every insect): the game's own rule let the head turn up to 75 degrees off a standing body and then snapped the body 20 degrees every other tick. A real ant's neck turns only a little; it pivots its whole body. Now a standing insect's body swings toward its head every tick (0.3 of the way, at most 15 degrees a tick; my picks, to confirm), smoothly, and ends facing where it looks. Confirmed in-game by the user 2026-10-17 ("everything works").
  - **The jump check** now also needs a jump to be 1.5 times bigger than the part's usual big moves (its 90th percentile), so a leg's ordinary quick swing is not flagged.
  - Left as is: when the ant walks off the foot of the wall, its gaster swings down from the wall's line in about two ticks (the waist following the front, 0.19 blocks a tick); it looks like a quick real settle, to watch in-game.
  - Game tests: `body,legs` all 23 pass. Once, in the first run, `insectsNoticeMovingPlayersByTheirPersonality` failed with the termite never freezing at the jump; it passed on its own twice and in the next `body,legs` run, so a rare flake, to watch.
- **Films without relaunching the game (2026-10-18, idea M2 of `docs/plans/animation-engine-upgrade-review.md`; the user's priority: faster animation making at the same quality; built overnight in session `film-reload`, branch `claude/film-reload-2c4721`, not tried by the user yet):** the in-game camera can stay open. `tools/film.sh watch <scenes>` opens a film session in the background (`./gradlew runFilm -PfilmWatch -PfilmDebug`) and films the scenes; after that the camera waits in the world (`client/FilmWatch`, small hooks in `FilmDirector`: `reshoot`, `halt`, the scene list). When a file in `src/main/resources/assets/antcolonies` changes (the motion library rewriting the clips) it copies the changed files to `build/resources/main` (what the running game reads) and reloads them: animations and models through GeckoLib's own cache only (0.1 s), anything else such as textures through the game's full resource reload (1.2 s); a file written again with the same contents is not counted. A changed shot list also refilms. `tools/film.sh shoot [scenes]` films again on request (and switches scenes), `stop` closes it, `status` says what it does; the camera writes `run-film/film-watch/status.txt` for the script. A change that comes while it is filming drops that shoot and starts again (the model tool writes its files about 10 s apart). **Java changes:** `tools/film.sh swap` compiles and swaps the changed method code into the open game through the Java debugger (`tools/film_swap.py`, `jdb`; Java's own hot swap, about 3 s); a change hot swap cannot do (a new method, field or lambda) is refused by Java, and `swap` then relaunches straight into the same scenes by itself (`tools/film.sh restart` does it on purpose); it warns when a class's starting values changed (a swap does not set them again). Short scene names: `-PfilmScene=climb`, `guard` for a folder. **Measured:** work scene, one look by a fresh launch 46 to 50 s, by a reshoot 21 to 23 s (change to pictures; the scene itself is 20 s); climb scene, fresh 80 to 90 s, reshoot 56 to 58 s (the scene is 55 s); a Java tweak swapped and filmed 22.5 s (work). So the launch, about 25 to 30 s a look, is gone; what is left is the scene's own playing. Checked: a changed head turn in a clip and a Java change in the live layer both show in the new pictures; the checks after a reshoot match a fresh launch's (climb: 25 to 28 jumps either way, the same worst). Found and fixed while building: after a reload the checks read frozen numbers (FilmChecks switches on its bone tracking once per model and the reload makes new bones; FilmWatch switches it on again: 57 false jumps before, 26 after). Picks, to confirm: files must stay still for 2 looks half a second apart before a reshoot; the session listens for the debugger on port 5005 (one film session at a time); the fresh world is kept each launch (reusing a world would save about 2 s but could carry one scene's leftovers into the next).
- **Faster bursts, the same pictures every run (2026-10-18, the user asked: "fix the 3 tick wait"; branch `claude/film-reload-2c4721`):** each burst picture waited a fixed 3 client ticks after its `tick step`, about two thirds of the climbing scene. Now `client/FilmStep` knows when a step has come through: the game sends a hidden marker message at the end of each stepped tick (messages arrive in order, so everything before it is there too) and the camera takes its picture on the first still tick after it.
