Two timing races found on the way, both in the old camera too: (1) the client ran its stepped tick before the game's news arrived on 16 of climb's 186 ticks (luck of how the client's and the game's 20-a-second beats lined up), drawing the ant a tick behind there: now the client's stepped tick is held until its marker is here; (2) a frozen game still runs empty ticks and may send an insect's state to the client on any of them (each insect counts its own sends), and how many came between two pictures depended on timing (about 5 with the fixed wait, 0 to 2 with a fast camera): now, within a burst, the game holds still at the end of each stepped tick until the camera has its picture (at most 3 s) and goes straight on, with no empty ticks, as in a real game. Also a picture is never taken on the stepped tick itself (the game is drawn blended by real time there). FilmDirector: two lines in the burst code, one in `halt`. **Times (a fresh launch of poses, work and climb):** 149 s before, 108 s now; climb itself 55 s before, 27 s now. **Same pictures:** poses and work clip moments identical to the old camera's (their pictures differ only by random particles: soil, steam). Each burst now comes out the same every launch; two full launches gave the same climb (positions, turn log, checks: 20 jumps, worst abdomen 0.213 in down_58). Still open, as with the old camera: between two bursts (the `go` to the next wall) and before the freeze the game is not held, so a fresh climb-only launch and a watch-mode reshoot came out differently (29 and 13 jumps; the reshoot matched the full launch through the whole climb up and parted at tick 111, just after it; the old camera 25 to 27). Holding it there too needs either every camera command run inside the hold or a small patch to Minecraft's entity tracker (a mixin, which the mod has none of yet): a follow-up. Climb's path is NOT the old one (it differs from tick 52, race 1). Old climb pictures kept for comparing need filming again.
- **The climbing film the same on every launch, gaps included (2026-10-18, follow-up of the faster bursts; branch `claude/interesting-bhaskara-5655c9` from `claude/film-reload-2c4721`):** the gaps between bursts still followed timing. Picked the hold (no mixin needed): while frozen for filming, the game's thread never runs an empty tick; it holds still and does only what the camera asks, in order (`FilmStep.serve`: the camera's commands now go straight to the game's thread with the walker's orders, no longer as chat messages, so a `go` can never come before a `tick freeze`), from the freeze until `tick unfreeze`; it lets go after 3 s with nothing asked, and at once when the camera quits. Four more timing or history leaks found and fixed on the way: (1) the walker's random id set its own pace for standing and antennae (0.88 to 1.12): it now has a fixed id giving 1.0; (2) the game sends an insect's place every 20 of its ticks counted from when it came, and the walker stood a timing-dependent number of ticks before the freeze: `walker` now comes into a frozen game and steps exactly 2 + 40 ticks (new step `step N`; the game is left frozen); (3) in a "frames 3" burst a frame drawn between the stepped tick and the next picture drew the next tick's 2/3, running the clip clock on too far, only when such a frame came (`FilmDirector.drawPartial` now holds the drawn time still while a step is on its way); (4) filmed alone the walker was out of the camera's view during its first ticks, so its clip started later; and GeckoLib's one clock and one set of bones per model kept the earlier scenes' insects' state: `walker` now puts a camera on it first and sets every model's clock and bones back as a fresh game has them (`freshclocks`). **Checked:** two fresh launches of poses, work and climb and one climb-only launch: climb.txt, turn.txt, times.txt and all 251 climb pictures byte for byte the same; checks.txt the same but for the insect's network number (#39 or #3, by where climb is in the launch); a watch-mode first shoot and reshoot the same too. Climb now 19 jumps (worst antenna_r_2 0.190 in up_21), different from the 20 to 42 before (its path and send rhythm are now the real game's, with no empty ticks); poses and work unchanged. Times: climb 27.55 s (was about 27.4); a three-scene launch about 111 s. `./gradlew build` passes. Old climb pictures need filming again.

- **Grama grass, the harvester ants' rich seed (M10's grama moved up by the user, 2026-10-16, before the range work; decisions in `docs/DESIGN.md`, "Custom plants"; branch `claude/range-and-danger-cc35f9`).** Two kinds share one block (`block/GramaGrassBlock`): **blue grama** (meadows 4 to 7 blocks across on Plains, Sunflower Plains and Meadow) and **black grama** (loose bunches round dead bushes in all three Badlands, also on sand and red sand). Its life (`block/GramaLife`, pure logic with unit tests `GramaLifeTest`): a planted seed comes up young and grows into leaves in about 1 day; rain in the world (not on the plant, since the badlands never show rain) sends up seed stalks, so a whole stand flowers together and ripens about 2 days later; ripe heads hold seed about 3 days, now and then shedding a seed on the ground (at most 2 lying round a plant), then dry off for about 2 days and go back to leaves; with no rain for about 5 days a grown plant seeds anyway (all my picks, to confirm). The ants cut ripe heads only (`food.plants` entries with `"state": {"stage": "ripe"}`: 16 seeds a blue head, 12 a black one, my picks) and fetch shed seeds like any seed; a head cut bare dries off and the plant lives on (new `EatenInStages.afterLastPiece`, used by `FoodStore.eatAway` for real ants and the numbers alike). The player: seeds to plant (`blue_grama_seeds`, `black_grama_seeds`, in the `seeds` tag), bone meal moves a stage on, a ripe plant broken gives 1 to 3 seeds; creative stacks "(grown)" and "(ripe)". World generation: `worldgen/GramaPatchFeature` with NeoForge biome modifiers; every plant of a stand is at the same stage. Looks B (Field detail) for both, picked by the user in the viewer (`python tools/model_viewer.py grama`); dried black grama made a weathered grey-brown so it shows (user). Art and data files written by `tools/grama_art.py`. Tests: `GramaLifeTest` (4), `SpeciesProfileTest.harvesterAntsCutGramaOnlyWhenRipe`, and the seed scene (`workersCarrySeedsAndClipGrassIntoTheFoodStore`) now has a ripe grama plant: every piece of the head is cut and stored, it dries off instead of breaking, and its shed seeds are fetched too. `antnest,away`: 34 of 34 pass (12.5 minutes on 8 servers). **Confirmed in-game by the user 2026-10-17** (meadows, flowering after rain, ants cutting ripe heads, planting and bone meal: "it all works").
- **Bigger grama meadows now and then (user, 2026-10-17):** blue grama meadows were all 5 to 9 blocks across; real blue grama grows in mats over whole stretches of shortgrass prairie, so now about 1 meadow in 8 is a big one, 15 to 20 blocks across (radius 7 to 10 in `worldgen/configured_feature/blue_grama.json`, a weighted list; the feature allows a radius up to 12). The rest stay 5 to 9. Black grama bunches unchanged. The game loads the file (far-meadow test passes); no test makes worldgen, so not seen in a world yet. Big meadows confirmed in-game by the user 2026-10-17 ("everything works").
- **Seed balance (user, 2026-10-17, from playing):** short grass was far too rich (10 seeds a tuft, and the plains are covered in it). Now a tuft is gone after **one take** (it grows back as before) and a ripe grama plant, blue or black, lasts **10 takes** (was 16 and 12) before it dries off. All in `red_harvester_ant.json` (`food.plants`). Recruiting scaled with it so it means the same: a rich patch near home is 15 pieces (1.5 ripe plants, was 24), one nestmate called per 10 pieces (one per plant, was 16). Tests: `SpeciesProfileTest` numbers, the away tufts in `AwayTests` now follow the profile's grass number (no more pre-cut 8 of 10).
- **Fix: the game froze (the user's game, 2026-10-17, `docs/BUGS.md` 43):** drawing an ant with no clip playing yet crashed the game (`AntAnimationController.gearFor` asked a `List.of` list for the place of nothing); nothing playing now counts as no walk yet. The save that hung after the crash is not explained by the log; a thread dump is needed if it comes again. Build passes; not tried in-game yet.
- **Fix: ants picking up dropped seeds and dropping them again** (the user's game, 2026-10-17, `docs/BUGS.md` 44): with the food store full and bread wanted, more foragers went for seeds than the mill had places for, and the rest dropped theirs on the spot and picked them up again. Now an ant takes a seed (off the ground or off a plant, `GatherSeedGoal`, `ClipGrassGoal`) only when it has somewhere to take it right then (`Bakery.hasPlaceForASeed`: a place in the store, held for it until it puts the seed down, `FoodStore.claimStorePlace`, or a mill place); otherwise the seed lies until there is room. The colony counts seeds dropped (`FoodStore.seedDropped`, for tests). New game test `NestTests.seedsLyingByAFullStoreAreLeftThereAndFetchedOnceThereIsRoom` (fails on the old code). `antnest,away,colony` all 48 pass; full run 111 of 113 on 8 servers (the middle-hole flake and the quarantined fallen tree, both passing alone; termite workers use the same goals, and `termitefood,mound,soldiers` all pass). Not tried in-game yet.

- **Long trails, step 1: trails that grow from their tip (brain 4c part 2 rethought; the user's decisions 2026-10-17, plan `docs/plans/long-trails.md`; branch `claude/range-and-danger-cc35f9`).** The range is the trails, not a circle. Each trunk trail's line now runs out to `trails.trunk.most_length` (96 blocks, the user's "up to 96 or more"); its **tip** (`TrunkTrails.reachBlocks`) is as far as its trips have gone, so a trip to food past the tip moves it out. **Traffic holds it** (the user's pick, like real ants): `TrunkNumbers.holds` gives `blocks_per_daily_trip` (6, my pick) blocks for each trip a day the trail gets; where its strength no longer holds its length, the tip pulls back (`TrunkTrails.pullBack`): the outer columns' wear is forgotten and their bare ground grows back, as a faded trail's does. `Colony.foragingRadius()` is now the longest trail's tip, at least `food.radius`, which for the harvester ant is now **12, a young colony's reach** (was 24; real young colonies forage a few metres out). A lone searcher out along a trail fans out past that trail's own tip (its drift reaches the tip plus the stretch, `ScoutGoal.reachOfWay`), not to the colony's longest trail. **Plant scan diet:** a colony with trunk trails no longer scans the whole square out to its reach (over 40,000 columns at 104 blocks); it scans the young reach plus the stretch, a band along each trail out past its tip (`TrunkTrails.columnsNear`), and the ground round each of its real workers farther out; plants out there count up to 24 blocks above or below the nest (long trails run over hills). Kept as before: the nest area (eggs, larvae, dead ants) and the patrol's candidates still reach at least 24 blocks; termites scan as before. Seeds on the ground are now looked for out to the search reach (was the food radius). Tests: `SpeciesProfileTest.aTrailReachesAsFarAsItsTrafficHolds`; the trail scene (`foragersWearLastingTrunkTrailsThatFadeAndGrowBack`) now checks that the range is the trails' tips, that a trip 30 blocks out moves the east tip out there, and that five days unused both tips pull back to about 12 blocks with their bare ground growing back. `antnest,away,colony` 46 of 46 (9.5 minutes on 8 servers); full run 110 of 111 (12.5 minutes on 8 servers; the one failure is the old middle-hole flake, `docs/BUGS.md` 22, which fails the same way without this change).
- **Long trails, steps 2 and 3: recruiting to rich patches and worth the walk (the user's decisions 2026-10-17, before the night's work; plan `docs/plans/long-trails.md`; branch `claude/range-and-danger-cc35f9`).** Real *Pogonomyrmex barbatus* and *P. rugosus* forage alone for scattered seeds, but a forager that finds a dense patch drags its sting tip home laying a scent line and touches nestmates, which follow it out. New part `colony/Recruits`. **A rich patch:** a real forager that cuts a piece or picks up a seed (`FoodStore.clippedGrass`, `GatherSeedGoal.take`) counts the food within 4 blocks: pieces left on the plants named in `food.search.recruit.plants` (blue and black grama; short grass is no patch) plus seeds lying there (the user: "plants and fallen seeds"). It is rich when that is at least `needed(distance)` = 24 pieces at 20 blocks or nearer, growing with the distance to the power 0.62 (about 48 at 60 blocks, 64 at 96: step 3, value against distance; my picks). **The scent line** (invisible, the user's decision): the finder's way home is noted point by point (every 3 blocks); once inside it runs about the nest calling for 10 seconds and touches the nestmates it meets (and at first those waiting by the entrance), up to **one nestmate for every 16 pieces in the patch, 1 to 8** (the user: "depend on how large the meadow"): foragers, or any idle worker (real reserve workers switch to foraging when recruited, Gordon), the nearest first; each is told the patch (its spot now) and the door lets it out at once (`Door.mayGo`). Too few real ones waiting: forager numbers go instead (they hold the patch as their spot, `Door.recruitedNumbers`). **Recruits follow the line** single file (the user's decision; `ScoutGoal.followLine`: every recruit walks the same points) and pass poorer food by on the way (`Recruits.passesBy`, also in `ClipGrassGoal` and `GatherSeedGoal`); at the patch they cut like any forager, and each that brings food from a patch still rich lays the line again, so a big patch draws a growing stream. The line halves every 3 minutes of game time unless laid again; a recruit that loses its line still has the patch as its spot. **The numbers recruit too:** a numbers search that finds a plant in a rich patch (by the plants the colony remembers) sends more of them there (`FieldLife.searchOnce`). Lines become trunk trails by their trips, as before. **Worth the walk (step 3):** a cleared trunk trail is walked about twice as fast as grass (`TrunkTrails.ON_TRAIL` 0.5, my pick; real foragers run faster and straighter on their trails, Fewell 1988), so a plant's trip is counted out along the trail to where the forager would leave it, then off it (`TrunkTrails.walkTo`, used by `FoodStore.findGrass`): food out along a trail is worth more than food as far off it, and a forager leaves the trail only for food worth the extra walk. Scattered seeds far out are still picked up by a searcher who comes across one, never recruited to. `/antcolonies list` shows " | recruiting: 1 scent line (7 blocks out), 2 on their way, 4 called out". Not saved (a line fades in minutes). Tests: `SpeciesProfileTest.aRichPatchIsWorthAWalkThatGrowsWithItsSize` (the needed food by distance, the recruits by patch size, grama counts and short grass does not); the seed scene now has two ripe blue grama side by side (a rich patch of 32 seeds) and checks that a scent line is laid only to it, that nestmates are called out, and that one called out takes food at the patch. the app could not start one from an automated wake-up while the user slept, so it was built here (the same branch, one copy for the morning). **What already followed the trails since step 1:** `Colony.foragingRadius()` is the longest tip, so the food map's look, the trail wear, the population's area (range + 16), the door's forager count and the break-in check all reach out with the trails; the plant scan looks only at the young square (12 + the 8 of stretch), a band along each trail out past its tip, and the ground round each real worker out farther (never a 104-block square). **Kept on purpose:** the patrol walks only the near part of each way (`Patrol.reach`, at most the food radius: real *P. barbatus* patrollers work the nest mound and the starts of the trails, Gordon), food far out along a way opens once that way's patroller is back (any distance); the nest area for eggs, larvae and bodies stays at least 24 (a forager that dies far out on a trail is left there, as real ones are); the alarm is termite-only and only fires for an enemy near a hole or a forager, so a long range does not make it jumpy. **Fixes:** the door worked out whether the colony is small only while the patrol was on, so in game tests (patrol off) every colony counted as young forever and its searchers never walked out its trails (`docs/BUGS.md` 41): the foragers are now counted door or no door. **The test fence:** in game tests a colony's trails stop at its own arena's edge (`TrunkTrails.setTestFence`, set by `gametest/TestIsolation` for every colony founded in a test). **New test, the far meadow** (`TrailTests.aFarMeadowPastTheTrailsTipIsFoundRecruitedToAndPullsTheTrailOut`, category `colony`, batch `far_meadow`): a finished nest with 30 real workers, an old trail east whose food is gone (tip 26 blocks out), and 35 ripe blue grama 34 to 40 blocks out, past the tip, on a moving test clock. A searcher fanning out past the tip finds the meadow; the tip moves out to it (34 to 36) and the range with it; finders lay scent lines home and call nestmates out (only to the meadow), and at least 2 called out take food there; the meadow taken away, once the last finders are home the line fades within a day and the tip pulls back (from 35 to 22 two aged days later). Passes alone in about 30 seconds. Also `Recruits.ageForTests`. Seen in the test: with most foragers out searching, a recruiter home often finds nobody inside to call ("called out 0"), as a real small crew would; a big colony's numbers fill in. Full run on 8 servers: 111 of 112 passed (17 minutes); the one failure was the old middle-hole flake (`docs/BUGS.md` 22), which then passed 3 of 4 times alone. Not tried in-game yet.

Checked four ways: the camera's own checks (`FilmChecks`) against the approved findings; body checks from the walker's log (reached every place it was sent; faces the way it walks on the floor; no stop-start turns, docs/BUGS.md 36; no snapped turns or tips, the old inside-corner bug; the three walks played); every picture against the approved ones (240 x 136 grey copies in `references/guard_films/`, 5.9 MB; two runs with no change gave the same pictures, so a change shows); the drawing time (`client/FilmFrameMeter`, new: `run-film/screenshots/frames.txt`, the drawing time of each frame per scene). - **Body plans and the gait maker** (2026-10-18, branch `claude/nostalgic-engelbart-1cf92f`; the animation upgrade review's M3, B1 + B2; the user's words: "Lets start with the gait and body plan maker"; plan `docs/plans/body-plans-and-gaits.md`). **Body plans:** every geo model has a plan, `assets/antcolonies/body_plans/<model>.json` (listed in `index.json`): a rename table from **role names** (the names the tools' code uses, today the placeholder bone names) to the model's **bone names** (the right-hand side, the only part that changes when the user's Blockbench worker, M2, arrives with its own names), the legs (key `l1`..`r3`, bones hip, knee, foot), the head (jaws, antennae, the `carry` socket), the head player's bones, and the waist joints with their share of the bend. Read by the tools (`tools/body_plan.py`; the lab, the motion library and the clip writer work in role names, clips are renamed to bone names as they are written) and by the game (`entity/body/BodyPlans`: the head player's bones, the load's bone in `PelletLayer`, the head and waist `BodyLanguage` bends, through `InsectModel`; a bone no plan names keeps the old name rules). New commands: `python tools/body_plan.py check` (every plan against its model: named bones exist, no bone left out, each leg three bones and a partner), `new <model>` (a starter plan), `rename-test` (renames every worker bone to a made-up name and proves the lab reads, checks and walks it exactly the same, all 17 clips). **The gait maker** (`ant_motion.gait(legs, lag, sides, lost)`, Wilson 1966): steps run as a wave from the back leg to the front on each side, a fixed lag apart, the sides half a cycle apart; lag 0.53 and 0.58 give the ant's two hand-typed step tables exactly (`TRIPOD`, `LOOSE_TRIPOD` are now made by it); it also gives the spider's alternating tetrapod, a four-legged walk and trot, the centipede's waves and the gait with lost legs (both middle legs lost: diagonal pairs, Hughes 1957); the self-test checks them all. The walk and turn code no longer names legs (it asks the plan), so it works for any leg count. **Balance check** (new, `ant_motion.balance` and a line in the lab's check of every walk): how far the centre of weight stays inside the planted feet through the cycle. **Proof nothing changed:** every model and clip file the tools write is byte for byte the same; the lab's checks for the worker and every termite give the same lines (plus the balance line); the pose viewer page is the same; `./gradlew build` with a new unit test (`BodyPlansTest`: every plan's bones are in its model, and the game's answers are the old ones for every bone of every model); guard films: all checks pass; the first run showed changed pictures in the walk, ledge and termite scenes, but the unchanged code run the same day changed 1 (the walk's first picture, the same one), and filming those three scenes again on the new code changed 0 of 600, so they were run-to-run noise (the world loading and its light), not the body. **Found, not changed:** `walk_hot` has the centre of weight outside its feet for 23% of the cycle (up to 0.53 units; fine for a fast runner, to look at with the user); with today's duty factors a walk with lost legs is unbalanced (real insects keep their feet down longer: B5, later). My picks, to confirm: the role names (today's bone names), the plan file's layout, the termites' waist (the abdomen alone, 0.25, as before).
- **Termite walks (B11)** (2026-10-18, branch `claude/gracious-greider-989f2d`; plan `docs/plans/termite-walks.md`, the user's four decisions; the user's words: "Go: build the termite walks plan, step by step, and prove it with the lab checks and the guard films"). Every termite (worker, king, small queen, minor and major soldier) now walks with clips made by the motion library from its own body plan, like the ant: `walk_slow` (the loose tripod), `walk` and `walk_fast`, each with its stride, swap and speeds notes, and `turn_r` / `turn_l` for stepping round on the spot; the old hand-set walk's feet slid 3.4 to 3.8 times the walk speed. **Each model has its own clip file** (`termite_worker`, `termite_king`, `termite_queen`, `termite_minor_soldier`, `termite_major_soldier`; `termite_soldier.animation.json` is gone), so each stride note fits its size. **The antennae tap the ground ahead by turns while walking** (the user's decision: blind termites feel their way; each tip rests 0.1 units over the ground for a moment). **The soldiers' legs moved forward** under the heavy head (the user's decision): the minor's front legs turn 55 degrees forward and are longer, its middle legs 10; the major's front legs are the longest (3.2), join at the very front at height 1.2 and turn 45 degrees forward (of 140 layouts tried, the only one whose front feet reach the whole step clear of the head), its middle legs turn 25; with the king's legs the major would be outside its feet 63 to 65% of its walk. **The motion library** (`ant_motion.walk`, `turn`) sways the body plan's waist bones instead of the fixed `petiole` and `abdomen` (the ant's output byte for byte the same). **The game:** turning on the spot is played for any model whose file has `turn_r` with a `reach` note (`AntAnimationController.turnReach`; was ants only, and the fixed 5.8 units is now each model's own, the ant's 5.83); `ClientEvents` and `TermiteQueenModel` use each model's own file. **The lab's centre of weight fixed:** each box weighed by its own size, overlaps counted once (`anim_lab.Model.weights`); before, each box was weighed by the upright box round it, so thin slanted antennae and jaws and the queen's crossed skin boxes counted many times over (the queen's centre sat 0.9 units too far back, the ant's 0.5 too far forward). With it the ant still passes every check (its `walk_hot` is now inside its feet, its `walk_fast` 3% of the cycle 0.06 outside, allowed for a runner). **Proof:** `python tools/anim_lab.py check` for all five termites: every walk and turn without a BAD line, the slow and normal walks balanced; `./gradlew build` with `ModelFilesTest` (every termite file has its walks and turns, their notes, and moves only its own bones); guard films (two new scenes, `i_soldier` the major walking and turning, `j_king` the king walking slowly): no part inside a block on flat ground; the jumps the checks flag are fast stepping, not pops (the soldier turns up to 15 degrees a tick, so its turn plays about 3 times its own speed, the same kind as the ant's approved `b_turn`; the king walks about 1.55 body lengths a second, the top of the slow band, until the real speed step); in `f_termite` the tapping antenna tips go 0.02 to 0.04 blocks into Termite Mound Ramps (the tap aims at flat ground; like the approved legs in ramps); 103 of 350 pictures changed (the new walks); the pictures wait for the user's approval. The `legs` game tests: 21 of 21 on 4 servers. **Found, not changed:** the old `peek` clip (the look out of a chimney) has an antenna tip 0.42 to 0.49 into the floor and a front leg through the head, the same in the file before this work. **My picks, to confirm:** the sweeps (worker 1.4 / 1.6 / 1.8 units, king 1.6 / 1.9 / 2.2, queen 1.2 / 1.4 / 1.6, minor 1.4 / 1.6 / 1.6, major 1.8 / 2.0 / 2.2), a foot lifting half its hip's height, the forebody swaying 1.5 degrees and the abdomen 2, the body bobbing 0.04, the queen's middle and hind feet 0.3 and 0.4 back, the major's front feet 0.6 back and its wider turn, the tap's numbers (16 degrees up, 6 in, 12 out), key frames 120 / 60 / 40 and 80 for turns.
- **Brain step 4g, step 5: neighbours** (2026-10-18, branch `claude/pensive-moore-530c7e` from `brain-danger`, built in its own session while the main one built step 4; the user's answers before building in `docs/DESIGN.md`, "4g step 5"). New `colony/Neighbours` (saved, both species; `colony.neighbours` in the species files) and a saved colony age (`Colony.ageDays`, `bornAt`; older saves count from their first load). **Meetings:** once a second a surface worker within 3 blocks of an insect of another colony of its own species, or standing on such a colony's trunk trail (`TrunkTrails.isOnTrail`), counts a meeting (`AbstractAntEntity.lookForNeighbours`; the same insect or trail again only after a minute); termites: a gallery piece planned within 3 blocks of another Macrotermes colony's gallery, and a piece at the web's edge within a piece's length of one (checked in the upkeep, once a minute per piece), is a meeting for both colonies. Each meeting adds 1 to its place (places within 4 blocks are one), halving every 2 game days. **Yielding:** once a place holds the meetings the colony's age needs (4 at founding, evenly down to 1 at day 8) the colony turns away there and stays away until the place has faded to half that. Harvester ants (`DangerMap.avoids`, so real foragers, scouts, recruits and the numbers alike): nothing past the place within 30 degrees of its direction is foraged, the trail that way stops growing short of it, no searcher goes out it, no new trail is laid that way. Termites (`Galleries`): no tip grows that way, a piece planned that way is not dug, and a piece reaching near the neighbour (not one of the 4 arms) is walled off, packed with soil back to its fork by the filling-in rules (out of sight, nobody inside); a piece touching the other web is never dug, so the webs never join. `/antcolonies list` gets a "neighbours:" line (places, how many it yields to, galleries walled off, the meetings needed at its age). Found while building: an old colony with one meeting would have yielded only about 90 seconds (the memory starts fading at once), so a colony that turned away stays away until half; and a young termite colony stopped by the other web met it once and gave up, so the upkeep now senses a neighbour a piece's length ahead. New tests (`NeighbourTests`, category `colony`): `oldColoniesYieldToNeighboursAndYoungOnesPushOn` (two harvester colonies, real workers meeting halfway and on a trail, the old one yields at once, the young one at 4 meetings and not 3, no new trail its way, saved, the fade) and `neighbouringTermitesWallOffTheirGalleries` (two termite webs growing toward each other: the old one yields at once, the young one walls off after 4 meetings, the webs never touch). My picks, to confirm: the 3 blocks, a minute between meetings with one insect, 4 blocks per place, staying away until half, walling the whole piece. Not tried in-game yet.

Each kind is a data file like the species profiles (`data/antcolonies/tree_kind/umbrella_thorn.json`, `camel_thorn.json`, `baobab.json`, read by `tree/TreeKind`: sizes, crown, the two shapes mixed by seed, the kind's blocks). **Real growth (the user's pick at the start of step 2):** the grown tree's skeleton is made once from the seed (the same numbers in the same order as the viewer, so a grown tree is the approved one: block counts within 1%), and a younger tree is that skeleton grown part of the way out from the ground: wood only lengthens at its tips and thickens, never moves. A young tree is its leader stem (trunk, then the first limb) with a small crown; its low side branches are marked as ones the tree drops (self-pruning, falling as fallen branches in step 4); growing limbs carry a fan of young leafy shoots; the baobab's trunk at every height is the thickest it has been, its stubby top branches dropped and regrown as it swells. Two structures: the **big** trees (`structure/big_savanna_tree.json`, set `big_savanna_trees`: one per 6 by 6 chunks, at least 3 apart, so crowns rarely touch, about 15 percent of the ground under a crown) and the **young** ones (`young_savanna_tree`, one per 3 by 3 chunks, never within 2 chunks of a big one). Fitting the ground: a tree takes only air and plants (never water or rock, a hillside it just goes into), its roots take the soil at its foot, its foot is the lowest ground round the trunk, the trunk goes down up to 8 blocks where the ground dips, no tree where the ground under the trunk varies by more than 6 or is under water. New land's trees are written in by their structure piece (`GiantTreePiece`, from world generation's threads through a queue); a seedling planted by a player writes one (`TreeSeedlingBlock.setPlacedBy`). A tree whose trunk is cut through at its foot (fewer than half its foot blocks left) leaves the book the next time it is looked at. Dead trees are written in but stand as they are (step 5). **4b, growing** (`tree/TreeLife`, `TreePlacer.grow`): about once an in-game day each loaded living tree grows a step, even in sight; only the blocks that differ change; a new block goes only into air, grass, flowers, snow (soil for roots) or where its own block still is, never a player's, a colony's or another tree's block (the crown grows round it; my pick, to confirm); blocks its new shape no longer has go (shed twigs, leaves moving out), and shed twigs drop one fallen branch under them. Speeds (my picks, to confirm, inside the user's ranges): seedling to young in 2 to 4 days, grown 23 to 40 days after it came up, ancient after 100 to 170, by each tree's seed (0.75 to 1.3 times the normal speed). The shapes of a grown giant take about 70 ms to work out, so the game works them out in the background (`TreePlacer.placedSoon`) and only places the changes; the stopwatch has a new part, "giant trees growing" (`/antcolonies perf`). Bone meal (`ModEvents.onUseOnBlock`) on a seedling or any block of a young tree grows it one day's step at once, never past the end of young (age 0.45), and does nothing to a grown tree. **4c, seedlings after rain** (real: acacia seeds sprout in browsers' dung far from the parent, mostly after a wet spell): the days of rain are written down; the morning after a rainy day each 64 by 64 square of loaded savanna within 96 blocks of a player tries 2 places, each taking with chance 0.35 (my picks, to confirm), on grass or bare soil with no crown over it, at least 4 blocks from another tree's foot and 8 from a colony's middle, scattered, not round a parent; kinds umbrella 6, camel 3, baobab 0.25 (very rare); leaves already drop their seedling 1 time in 20 for a player (step 1's loot). **4d, most die young** (real: nearly every acacia seedling is browsed, burned or dries out): a daily chance to die, high at first and falling to nothing at grown, so about 9 in 10 seedlings never reach young and about half the young never reach grown (my picks, to confirm); a dead seedling vanishes, a dead young tree is taken away and leaves a fallen branch. Each tree rolls its own dice per day (`TreeLife.dice`). **4e, time away:** a tree whose land was not loaded lives every day it missed when its land loads (growing, maybe dying) and its shape is put in at once, waiting while a player can see its foot, middle or top; land away gets the seedlings of the rain days it missed, which have lived (most died) since. A new command, `/antcolonies trees`: how many trees are in the book and the nearest seedlings and young trees with their place and age. **4f, tests:** `TreeLifeTest` in `./gradlew test` (speeds for 2000 seeds; 20,000 seedlings: 7 to 13 in 100 reach young, 40 to 60 in 100 of those reach grown); new game test class `TreeGrowthTests` in `trees` (5 tests: a tree grows a step a day for 12 days, only adds log wood, leaves a player's glass block alone, and is forgotten when cut through; bone meal; seedlings after rain scattered and none after dry days or under a crown, and the seedlings of 30 rain days away; a seedling and a young tree dying, the young one leaving a branch; a tree 15 days away caught up at once, and a record saved and loaded). The game test arena has no ground in it, so these tests lay their own grass. `trees` 9 of 9 and `termitefood,away` 37 of 37 on 8 servers. Not tried in-game yet.

- **Giant trees, step 5a: old age and the dead stages day by day** (2026-10-19, branch `claude/tree-death-8ad727`; the user's decisions in `docs/DESIGN.md`, "Death and the fall"; sub-steps in `docs/plans/m7.5-giant-trees.md`). Every tree has its own lifespan from its kind's range by its seed (`lifespan` in `tree_kind/*.json`: umbrella thorn 150 to 250 days, camel thorn 300 to 500, baobab 1,000 to 2,000; the user's real ratios; `TreeLife.lifespan`), counted from the day it came up (`Forest.Tree.lived`, saved; trees of new land start with the days their age stands for). A grown tree reaching it dies of old age (`Forest.kill`; `died`, `cause` saved), and its dead stages follow by the days since death (`TreeLife.deadStages`, my picks inside the user's "real, squeezed"): acacias wither the day they die, stand as a bare silver snag from the next day for 2 to 4 days, termite mud 3 to 6 days, limbs broken 5 to 10 days, then the fallen log for good (11 to 21 days after death); baobabs slump the next day, a wet fibre heap 3 to 6 days, a dried heap 10 to 20, then gone (the record ends). Dead trees of new land get a death day inside the stage they stand in (`TreeLife.daysDeadIn`) and go on from there. Each change places only the blocks that differ, one stage at a time in sight, several only out of sight (`Forest.Tree.placedStage`). Wood never jumps: the snag's wood is where the living tree's was, the mud and shed trees' where the stage before had it (`TreeShapes.keepWithinLiving`; branch lines drawn as a part of the full line, `TreeDraw.thinLine`; broken limbs taper as the whole limb would, `TreeDraw.broken`). `/antcolonies trees` lists the dead trees near you (stage, days dead, cause). Checks: `TreeShapeTest.deadWoodNeverJumps`, `TreeLifeTest.lifespansByKind` and `deadStagesTakeTheDecidedDays`; game tests `aGrownTreeDiesOfOldAgeAndFalls` (an umbrella thorn through every stage: no leaves on the snag, mud, limbs down with fallen branches, the fallen log and its stump) and `aDeadBaobabSlumpsIntoAHeapAndIsGone` (with a dead tree of new land and saving and loading). `trees,termitefood,away` 46 of 48 on 8 servers: the stump check was too strict (an old stump is hollow, box 3, fixed), and the away termite gallery test failed side by side and passed alone (`docs/BUGS.md` 55). - **Giant trees, step 5b: withering and shedding** (2026-10-19, branch `claude/tree-death-8ad727`; the user's decisions "wither, then drop" and "real, squeezed"). A dead tree's slow changes now go in 8 steps through the game day, not only once a day (`TreeLife.STEPS`, `deadStep`; `Forest.time`, `Tree.step` and `placedStep` saved; a dead tree is looked at every second and changed only when its step moves on). **Withering:** new blocks Withered Umbrella Thorn, Camel Thorn and Baobab Leaves (`*_dead_leaves`, the same leaflets dried straw brown, `tools/tree_art.py`; burn easily, give themselves to shears or silk touch, now and then a stick); the day a tree dies each leaf turns brown at its own moment and drops 4 tenths of a day later, all gone within 9 tenths of the day, with bits of brown leaf drifting down (falling dust particles, not from every block); the next day the bare snag's bark goes silver. **Mud climbing:** the termites' mud climbs the snag from the ground up with a ragged front over the first 6 tenths of the mud stage. **Limbs breaking off:** one limb (with its branches) at a time, spread evenly over the first 8 tenths of the shedding stage in an order by the tree's dice; its fallen pieces land with it; anything alive under a breaking limb with nothing solid between is hit for 3 hearts (my pick; an insect dies; death message "was hit by a falling branch", damage type `falling_branch`). **The baobab's dried heap** shrinks from the top over its stage, to a low remnant, then is gone. How: `TreePlacer.placed(..., step)` blends one stage's shape into the next block by block (`blend`, `withering`; by height, or by limb), so only the blocks that change are placed, and branch arms never point into a break. Trees of new land in a dead stage are given a death day after its slow change is done (they show the finished shape). Checks: `TreeLifeTest.deadStepsGoForwardWithinTheirStage`; the old-age game test now walks the dead tree through in quarter days (at midday of its death day some leaves green, some brown, some dropped; none left by the end of the day; the mud count growing through the mud stage; the high wood falling at least 3 times while shedding; a chicken under a breaking limb hit) and the baobab test checks the dried heap shrinking. The mud check first caught step 0 of the mud stage drawing the finished mud (fixed: step 0 is the bare snag). Not tried in-game yet.
- **Giant trees, step 5c: the fall** (2026-10-19, branch `claude/tree-death-8ad727`; the user's decisions "real topple" and "crush all"). **When:** a shed acacia due to fall (its falling day from 5a) stands waiting for a storm: each second of rain over it a 1 in 60 chance to come down, 1 in 20 in a thunderstorm (my picks); after 3 days without one it falls on a still day (`TreeLife.RAIN_WAIT`, `Tree.fell`, saved). **Which way:** toward its heavier side, where most of the shed tree's wood (bare or under mud) hangs from 4 blocks up; a snag whose weight sits within half a block of its middle keeps the way its dice gave (`DeadWood.heavierSide`; the fallen shape is built that way). **In sight** (`tree/TreeFall`): 2 seconds of creaking (low groans and wood cracking, bark bits dropping from the hinge), a loud crack, then the standing wood (everything from 2 blocks up that is not part of the stump) is taken away and drawn by block displays, only the outer blocks (at most 2,500), swinging over on the hinge of the stump's edge on the falling side, slowly at first and fast at the end like a falling pole, over 3 seconds, past level until its top meets the ground (poses every 3 ticks, gliding between); then the crash: the displays go, the hollow log is placed, a boom, dust along the log and bark bits. **Crush:** anything alive the swinging wood passes through, or that is where the log comes to lie, is hit once for 15 hearts before armour (my pick; damage type `falling_tree`, "was crushed by a falling tree"; an insect dies); soft blocks it passes through or lands on are smashed and drop (leaves, plants, glass, anything easier to break than stone), the ground (what a shovel digs), stone, wood and water stop it (the log has a gap there). **Out of sight or unloaded:** the log is placed at once, still crushing and smashing where it lies. A stop in the middle of a fall: the tree (saved as toppling) is placed fallen at once when its land loads, and the leftover displays (tagged) are thrown away as they load (`ModEvents.onJoinLevel`). Fallen, it stays fallen. Needed an access transformer (`META-INF/accesstransformer.cfg`, switched on in `build.gradle`) to move the displays from the code. **For testing:** `/antcolonies trees kill` (the nearest grown tree within 32 blocks dies now: watch it wither) and `/antcolonies trees fell` (the nearest grown acacia within 32 blocks, living or dead, is put into its shed shape at once and falls in sight). Checks: new game test `aFallingTreeCrushesWhatIsUnderIt` (a chicken on the log's line killed, a glass block in its way smashed, a stone block kept, the standing wood gone and over 50 displays while it falls, none left after, the log placed, no second fall, stays fallen); the old-age test now expects the 3-day wait for a storm and checks the fall toward the heavier side when the tree leans; new `TreeFallTest` (72 acacias: the log lies within 45 degrees of the heavier side). Its first runs caught the heavier side counted without the mud-cased wood, and the glass under the swing missed (now what lies where the log lands is smashed too). Not tried in-game yet.

A giant's wood (living, bleached, rotten, charred) and green leaves no longer burn away (`TreeBlocks.setFlammable`, burn chance 0; they still feed a fire next to them); withered leaves, thorny twigs, pods and frass burn as before. **The moment a fire appears** next to a giant tree's block (`tree/TreeFire`, on Minecraft's block-change event): bark chars black, green leaves scorch brown (withered). A fire in thin air goes out at once (Minecraft's rule), so a grass fire scorches only the leaves just over it and the bark at the foot, as a real savanna fire does. **Which tree dies** (`Forest.inFire`): a young tree (age under 0.45) the fire touches dies at once (taken away, a fallen branch left); a grown living tree whose trunk is in the fire (its lowest 4 blocks, within 4 of its middle) rolls once a day: 1 in 10 just grown up to 6 in 10 ancient (`TreeLife.fireDeathChance`, my picks; real: thick bark keeps a sound acacia alive with a black foot, an old hollow trunk burns out like a chimney). **Killed by fire:** its dead stages are black instead of silver (the snag, the mud-climbed snag, the shed snag and the fallen log all charred, `TreePlacer.charred`), and an old hollow one (half way to ancient or more) smoulders: smoke out of its top for half a day (my pick). **Scars stay:** a charred block of a tree that lives on, or of a dead one, stays charred as its shape changes (`TreePlacer.same`: a charred or scorched block is still the tree's own). Check: new game test `fireCharsBarkKillsYoungTreesAndSomeGrownOnes` (bark charred and still there 5 seconds later, a second fire the same day does not roll again, the dice spare one grown tree and kill an ancient one, which smoulders and stands as a black snag with no silver wood, a young tree in the fire gone, a leaf over a fire scorched). The dead-tree scene now waits until the tunnels are all grown and puts the trunk 10 to 15 blocks from the nearest hole (no runway possible); in the run the way went out past the limit as meant. `trees,termitefood,away` 56 of 56 on 4 servers (8.5 minutes). **Confirmed in-game by the user 2026-10-19:** the termites now dig a tunnel to the dead tree (but stopped at its end and idled until night instead of eating it from below: the next fix). Other things checked, no change needed: mud casts count as ground for a trunk's foot (so the next block up is found once the foot is eaten), wood is never sensed where the colony was attacked or yields to a neighbour; small things left as they are: the silver branches are not counted in a dead tree's size, wood more than 24 blocks above or below the nest is sensed but never tunnelled to (as with any rich food), and nothing is sensed while away (it is within a minute of coming back).
- **Fix: they dug to the dead tree and idled at the tunnel's end till night** (the user's report 2026-10-19, with a picture): read from the user's own game log and saved world. By day a termite may only walk to a spot that counts as inside the nest, and a feeding gallery cell counted as inside only with something solid right over it (the food, a mud cast). Under the dead tree the gallery stepped one block down from the tunnel, so the lower step had the upper step's open space over it, looked open to the sky, and by day no digger would go down to it; they all asked for the same block, found "no way", and stood in the tunnel until night let them out (`Nest.isInside`: now a gallery cell with another inside cell over it is inside too, worked out from the top down). A second bug the test found: a dead giant's roots run on under the grass where the ground is higher than its foot, and the wood call could pick a buried root as the place to dig to; the colony only lists food it can see from above, so no tunnel was ever planned (`DeadWoodCall.isUncovered`: the foot is now wood lying open, only more wood, air, plants or mud plates over it). The dead-tree scene now uses a real dead giant (a mud-climbed umbrella thorn placed by the tree placer) standing in a dip a block deep, as the user's did, checks every dug gallery cell under soil counts as inside, and waits until the tree is bitten from below by day; with either fix taken out it fails (checked); `docs/BUGS.md` 60. `trees,termitefood,away` 56 of 56 on 4 servers (8 minutes). Not tried in-game yet.
- **Fix: they ate the dead tree's foot and stopped there** (the user's ask 2026-10-19, "after they break into the tree block after block and continue"): the dead tree test now asks for the trunk eaten from inside 3 blocks over its foot. It showed the workers eating the first blocks and then stopping: by day a termite only goes for wood the colony knows of, and the wood over a block eaten into a mud cast was known to nobody (no worker sees it from outside by day). **Fix:** a block eaten through teaches the colony the wood touching it (`FoodStore.eatAway`), a worker inside feels the wood within its reach (the 26 cells round it, `FoodMap.tick`), and the plant list takes in wood over a mud cast or the colony's own gallery (`FoodStore.scanUnderCrown`). With the first part switched off the test fails (the roots eaten, 8 of 71 reachable blocks known). The test hurries each bitten block to its last piece (a test colony brings home about one piece every 5 seconds, 32 to a block); the last bite and finding the next block are the workers' own. In play the trunk is eaten slowly, block after block, as real termites take months over a big log. `TermiteFoodTests.aStandingDeadTreeIsSensedAndGetsAGalleryToItsFoot`. **Confirmed in-game by the user 2026-10-19 with the rest of step 6** ("everything works perfectly"); merged into `master` with the user's OK.

- **The errand helper, step 1: one shared "walk to work"** (2026-10-19, branch `errand-helper` from `master`; plan `docs/plans/errand-helper.md`; from the review of `AntNavigator` the user asked for: the legs are fine, what makes a new job slow to add is that every goal writes its own walking code: 28 hand-written "close enough" checks in 19 goals, 23 copies of "no route: give up", 6 differently written "leave it alone for a while" memories, 5 copies of "no way to this spot: pick another, up to 4 times"). New `entity/goal/Errand` (mind layer, it only tells the legs "go to this cell"): `within` (the shared reach rule, under 1.5 blocks up or down), `goTo` (walk on, set off again once a walk is over, with no way there the thing is left alone for 400 ticks and the goal gives up), `setOffTo` (another spot when there is no way, giving up after 4), `leftAlone` for the goal's search for work. `GatherSeedGoal`, `CarryCorpseGoal` and `DigGoal` moved onto it (91 lines out, 48 in); the same rules as before, nothing should change in the game. Full run 2026-10-19 on 8 servers: 139 of 141 in 12 minutes; the two failures, both neighbour tests (`neighbouringTermitesWallOffTheirGalleries`, `oldColoniesYieldToNeighboursAndYoungOnesPushOn`), passed 3 of 3 alone (box 4, the open leak of `docs/BUGS.md` 55; neither goes near seeds, bodies or digging). Nothing to see in-game.
- **The errand helper, step 2: every goal that walks to work on it** (2026-10-19, branch `errand-helper`, the user's OK). 21 more goals moved onto `Errand`: carrying eggs, pellets and seeds, eating, feeding the larvae and the queen, milling bread, clipping grass, the night soil, plugging a foraging hole, sealing a leak and the entrance, the hill repair, shaping the mound, the queen to her room, the king by the queen, the soldiers' posts, the nurses by the queen, the patrol, going inside, the founding queen's walk to a level spot. The helper grew a little: `setOff` (now: to a cell, with the mind's own step costs, or to any foothold a rule accepts), `walkOn` (keep walking, set off again once a walk is over), `isIn`, `isWalking`, `within` with a height rule of its own (the pellet onto a hill from a step lower or higher), `setOffFor` (set off at once unless left alone), and its own leave-alone time (the soldiers' minute, their old memory now in the helper). **New "no way: leave it alone for 20 s" memories** (the lesson of `docs/BUGS.md` 42) in the 8 goals that had none: the night soil piles, the hill repair, plugging a hole, sealing a leak, sealing the entrance, shaping the mound, the queen to her room, the king to the queen; the one change in the game: an insect cut off from that work searches for a route at most once in 20 seconds instead of about every second (my pick: the same 20 s as the other goals). Left out on purpose: `BuildMoundGoal` (its colony-wide "unreachable" list and reach fallbacks are its own rules), `ScoutGoal` (step 4), the fights and chases (`DefendGoal`, `HuntTermitesGoal`, `NoticeGoal`: moving targets, for the M8 fight system), wandering, grooming, naps. Size: the 24 walking goals went from 3,095 lines of code before step 1 to 3,006, the helper is 123, so the whole is about the same size (the new memories cost lines); what shrank is each goal's walking code, now a line or two per walk, and a new job's. Full run 2026-10-20 on 8 servers: 141 of 141 in 11 minutes (the two neighbour tests that failed in step 1's run passed too). Nothing to see in-game but the 20 s memories.
- **The errand helper, step 3: one route search for several spots** (2026-10-20, branch `errand-helper`, the user's OK: "Start step 3 ill test it all together"). The route planner finds the nearest of several cells in one search (`AntPathFinder.find` with a collection of goal cells, searched toward the box round them, so the nearest is found; each cell reached on its floor as before); the legs walk to the nearest (`AntNavigator.moveTo` with a collection, `getGoal` says which; planning again keeps to that cell); the errand puts a load down at its spot, else at the nearest free spot it can reach (`Errand.setOffToNearest`; a spot that turns out no good is skipped for the rest of the errand, `notThere`). Before, a carrier tried up to 4 spots one after another, each a route search of its own, and when a chamber was cut off each was a whole failed search (up to 6,000 steps); now one. The colony's pickers got list versions (`Brood.eggSpots`, `FoodStore.foodSpots`, `Refuse.corpseSpots` and `destinations`), the random pickers built on them, so everything else picks as before. **Changes in the game (my picks, to confirm):** a digger walks to the nearest spot to stand at (before: the spot on top of the block first, then the sides in a fixed order); eggs and bodies are put down at the nearest free spot, so the nursery and the refuse heap fill from the chamber's door inward (before: a random free spot), termite bodies onto the nearest comb; **seeds keep their random spot round the store** (a heap grows to a whole block, and heaps filling from the door inward would stand in the way), with the nearest other spot when there is no way there; **pellets for the hill unchanged** (random free spots with claims: the nearest hill spot is where every ant leaving the hole passes, `docs/BUGS.md` 8); the queen's feeder still goes to her mouth first, then to her. Unit tests (`AntPathFinderTest`): the nearest of several spots, as short as the way to it alone; cut-off spots skipped, all cut off found out in one search. Speed (`ANTCOLONIES_TESTS=stresstests`, 3 runs of the old code and 3 of the new, every 5-second window of each stage averaged, not only its last): no clear change; the stress test swings a lot from run to run, in both versions a burst of failed route searches about once a second for a minute now and then (1,000 steps each, cause not found yet), which hides the small difference; the gain is in the cut-off case (one search instead of up to 4), which the stress colony rarely meets. Full run 2026-10-20 on 8 servers: 141 of 141 in 11 minutes.
- **The errand helper merged into `master`** (2026-10-20, the user's OK: steps 1 to 3, a fast-forward, `master` had nothing new). Full run on `master` on 8 servers: 140 of 141 in 15 minutes; the one failure, `foragersWalkALongGalleryOutAndBack` ("no tunnel was planned to the hay ... reach 24"), is the open long walk flake of `docs/BUGS.md` 51 (seen before the errand helper existed), 2 of 3 alone.
- **Fix: termites never dug to known food past a young web's range** (2026-10-20, on `master`, the user's ask: "look into the long walk flake"; `docs/BUGS.md` 51). With the galleries on and their range equal to the food radius (24), the plant scan skipped the scan along the galleries and round known food, so known rich food farther out was never seen and no tunnel planned to it; now that scan always runs with the galleries (`FoodStore.plants`). The long gallery scene puts its hay down once the arms are planned (the order that failed), `Galleries.armsPlanned()` for it, and logs more on a failure. Failed every run without the fix; 36 of 37 with it (one timeout, no trip home through the tunnel, still open in 51); `termitefood,away,mound,soldiers,hills,trees` 79 of 79 on 8 servers. In the game: a young termite colony may now dig to far known food (hay, big dead wood) sooner. Not tried in-game yet.
- **Project Apex, colony parts step 1: one parts list** (2026-10-21, branch `claude/colony-parts-e4f872` from `master`; plan `docs/plans/apex.md`, "colony parts", my picks, left to me by the user). `Colony`'s 33 parts were each written in by hand in 4 to 5 places (field, constructor, `tick()`, `save()`, `load()`), in lists of different orders. Now each part is one row in `Colony`'s constructor, a `ColonyPart` (new, `colony/ColonyPart`): its name (`nest`, `food_store`, `mound`, ...; for the species files of step 2), its tick and its tick while away each with a place in the order (gaps of 10, so a new part fits between two), how it saves and loads, or why it is not saved; `Colony` ticks, saves and loads the list in one loop each, and `AwayLife.tickAway` ticks the away list (`Colony.tickPartsAway`). A part that says neither how it is saved nor why not stops the colony being made, so a forgotten save line cannot slip in. The typed getters (`colony.mound()`, ...) stay (step 3). Every species still gets every part (step 2 picks). **Nothing changes in-game.** Kept on purpose, as they were (not fixed): (1) the tick order while loaded and while away are different orders (the hidden trips tick before the galleries while away, after them while loaded; the builders' numbers only while away), so each part has two places; (2) the nest, the hill and the food store tick only one piece of themselves from here (the galleries, the night soil, the bare patches), and the hill's night soil only for a species without a mound, as before; (3) 11 parts have no tick here (the queen care, the king care, the brood, the bakery, the refuse, the walls, the soldiers, the forager spots, the foraging trails, the worker food, the nest ledger): their insects' goals or other parts drive them, as before; (4) 6 parts are not saved, each marked so in its own file already: the nest walls, the refuse, the foraging holes (claims and short memories), the recruits (a scent line fades within minutes), the mushrooms (the weather at the last look, for the list), the soldiers (they take their posts again); (5) the parts now **load** in the order they are made instead of the old save order: no two parts share a save name (63 names checked), so the save order never mattered, and only one part's load reads another's, the nest ledger counting an older save's owed food into the food store and the bakery, which still load before it. **New check** (in the two `away` scenes that already save and load a living colony, `AwayTests.saveAndLoad`, harvester and termite; no new scene): a copy of the colony is loaded from its save, every part the scene left empty gets a test sample (`ColonyPart.sample`, `fillForTests` in 15 parts: a few of its saved numbers set, no blocks touched), then no saved part may be empty and each part saved, loaded and saved again must match; it names the part that differs (proved by breaking the door's load on purpose: the test failed naming `door`). Both scenes: all 27 saved parts. Full run 2026-10-21 on 8 servers: 140 of 141 in 11.5 minutes; the one missing test did not fail: shard 3's Java runtime crashed (an access violation in the garbage collector's own thread, `hs_err` file; not something pure Java code can cause) after its other 17 passed, and all 18 of that shard's tests passed on a rerun (3 minutes). Speed (`stresstests`, 3 runs of the old code and 3 of the new, alternating, every 5-second window of each stage averaged): the colony brain's own line 0.168 ms a tick before, 0.167 after (174 windows each), so no change, as expected; the whole mod swings from 1.2 to 3.5 ms a tick in both versions with the known bursts of failed route searches (`docs/plans/apex.md`, "Also found"), which hide anything smaller. Nothing to see in-game.
- **Project Apex, colony parts step 2: species pick their parts** (2026-10-21, same branch `claude/colony-parts-e4f872`, my picks, left to me by the user). New **parts registry** (`colony/ColonyParts`): every kind of part signed up by name, with its row from step 1 (ticks, save and load, test sample), in the order made and loaded; `Colony` makes its parts from it. A **shared** part is in every colony; any other only in a species whose file lists it under the new `"parts"` (`SpeciesProfile.parts`, with a `_parts_note` in each file). A name nothing signed up, a shared part listed, or a name listed twice **stops the species files loading** with a message naming the file, the part and the parts a species can list (`ColonyParts.checkSpecies`, from `SpeciesProfiles`). After the split (Apex part 2) the framework keeps the registry and the shared parts and each species mod signs up its own (`ColonyParts.register`). **The two lists, worked out from each part's own "do I apply?" check** (a part is listed only for a species it does something for today): shared (18): nest, nest walls, food store, food map, queen care, brood, refuse, foraging holes, foraging trails, danger map, neighbours, population, away life, nest ledger, nest life, field life, build life, worker food; the harvester ant adds 7: hill, bakery, trunk trails, patrol, forager spots, door, recruits; Macrotermes adds 8: king care, mound, closed holes, hidden trips, fungus garden, mushrooms, soldiers, alarm. Differences from the plan, on purpose: the **door** is not shared (the plan guessed it was): Gordon's door rule switches itself off without `food.door`, so only the harvester lists it; the **foraging holes** and the **foraging trails** are shared, not termite parts: the harvester's ways out (`ForagingHoles.waysOut`) include its entrance, and the trails remember the harvester's seed trips too; the **danger map** and **neighbours** are shared (both species use them); the **galleries** are no part of their own (they live inside the nest and switch themselves on with `nest.galleries`). **How far it goes in this step:** a part its species does not list is never ticked, saved or loaded; every colony still *makes* every part so the old getters (`colony.mound()`, ...; 91 mound calls) keep working until step 3 (ask before use). Every tick that stops for a species was checked to return at once for it today, before rolling the colony's dice, so **nothing changes in-game**. **Old saves:** each part keeps its save names; an older save holds every part, as every colony once had them all: a part the species no longer lists is skipped, and logged once per species and part (`[parts] a saved ... colony held data for the part ...`) only when the save held more than a fresh part would (left-over defaults are not news); a listed part missing from a save starts fresh, as before. **One stray write moved** (no change in-game): asking whether the king wants food (`KingCare.wantsFood`, from the nurses while away) set a meal clock even in a colony with no king, so every harvester colony saved a king's clock; it now leaves the clock alone without a king (a new king starts it, `joined`). **New checks:** in the two `away` save-and-load scenes (`AwayTests.partsAreTheSpecies`), each colony has exactly the shared parts and its species' list, and nothing was written into a part its species does not list (`Colony.unusedPartsWrittenTo`: saved differently from a fresh part); on the game test server every colony save also logs such a write once (`[parts] a ... colony wrote into the part ...`), so a full run finds one in any scene; a unit test checks both files' lists and that the built-in harvester numbers (used when its file is broken) have the same list. Note: a colony's parts are fixed when it is made or loaded: a changed `parts` list reaches existing colonies only after the world is loaded again (not on `/reload`). Proved on purpose: a misspelled "mund" in `macrotermes.json` stopped the test server loading with the message naming the file, the part and the parts a species can list. `away,colony,antnest,termitefood,soldiers,mound` 95 of 95 on 8 servers (15.5 minutes), no part written to that its species does not list in any of the 95 scenes; both `away` save-and-load scenes now have 21 saved parts each (27 in step 1: the other species' parts left out). The full run waits for step 3 (steps built in threes, the user's rule). Nothing to see in-game.
- **Project Apex, colony parts step 3: ask before use** (2026-10-22, same branch `claude/colony-parts-e4f872`, my picks, left to me by the user). A colony now **makes only** the 18 shared parts and the ones its species file lists: a harvester colony has no mound, fungus garden, soldiers or alarm at all, a termite colony no hill, bakery or trunk trails. Code that means something only for some species **asks first**: `colony.part(ColonyParts.MOUND)` gives the part, or nothing for a species that does not list it (never a do-nothing stand-in), and `colony.has(...)` says yes or no. The 15 old getters (`colony.mound()`, `garden()`, `mushrooms()`, `soldiers()`, `alarm()`, `kingCare()`, `closedHoles()`, `hiddenTrips()`, `hill()`, `bakery()`, `trunkTrails()`, `patrol()`, `spots()`, `door()`, `recruits()`) are gone; about 450 calls moved over, in five batches (termite goals, ant goals, the colony's parts, the commands and events, the tests). For each call the answer without the part is exactly what the part itself answered for a species without its numbers today (the door lets everyone go, the patrol keeps every way open, no trail is quicker, no soldiers, no alarm, ...), so **nothing changes in-game**. Termite-only and ant-only goals ask once when they start (`PatrolGoal`, `MillBreadGoal`, the mound and soldier goals). Small helpers so shared code reads plainly: `Colony.buildsMound()`, `Colony.moundParts(level)` (no mound: no pieces), `Brood.mealFor(rank)` and `Brood.soldierWanted(level)` (no soldiers: the larva meal, no soldier wanted), `ClosedHoles.isClosedIn(...)`, `ModEvents.alarmsNear(...)`; `/antcolonies list` leaves out the lines of parts a colony does not have, as it printed nothing for them before. **The parts' own "do I apply to this species?" checks:** only one was purely that, `FungusGarden.exists()` ("does this species keep a garden?"), now answered by the garden being there, so it is gone; every other check reads a number section of the species file (`numbers() == null`, the bakery's bread stock above 0) and also guards reading those numbers, so they stay. **Old saves:** save names unchanged; a part a species no longer lists is still skipped on loading, and logged once when the save held more than a fresh part would: to know such a part's save keys a throwaway one is made from the registry only while loading and never kept. `Colony.unusedPartsWrittenTo` and the save-time `[parts] ... wrote into the part ...` warning are gone (a part that is never made cannot be written into). **Check:** `AwayTests.partsAreTheSpecies` (both `away` save-and-load scenes) now also asks the colony for every kind of part and checks the answer is yes exactly for the shared and listed ones. No bug found. Tests: after the termite goals `mound,soldiers,termitefood` 42 of 42; after the ant goals `antnest,colony,away` 52 of 53 (the larva starving test, 0 bodies, passed in the full run); **full run 2026-10-22 on 8 servers: 139 of 141 in 18 minutes**, the two failures the long harvester seed tests (`workerscarryseedsandclipgrassintothefoodstore`: no nestmate free to call out from the grama patch; `queenlivesinthefoundingchamberuntilherownroomisdug`: seeds still outside the store), both passing alone on this code and on `master` (box 4, the old seed flakes, `docs/BUGS.md` 49; not chased here). Speed (`stresstests`, 3 runs of `master` and 3 of the new code, taking turns, every 5-second window of each stage averaged): the colony brain's own line 0.368 ms a tick before, 0.366 after (228 windows each), no change, as expected; the whole mod swings both ways from stage to stage (1.1 to 2.2 ms) with the known bursts of failed route searches. Nothing to see in-game.
- **Project Apex, colony parts step 4: colony news** (2026-10-22, branch `claude/colony-news-d4bc74` from `claude/colony-parts-e4f872` at step 3, my picks, left to me by the user; plan `docs/plans/apex.md`). A small shared way for a colony's parts to hear what happened without the one telling naming them: `colony/ColonyNews`, one per colony (`colony.news()`). Kinds today: **a member hurt** by a living thing (with whether it was killed), **a member died**, **food found**, **a neighbour met**. A part's row in the parts registry says what it hears and at which place (`ColonyPart.hears`, places in gaps of 10 like the tick places; two parts at one place is refused); the colony signs its parts up when it is made, so a part a species does not have hears nothing. The user's two rules: a listener is called only when its own kind of news comes, and news is only sent while someone listens (`tell` does nothing for a kind with no listener). After the split the framework holds it and its kinds; a species mod's parts sign up the same way and may make kinds of their own (`ColonyNews.Kind.of`). **Moved onto it, only the wiring there was** (nothing new happens in-game): an insect hurt (`AbstractAntEntity.hurt`) named the alarm, the patrol, the danger map and the forager spots itself; now it tells "a member hurt" and they hear it in the same order (alarm 10, patrol 20, danger map 30, forager spots 40; the "hurt near its own spot: give it up" rule moved into `ForagerSpots.memberHurt`). A dying worker ant (`WorkerAntEntity.die`) named the patrol, the forager spots and the recruits: now "a member died" (patrol 10, spots 20, recruits 30; only worker ants tell it, as only they called those parts before). A seed picked up (`GatherSeedGoal`) or a plant cut (`FoodStore.clippedGrass`) named the forager spots and the recruits: now "food found" (spots 10, recruits 20). A meeting with another colony, by insect or trail (`AbstractAntEntity.lookForNeighbours`) or gallery (`Galleries.galleriesMet`, both colonies told), called the neighbours part: now "a neighbour met". So a harvester colony hears a hurt with the patrol, the danger map and the spots, a termite colony with the alarm and the danger map; a termite colony has no listener for food found or a death, so those are not sent there at all. **Looked at and left, on purpose:** (1) "a hole opened or closed": no part tells another today (each asks `isOpen` / `isClosed` when it needs to), so there was no wiring to move; the kind is left for its first listener (M8), my pick, to confirm; (2) the closed holes notice a changed danger memory by its version number (a cache key on a question, not a message; turning it into news would need a "danger changed" kind for the fading too, and could shift when holes close by up to a second); (3) questions stay questions: `DangerMap.avoids` still asks the neighbours, the termite "closes its holes" rule and the harvester's trunk trails (a shared part naming a harvester part: worth a shared "may we forage here?" vote before the split, a follow-up); (4) a hostile seen (`DangerMap.seen`) has one listener and is the danger map's own sense, left; (5) a recruit sent to a patch (`Recruits` telling the forager spots its new spot) is an order inside the harvester's own parts, not news. **Checks:** a unit test (`ColonyNewsTest`: own kind only, place order, nothing sent with no listener, a species mod's own kind, two at one place refused) and in both `away` save-and-load scenes (`AwayTests.partsAreTheSpecies`) each kind's listeners are exactly the colony's parts that were called by hand, in that order. `./gradlew build` passes. `colony,away,termitefood,antnest,soldiers` on 8 servers: 86 of 88 in 11 minutes; the two failures, the full-store seed test (route failures as in `docs/BUGS.md` 49) and `hayNearAHoleGetsARunwayOfMudAndIsEatenUnderItByDay` (the runway closed, 0 open sides, but not by day within the test's time; termite colonies hear exactly the same calls in the same order as before), both passed 3 of 3 alone (box 4, not chased here). The full run and the speed check for steps 1 to 4 come in step 5. Nothing to see in-game.

Full run on 8 servers: **141 of 141**, 10 minutes, no failures (step 3's two seed flakes, `docs/BUGS.md` 49, and step 4's runway failure both passed this time). Speed: the stress test (`ANTCOLONIES_TESTS=stresstests`), 3 runs of `master` and 3 of the branch taking turns, every 5-second window of each stage averaged, not only the last (153 windows a side): colony brain 0.170 ms a tick before, 0.163 after; whole mod 1.72 before, 1.96 after, the whole gap in route searches (0.31 before, 0.48 after), the bursts of failed searches about once a second already noted 2026-10-20 (`docs/plans/apex.md`, "Also found"), which hit some runs and miss others (one run of each version had none; one new run had 2.6 ms of searches in a stage); without them 1.41 before, 1.48 after, within the swing between runs. No speed change to see, as expected. Nothing changes in-game. **Confirmed in-game by the user 2026-10-22** ("everything is working"), with the alarm test from a player breaking the nest and the mound (`6f0648c`); **merged into `master` the same day with the user's OK** (a fast-forward, `master` had nothing new).
- **Project Apex, colony parts steps 6 and 7: colony questions** (2026-10-22, branch `claude/apex-questions` from `master`; plan agreed with the user, `docs/plans/apex.md`). Yes/no questions a colony asks of its parts without naming them, the way the news tells them things: `colony/ColonyQuestions` (`colony.questions()`); a part's row in `ColonyParts` says what it answers and at which place (`ColonyPart.answers`, gaps of 10, two at one place refused); a question is yes when any part says yes, asked in place order, the first yes ends it; nobody answering is no. **Step 6:** "keep off?" (`KEEP_OFF`, `colony.keepsOff`, in place of `DangerMap.avoids`, 10 callers moved) answered by the neighbours (10, a neighbour yielded to), the closed holes (20, where members were attacked: the termites' rule moved out of the danger map, `ClosedHoles.keepsOff`), the danger map (30, a dangerous place, `keep_away`, `DangerMap.keepsOff`) and the trunk trails (40, the trail out there passes danger, `TrunkTrails.keepsOff`), the order the danger map asked them in; "not cleared yet today?" (`NOT_CLEARED`) answered by the patrol, so `Colony.mayForageAt` names no part; `colony.whoKeepsOff` names the part that said yes, for test messages. **Step 7, the hole questions** (changed from "the hole news" with the user: news stays in one colony and had no listener even in M8, while the split was blocked by the shared foraging holes and galleries asking the termite `ClosedHoles` by name in 12 lines): "is this hole kept shut?" (`HOLE_KEPT_SHUT`, `colony.isHoleKeptShut`, in place of `ClosedHoles.isClosedIn`), "is any hole kept shut?" (the plug search's quick check) and "may no gallery grow through here?" (`NO_GALLERY_THROUGH`, `ClosedHoles.keepsGalleriesOff`), all answered by the closed holes. The hole news moves to M8. Same answers as before: **nothing changes in-game.** **Checks** in existing scenes: the trail danger scene (the danger map keeps off the east food; past it the danger map or the trunk trails), the termite killed by the bush (the closed holes), the neighbours (the neighbours), and in both `away` save-and-load scenes each question's answerers are exactly the colony's parts that used to be asked, in that order. Tests on 6 servers (the user's ask): `colony,antnest,away,termitefood,mound,soldiers,hills` 101 of 101 in 13 minutes; full run 139 of 141 in 16 minutes, the failures `aFallenTreeLiesOnTheSlopeUphill` (`docs/BUGS.md` 62) and `neighbouringTermitesWallOffTheirGalleries` (55), both 3 of 3 alone (box 4, old flakes). Speed (the user asked for a quick check after all): 3 stress runs of each, only the runs where every termite ticked compared (2 a side, see below): colony brain 0.40 ms a tick on `master`, 0.39 on the branch; the whole mod without route searches 1.90 before, 1.77 after; route searches swing 0.08 to 0.81 between runs (the known bursts): no change, as expected. In 1 stress run in 3, on both, most real termites stopped ticking a few seconds in (`docs/plans/apex.md`, "Also found 2026-10-22"); those runs were left out. Found while planning: Apex step 8, about 81 lines in 15 shared colony files still name a species part. Not tried in-game yet.
