- **Project Apex part 2, the split: the review's clean-up** (2026-10-23, branch `claude/mod-split`, after the user asked for a look over the split). The red harvester ant's built-in numbers (used when a species file is missing or broken) moved out of the framework into the desert mod (`desert/species/HarvesterBuiltIn`), signed up at start with the new `SpeciesProfiles.signUpBuiltIn`; a species without a good file gets its own built-in numbers, else the first signed up (today the harvester's, as before), and with none at all the game stops with a message instead of pointing at blocks of a mod that may not be there. The larva's meal before its colony tells it (`AntLarvaEntity.MEAL_UNTIL_TOLD`, 5 as before) and a colonyless worker's hunger (none, as before) no longer read the harvester's numbers. `DigGoal`'s comment no longer links to a desert file; `DesertSpecies` names its own data folder. The framework now names the harvester ant nowhere in its code. Nothing changes in-game. `./gradlew build` with every unit test; `colony,away,antnest` 53 of 53 on 6 servers (9 minutes). Not tried in-game yet.
- **Fix: termites feel a jump the moment it happens** (2026-10-22, branch `claude/termite-notice-flake-dadc24`, the user's pick A, real behaviour; `docs/BUGS.md` 66). Found by `termitesFeelNoSneakingStepsButFreezeAtAJump` failing once in the round 2 full run. A termite listened for jumps only once a second and measured the jump's distance when it listened, so one strolling away from a jump 5 blocks off could be past its 6-block shake range by then and never froze; and every freeze came up to a second late. Now a player's jump or landing reaches every insect within its profile's shake range at that moment (`GroundShakes.hard`, which finds them; `AbstractAntEntity.feelShake`), and `NoticeGoal` freezes it on its next step, without waiting for its once-a-second look (a jump felt while it may not stop, climbing or carrying, still counts for a second, and it gets one chance per jump). Walking and sprinting players are judged as before. In-game: termites freeze within a tenth of a second of a jump instead of up to a second later, and never miss one that was in range. The test now has the termite walking away from the player as the player jumps, and fails if the freeze comes more than 3 ticks after the jump. Checked: with the old code and the walk-away, 5 of 31 copies failed with the full run's message; with the fix 62 of 62 copies passed, every one frozen 1 tick after the jump; `body` 4 of 4 on 6 servers; `./gradlew build` passes. Not tried in-game yet.
- **Fix (tests only): a test's ground stopped running when a neighbouring test finished** (2026-10-23, branch `claude/test-ground-tickets` from `claude/mod-split`; `docs/BUGS.md` 67). Two neighbouring tests share some of the chunks round their arenas, and Minecraft does not count loading tickets, so the first test to finish switched the shared chunks off under the other. `TestIsolation` now counts the tests holding each chunk (`GROUND_HOLDERS`) and lets it go with the last. Proved by a log: 318 shared running chunks stopped before, 0 after. `termitefood,away,trees` 55 of 56 on 6 servers (the long walk timeout, `docs/BUGS.md` 51, 3 of 3 alone). Nothing changes in-game.
- **Notes merge cleanly** (2026-10-23, branch `claude/notes-merge-ec989f`; the user's ask: the shared notes clashed on almost every merge, and two fixes both took bugs 63 and 64, renumbered to 66 and 67 by hand). Three pieces, notes and tools only, nothing changes in-game: (1) `.gitattributes` gives `docs/HISTORY.md`, `docs/BUGS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` git's `union` merge, which keeps both sides' added lines instead of stopping with a conflict; (2) `tools/bug_number.py` hands out a new bug's number: it reads `docs/BUGS.md` on every branch here and on GitHub and takes the next number as a git name `refs/bug-numbers/<n>`, which git makes only if nobody has it (shared by every working copy on the PC) and which is pushed to GitHub; `check` lists doubled numbers; (3) `./gradlew build` runs `checkBugNumbers`, failing on a number used twice. The rule for every session is in `CLAUDE.md`, "Notes on branches". **Proof:** two throwaway branches from the same commit each took a number (68 and 69) and added a change-log bullet, a bug and a status line at the same spots; merged with no conflict, both lines kept in each file, no number twice; the same merge without the new rule (in a throwaway copy) clashed in all three files; a third branch that picked 68 by hand merged too and the build stopped on it ("used twice: 68"). The throwaway branches were deleted and 68 and 69 given back. My picks: union for `CLAUDE.md` too (its status clashed as well; the price is that a line both sides edit is kept twice, so the rule says add, don't rewrite, and look the merge over); numbers stay plain 68, 69, ... rather than dates or branch names.
- **Fix: one chicken attack left a foraging hole for good** (2026-10-23, branch `claude/alarm-stuck-65-c2133c`; `docs/BUGS.md` 65, the user's in-game test of the split: "the colony stayed on alarm, even after I killed the chicken"). The alarm itself calmed as designed; the danger memory counted every peck as an attack (a worker pecked to death and one pecked once: strength 10, over the 6 for "again and again"), so the hole by the dead tree was left for good and the tree kept off for over 3 days. Now each member counts once an attack at its worst wound (`DangerMap.hurt` with the member, news from `ColonyParts.DANGER_MAP`), and a place is left for good only after 2 separate attacks (`DangerMap.Place.attacks`, saved; hurts and deaths under `seen_every_ticks` apart are one attack; older saves count 1). My picks, to confirm (`docs/DESIGN.md`, "4g fix"). The holes test (`holesTakeTurnsNearFoodFirstAndMoreInTheRain`) now has the user's chicken: failed on the old code, passes; it lets a night pass before the faded hole's turn, as the night's holes are picked when the alarm settles. Not tried in-game yet.
- **Project Apex part 3, T1: each species' numbers read by its own mod** (2026-10-23, branch `claude/apex-termites`; plan `docs/plans/apex-termites.md`). The framework's `SpeciesProfile` read every species' number groups, so a new species' numbers (the Matabele raid size, scouts) would have meant a framework edit. Now the framework reads only the shared groups (colony, workers, queen, brood, food, refuse, nest, founding, laying, body language, the parts list) and a species mod signs up its own sections of the same file by their path (`species/SpeciesSection`, `SpeciesSection.signUp("mound", codec)`, read by `SpeciesProfile.read`, asked with `profile.section(...)`): the savanna's queen body, mound, garden, soldiers, alarm, galleries and grass regrowth in `savanna/.../species/SavannaNumbers`, the desert's door, patrol, trunk trails and night soil in `desert/.../species/DesertNumbers`. The species files keep their exact layout; a broken section still stops the file loading. The framework's nest layout reads the garden's room count through a small shared view (`SpeciesProfile.GardenRooms`); the soldiers' meal times and the mound's chimney trips reach shared code through two new hook questions (`ColonyHooks.Castes.mealIntervalTicks`, `ColonyHooks.Structure.mouthTripChance`), answered in `SavannaParts`. Checked: every number read before and after compared (263 of 263 for Macrotermes, 199 of 199 for the harvester ant; the only differences: the termites no longer carry the harvester's unused night soil defaults, and the garden's rooms are read twice), new unit tests (`SpeciesSectionTest`, each mod reading its own sections, a broken mound section breaking the file), build and 174 unit tests pass. Nothing changes in-game.
- **Project Apex part 3, T2: the galleries in four parts** (2026-10-23, branch `claude/apex-termites`; plan `docs/plans/apex-termites.md`). `Galleries` (2,236 lines, the termites' whole underground web) is now a small hub (`Galleries`, 455 lines: the switch, the numbers, the tick in today's order, the colony list line, save and load, and every `Underground` answer the framework asks) and four parts by job: `GalleryWeb` (the arms, tips, forks, growth toward food and dead wood, neighbours met, the pieces out to food), `HoleTurns` (the holes along the galleries and which open each night), `GalleryUpkeep` (unused branches filled in, galleries walled off from a neighbour, the holes last seen dug) and `GalleryCatchUp` (the catch-up after coming back). Every method moved word for word; only what one part reads of another now names that part. The save keeps one `galleries` entry with the same names, so a world saved before loads the same. The rest of T2 (the feeding galleries, the mud casts, the mound's away building, the comb's line in the food store) was already behind sockets since the split (S2, S3); a search of the framework's code lines for termite-only words found only two names, renamed (`Brood`'s `onComb` to `onBed`, `WorkerFood.oldCombKnown` to `storeHasMeal`). Kept as shared words, on purpose: the king (the royal-pair founding every termite uses), mound (ant hills are mounds too) and sheeting (some ants, Crematogaster and Azteca, build covered runways too). Nothing changes in-game. Build and unit tests pass, the gallery proof over its made-up landscapes too. T1's `termitefood,away,soldiers,mound,antnest,colony`: 93 of 95 on 6 servers, the two old flakes (BUGS 55, the neighbours, and 22, the middle hole). T2's `termitefood,away,hills`: 45 of 45 on 6 servers (8 minutes). Round 1 full run (T1 + T2): 142 of 143 on 6 servers, 15 minutes, the one failure the old neighbours flake (BUGS 55, T5's work).
- **Apex part 3, T5: the neighbours flake (`docs/BUGS.md` 55) fixed** (2026-10-23, branch `claude/apex-termites`). A young termite colony that gave way to an older neighbour sometimes left its tunnel open: the place it remembered meeting them was a few blocks past its tunnel's end, beyond the yield check's slack. Now a piece is sealed when the colony gives way at its own end of it or where the neighbour's tunnel lies (`GalleryUpkeep.wallOffNeighbours`), the agreed design. The test also passes the other real end, the old colony sealing off first so the young one never needs to give way (box 3). New test switch: `ANTCOLONIES_EVERY_SHARD=1 tools/run_game_tests.sh n` runs the chosen tests on every server, to repeat a flaky one n times at once (`TestFilter.keepShard`). The test 6 of 6 after.
- **Apex part 3, T5: the fallen tree up a bowl flake (`docs/BUGS.md` 62) looked into** (2026-10-23, branch `claude/apex-termites`). The bowl's ground under three fixed columns goes missing beside other tests, never alone (12 of 12); the culprit not found in reruns, so the test now logs, the moment that ground goes, the tick and every test near it (`[bowl-ground]`, `TestIsolation.describeTestsNear`). Test only, nothing in-game.
- **Project Apex part 3, T3: one "what I carry" slot in the shared body** (2026-10-23, branch `claude/apex-t3-t4-2ba1d3`, plan `docs/plans/apex-termites.md`). Every carried load is now one row in a list (`entity/load/LoadKind`: a name, how it is saved, read back and dropped on death); the framework signs up its own five (`entity/load/FrameworkLoads`: soil pellet, seed or other item, food in the crop, egg, dead nestmate), a species mod adds its own with `LoadKind.signUp`, no framework edit (the Matabele ants' dead termites, a hurt nestmate). The body keeps one synced slot (`entity/load/CarriedLoad`, with its own data serializer, `registry/ModDataSerializers`) and one save through the rows, today's save words kept (`CarriedBlock`, `CarriedSeed`, `FoodInCrop`, `CarryingEgg`, `CarryingCorpse`), so a load carried in an old world loads. The renderer (`PelletLayer`, now `client/CarriedLoadLayer`) draws each row's look (`client/LoadLooks`, same sizes and turns as before); the old getters and setters stay as small wrappers, and a setter only lets go of its own kind (a log line `[load]` if a load ever replaced a different one). New check in `whatAnAntCarriesIsKeptAfterAReload`: food in the crop survives a reload, and a hand-written old save with only a seed loads as a seed. Nothing changes in-game.
- **Project Apex part 3, T4: the route search bursts** (2026-10-23, same branch). Found with logging in the termite stress test: most bursts came from `ShapeMoundGoal`. The mound plan can hold a tunnel cell at the mound's foot with only outside air beside it, which no termite can carve from inside; every builder was handed it in turn, searched the whole nest for a place to work from (two searches each, with and without habits), failed, and left it alone 20 s, so the burst came back every 20 s (one run: 307 of 423 failed walks, about 50 termites on the same spot). Fixed without any change in behaviour: the mind now tells the legs how close its work places lie (`AntNavigator.moveTo` with a reach, `Errand.setOff`, `MoundReach.REACH` = 1), and when no foothold that close passes, the walk fails at once exactly as the search would have, without the search. A second, rarer source found and **not fixed** (it would change what is seen, the user's call, in `docs/plans/apex-termites.md`): a nurse with no way to the queen (`StandByQueenGoal`) tries again every 2 seconds, four failed full searches of about 20 ms a try; seen in 1 stress run of 4, three insects in a spot cut off from the queen. Speed, termite stress test 6 runs a side, alternating (before = T3 only): failed searches 4,238 before, 2,626 after (750 against 451 a run); the 60-termite building stage's failed searches gone in some runs, not in others; route search time 0.37 ms a tick before, 0.51 after, and our mod 2.18 against 2.30 ms, the same within the noise (route search time runs from 0.10 to 1.27 ms per run on either side, set by whether a run gets the nurse bursts). Nothing changes in-game.

- **M8 step 1, the Matabele ant climbing, drafts (2026-10-23, a session started from the main session; branch `claude/matabele-climbing-d8cbe5`; nothing in the game):** the draft body plans now hold the Matabele's climbing numbers and spine (`python tools/body_plan.py measure`: the major's front feet 11.29 units ahead of its middle, hind feet 7.21, head end 13.04, gaster end 10.75, tightest curve 6.42; the minor's alike), so the game's climbing code would bend it like any other body. The Matabele cannot be filmed in the game yet, so a new tool does the game's own climbing maths in Python, number for number (`tools/matabele_climb.py`: `EdgeOver` over edges and round corners, `Spine` "follow the leader", the renderer's lift and push off the wall): a course up a 3-block pillar, over the top edge, along the top, head first down the far side and round the corner at its foot, at 1 body length a second on the slow walk, with every part checked against the blocks. What it found, and the versions to pick from in the pose viewer: **the gaster** (where the spine piece is judged to lie): A as measured (from the gaster's pinch) cuts the top edge's corner, the major up to 0.12 blocks; **B from where the gaster really begins** (behind the waist node, 1.61; my pick, now in both plans as a hand-set number, the new `hand_set` list in `body_plan.py`, kept by `measure` until approved) grazes 0.04, the approved harvester worker's own level on the same course; C from the waist itself is stiffest. **The antennae** walking up to a wall: the walk's, low and far ahead over the trail, went 0.09 blocks into the wall; **a new head clip `climb_feel`** (my pick): raised in a V, tapping the wall ahead left then right, played by the head from half a body length before the wall until the body stands half up it. **The middle** (the user's ask, "it's pretty rigid, make the middle also animated and curve"): a real ant's thorax is one fused piece carrying all six legs (here the forebody, which also holds the legs, so it cannot bend without pulling the feet off the wall); it bends at the neck and the waist, so: stiff (as before), **neck** (the big head nods up to 30 degrees to follow the surface under it, down onto the top as it crests, down the far side, up at a wall's foot; in the game it would read the ground ahead as the spine reads the ground behind, a new socket) and **neck and waist** (my pick; the spine's bend moved forward onto the petiole, shares 0.45, 0.33, 0.22 in place of 0.4, 0.35, 0.25: a Megaponera gaster is one hard capsule; more would cut the top edge). Not added: a heavy head tipping forward on walls, as the jaws already hang about 0.6 units off the surface and more would put them in the wall. The box sizes (0.6 x 0.45 major, 0.3 x 0.22 minor) are the preview's picks only; the user picks the sizes. **To look:** `python tools/ant_pose_viewer.py matabele`, open `references/matabele_pose_viewer.html`, click "Major: climbing" or "Minor: climbing" (behind the row, each on its own pillar) and pick the versions with the buttons under the scene. Checks: `python tools/matabele_climb.py` (about 1 minute; fails if the picked ways put a part in a block: nothing does), anim lab 0 problems on both models, `body_plan.py check` all OK, the worker's pose viewer page unchanged in use, `./gradlew build` passes.

- **M8 step 1, the Matabele walks at 3 blocks a second (2026-10-23, the user's ask: the fast walk made them look spidery; on `master`):** movement speed 0.35 to 0.273 for both (the lab's rule, about 11 blocks a second per point of attribute, an estimate the in-game camera can check); `WALK_SPEED` in `tools/anim_lab.py` 3.0. The game picks each walk by speed in body lengths a second and plays it at the stride its note names, so the feet stay planted by themselves; at 3 blocks a second the major (1.7 blocks long) is at 1.75 body lengths a second, the minor (0.78 long) at 3.9, so the walk band edges for the Matabele (its own `WALK_BANDS` in `tools/matabele_anims.py`) are lowered from 1.6 and 3.7 to 1.2 and 3.4: the major plays the walk and the minor the fast walk, both well inside their band (my pick). - **M8 step 1a, part 2: a dead mound wears down (2026-10-24, branch `claude/m8-dead-mound-wear-35a011`):** nobody patches a dead mound, so rain eats it in the real order, sped up. `colony/MoundWear` (pure numbers, unit test `MoundWearTest`): the mound is worked out column by column at death, how tall it stood, its body (the mound with everything narrower than about 5 blocks taken off, the spires) and the final hump; `colony/DeadMounds` does the blocks, ticked with the book (every 5 s, at most 256 blocks a look across every dead mound, my pick), by the world clock, only forward, catching up when the land loads; saved with the book. The order: spire tips, chimney mouths and ramps crumble between day 0.25 and day 3, the highest first; the spires wear down to the body by day 5; the body slumps (fast at first, slower as it flattens) into a low rounded hump by about day 30 (each column a little apart, the last between about day 26 and 35); the hump is 12 percent of the mound's height, at most 4 blocks (a full 32-block mound leaves 4); from about day 12 to 15 a ring 2 blocks wide of washed soil (one layer of old mound soil) settles round the foot on level bare ground; grass takes each column once it is hump, from day 6 on, a tuft on about 1 in 3; then it stays for good. Never touches a block a player put (the column under it stays as it is) or anything underground; the hollow shaft and tunnels bared by the wear are closed over with washed soil (the holes into the shaft and galleries are part 3). `Mound.isPieceBlock`, `isMoundBlock` made package-wide. **All timings and sizes my picks, to confirm.** Game test `aDeadMoundWearsDownToAGrassyHump` (category `mound`: day by day, the clock set back, a save and load, a player's cobblestone kept, the underground unchanged, no windows to the sky, settled by day 40); found and fixed while building it: a tunnel at the mound's foot under a crumbled ramp kept no grass, now it silts up and grasses over. `mound,termitefood,away` 49 of 49 on 6 servers (9.5 minutes); not tried in-game yet.
- **M8 step 1a, part 2 confirmed in-game by the user 2026-10-24** ("it works perfectly now", a worn hump in the rain with the dead colony's plugged gallery holes round it).
- **M8 step 1a, part 3: the way in, and the works round a dead mound weather (2026-10-24, branch `claude/m8-dead-mound-holes-09e831`):** the way in: the mound's hollow shaft is no longer closed as the mound wears (part 2 filled it), so the old chimney stays a hole from the top of the hump down to the entrance and the nest shaft; and a tunnel block at the top of the final hump falls open upward instead of turning to old soil, about 1 in 3, at most 3 to a mound (`MoundWear.opensUp`, `MOST_TUNNEL_WAYS`; my picks, to confirm), kept free of grass; every other bared tunnel side still shuts as before. The works (the user's yes of 2026-10-24): when a termite colony ends, its galleries part writes down what it had built out round the nest (`colony/DeadWorks`, saved in the dead mound book with its mound): every foraging hole, the gallery and tunnel cells, the feeding galleries and the food over them, the runways of sheeting (crumbling ones too); the nest's shaft, rooms and the mound are left out (they stay, the Matabele ants' home later). Worked by `DeadMounds` with the wear, the same rules: the world's clock, only forward, catch-up on load, inside the 256-block budget, only the termites' own blocks (mud crust, mud casts, a soil plug, an open hole or gallery), never a player's block, never burying an animal (it waits). The order, all my picks, to confirm (`MoundWear.surfaceMudDay`, `holeDay`, `galleryDay`): the thin mud on the surface first, day 0.5 to 3 (runway sheeting, mud plastered over the wood or hay it was eating and the casts left of it, gallery walls under the open sky); the holes silt up day 1 to 5 (a plug or an open hole turns to grass, an open hole under cover to soil, a plug under cover stays); the galleries under the ground last much longer and fill with the ground's own soil (`GalleryUpkeep.soilFor`) day 15 to 45, the far ends first and the cells by the nest last. `/antcolonies deadmounds` tells the ways in and the works left. Checks: `MoundWearTest` (3 new: the order and ranges, far galleries first, about 1 in 3 tops open), and the dead mound scene `aDeadMoundWearsDownToAGrassyHump` grew the works (a tunnel to an open hole and a feeding gallery under mud-plastered hay, a tunnel to a plugged hole with a runway of sheeting) and the way in (the shaft open to the sky on day 40, at most 3 tunnel tops open, no other tunnel open to the sky); its old "the shaft closed over, nothing under the ground changes" checks changed on purpose (box 2), and the hump now stands 3 or 4 tall (its very middle is the open chimney, the rim round it the top). In the scene the full 32-block mound always opened 3 tunnel tops. Found and fixed on the way (`docs/BUGS.md` 69): a spire piece under a player's block crumbled (now it stays), and the scene's "open to the sky" checks read the lagging sky light (now the height map; the game's mud check too). The dead mound scene 24 of 24 on repeat; `mound,termitefood,away` 49 of 49 on 6 servers (7 minutes). Framework: `FoodWays.crumblingRunwayCells`, `addNewTunnelForTests`. Not tried in-game yet.
- **M8 step 1b: living and dead termite mounds in new savannas (2026-10-24, branch `claude/m8-mounds-worldgen`, from the part 3 branch; not merged, not tried in-game yet):** the user's four decisions (`docs/DESIGN.md`, "World generation, details"). World generation picks the place and what stands there (`worldgen/TermiteMoundStructure`, a structure with no blocks of its own, `TermiteMoundPiece`; set `structure_set/termite_mounds.json`: one to a square of 5 by 5 chunks, at least 3 chunks apart, about 80 blocks between mounds, 60 to 100; never in water or on ground rising more than 4 blocks within 8). The server puts the colony in once the land 24 blocks round it is loaded, with the termites' own code (`colony/NewLandMounds`, saved as `antcolonies_new_land_mounds`; one a half second; skipped if another colony or dead mound is within 40 blocks or the ground is not soil). The numbers are in `worldgen/structure/termite_mound.json` (`numbers`). **Living** (2 in 3): the age mix 6 full : 3 half grown : 1 young; full mounds 85 to 100 percent of the full height, colonies 100 to 300 days old, 400 to 700 workers; half grown 40 to 65 percent, 40 to 80 days, 150 to 300 workers; young 8 to 20 percent, 10 to 25 days, 30 to 60 workers (my picks, to confirm: real mature colonies have a million or more; kept to what our termite food can feed, they grow on from there); workers of every age, the soldiers so many keep, the nest dug (through stone too, real savanna soil is metres deep), 2 or 3 layers of comb on the gardens' floors, the swollen queen and the king in the royal chamber, and its builders' time handed to the away catch-up, which grows the galleries out of sight. **Dead** (1 in 3, the user's pick): most stood full size; the stages 15 : 20 : 20 : 45 freshly dead (0.2 to 3 days, spires up, *Xylaria* on the comb), wearing (3 to 12), slumping (12 to 30), old grassy humps (35 to 200); a colony of that size is made and ended at once, so the part 1 dead mound book writes it down, then its death set back by those days and the part 2 wear brought up to the day (`DeadMounds.agedForNewLand`). **Trees on old humps** (the user's pick; half the humps, `hump_woods`): a thicket of 1 to 3 young umbrella or camel thorns (65 in 100), one grown thorn tree (27) or a young baobab (8), 2 to 6 blocks off the old chimney, into the tree book (my picks of the mix, to confirm). **Giant trees of new land keep 16 blocks clear of a mound's middle** (`TermiteMoundStructure.crowds`, worked out from the world's seed alone, in `GiantTreeStructure.plan`). Commands: `/antcolonies newmound full|half|young|dead [days]` puts one at your feet now; `/antcolonies newmounds` counts them. Tests: `TermiteMoundPickTest` (the spreads over 20,000 rolls, the middle fixed by the seed), game tests `TermiteNewLandTests` in `mound` (a full colony whole, queued one put in under 400 ms, a fresh dead mound standing, an old hump grassy with trees and a burrow). `mound,trees,termitefood,away` 69 of 70 on 6 servers (10 minutes); the one failure the long gallery walk's rare timeout (`docs/BUGS.md` 51), 3 of 3 alone.
- **M8 step 1b, the mound's shape grown in the background (2026-10-24):** found while timing it: a new colony's mound shape (all 32 stages grown by the termites' building rules, `MoundGrowth`) takes about 1.2 seconds, which would stop the game each time new savanna loads. Now a queued place's shape is grown on a background thread first (`MoundPlan.grownSoon`, a quarter of the PC's threads, 1 to 4; the colony's id picked beforehand, `ColonyManager.createColony` with an id), and it goes in in about 10 ms. Colonies loaded with a world start their shapes the same way while it loads, so coming back to a savanna with many mounds does not stop for a second per colony. The kept shapes went from the last 8 colonies to 24 (about 2 MB each). Nothing else changes.
- **M8 step 1b extras (my picks, to confirm; each a number or a switch in `worldgen/structure/termite_mound.json`):** (1) **greener grass on old humps** (`hump_grass`, true): short and tall grass over the hump. Real: mound soil is clay rich in the termites' leftovers and holds water, so mound islands stay green into the dry season. (2) **aardvark burrows** in 4 old humps in 10 (`aardvark_burrows`, 0.4): a tunnel 2 wide and 2 high from 10 blocks out down into the old shaft, coarse soil thrown out at its mouth. Real: aardvarks dig into termite mounds to eat; their old burrows become homes for warthogs, porcupines, hyenas and many smaller animals (and later a way in for the Matabele ants).

Her box 0.72 x 0.54, health 20, sting 3, speed 0.137 (about 1.5 blocks a second, half the workers': heavy with eggs), all my picks. **Her gaster curves as she climbs** (the user's ask): it is a chain of bones, one per plate (`gaster_back`, `gaster_mid`, `gaster_end`, `gaster_tip`, the 4th to the 7th abdominal segment; the sting on the last; `matabele_models.gaster_chain`), and her body plan has a waist piece (petiole 0.55, gaster joint 0.45, 45 degrees) then one spine piece per plate. A long gaster following the ground at an edge cut the corner one way or the other (on the climbing course the tip went 0.6 blocks into the pillar at the foot coming down, 0.3 into the top going over), so a piece may now rise and droop by different amounts: new optional `max_up` and `max_down` in a body plan's spine piece (`BodyPlans.Piece`, `Spine`; a plan without them bends `max_pitch` both ways, so every other body is unchanged). Hers: the first plate lifts up to 90 and droops 18, the others lift 60 and droop 12 (a real gaster lifts a long way at its soft joints; over a brink it sticks out in the air), worked out on the course, deepest 0.038 blocks (allowed 0.045). The major and minor models and clips are byte for byte the same. New `MatabeleQueenEntity`: starts a colony where she is put down, starves only once she has workers, the colony ends with her. `matabele_larva` and `matabele_corpse` bodies (textures by `tools/matabele_brood_textures.py`), not used yet. **Framework sockets (species-neutral; nothing changes for the termites or the harvester):** `SpeciesBodies` may give a caste a body picker (each new member's body by the dice, used by `Population.comeOut` and `Brood.growUp`) and a draw size for its young and dead (`AntLarvaRenderer`, `AntCorpseRenderer`); `AntRenderer` draws an insect at its own scale times the renderer's (1 for every other insect). **Command:** `/antcolonies matabele <workers>` makes a queen where you stand with that many workers, real ones round her up to 50, the rest numbers inside (they come out once there is a nest, 1d). **Tests:** new `MatabeleColonyTests` (category `colony`): a queen starts a colony, 4 majors and 4 minors join and are counted, each length in the spread, the nearer model, its drawn size and box, kept through a save; the mix over 4,000 rolls (half majors, a quarter minors, about 77 in 100 with the major's model); a dead worker leaves the count; the command's colony 60 workers, 50 real, 10 inside; nothing dug. The climb test now climbs the longest major (18 mm) and the shortest minor (5 mm). `colony,legs,away` 52 of 52 on 6 servers (3 minutes); guard films 15 of 15 unchanged. In-game change to note: a typical 10 mm minor is drawn a third bigger than the minor model was (real minors are about two thirds of a major's length).
- **M8 step 1d, the Matabele shallow nest (2026-10-24, branch `claude/m8-shallow-nest-e3f664`, from `claude/m8-matabele-colony-abe61a`; not tried in-game yet):** the user's four answers (`docs/DESIGN.md`, "Step 1d"). A Matabele colony settles in the best site within 32 blocks of its queen when it starts (`savanna/colony/MatabeleNest`): a dead termite mound (up the open chimney to the old nest's rooms, the old royal chamber the queen's at once, combed rooms taken once the Xylaria is gone), else under a fallen log (tag `antcolonies:matabele_nest_logs`: the dead trees' wood and any log; a room under the trunk and the queen's under it), else the ground (a shaft and two rooms, 3 and 5 down). The framework gains one neutral socket, a nest of ready spaces (`Nest.settle`: the species hands over its rooms, the workers dig only the blocks still in the way, saved as `nestSite` and `nestReadyRooms`; nothing changes for planned nests), and `WaitAtNestGoal` (an insect that should be in but has no way in yet waits round the entrance). The queen walks to her room (`QueenToRoomGoal`, `GoInsideGoal`) and digs nothing; workers dig (`DigGoal`) and stay inside (`forage_at` empty, my pick: real ones leave only to raid). `/antcolonies matabele <workers> [mound|log|ground]` picks the site (or fails if the kind named is not near); `/antcolonies list` says where the nest is and how many rooms are open. New tests `MatabeleNestTests` (category `colony`): a dead mound, a fallen log, open ground, the queen in her room in each; the 1c test's "nothing dug" check now checks the queen alone digs nothing and both colonies settled. Found while testing: the ground finder must not read the map's top block (a test arena's barrier roof, a cave roof), and a dead mound's open chimney sees the sky, so it is followed down regardless. `colony,legs,away,mound` 69 of 69 on 6 servers; build passes. Eggs still wait for the prey pile (1e: the queen lays only when fed).

Drawing scales 0.95 to 0.633 (major) and 1.14 to 0.76 (queen, still 1.2 times a major); boxes 0.6 x 0.45 to 0.4 x 0.3 and 0.72 x 0.54 to 0.48 x 0.36 (`SavannaClientEvents`, `SavannaEntities`, `tools/anim_lab.py`, `tools/matabele_climb.py`); the body plans' climbing numbers are in model units, so unchanged. - **M8 step 1, R1, the Matabele nest sites made real (the user's decisions 2026-10-24, branch `claude/m8-shallow-nest-e3f664`; not tried in-game yet):** from the real paper (Yusuf 2026, `CLAUDE.md` notes): the ants build nothing, they move into what is there. Under a fallen log: only a log 3 or more blocks across and twice as long as wide is a roof; a small 3 by 3 room is dug one block down under it (the log its ceiling; was two rooms 2 and 4 down under a 1-wide log), the entrance beside the log; a log has room for one colony to every 4 blocks of length, at most 2. Open ground is no real site: it is kept only as a stand-in for an animal's burrow (a shaft and one room 2 down, was two rooms 3 and 5 down) so a queen put down in bare land has a home (my pick, to confirm). Not built yet: two entrances to one nest (the shared nest has one entrance), guest animals, going back to an old nest, moving every 40 days (step 6), colony sizes 100 to 2,300 and a raid's share (steps 1f and 4). `MatabeleNestTests` 6 of 6 (a thick log, two colonies and no third, a thin log, a standing tree, a burrow, the mound), `colony,legs,away,mound` 72 of 72 on 6 servers.

- **M8 step 1d, the nest notes sorted out and two last points (the user, 2026-10-24, branch `claude/m8-shallow-nest-e3f664`; not tried in-game yet):** the user saw the plan and the code disagree on where the Matabele start and how much they dig. The code already followed R1; the old lines in `docs/DESIGN.md` and `docs/plans/m8-matabele.md` (rooms 2 to 5 down, digging inside a dead mound, the 10-block gap, any log 4 long, 400 to 2,500 ants, moving only when forced) are now marked "Replaced by R1" and plan step 1d says what is built. Two code changes, the user's picks: **the foot of any tree 3 or more across, dead or living, takes a colony** (real nests lie "under trees"; the colony list now says "at the foot of a tree"), and **a ground burrow may lie 3 blocks from another colony (was 10), its rooms never touching another nest's** (`MatabeleNest.TAKEN`, `touchesAnother`). - **M8 step 1d, the bare-ground burrow slopes in (the user, 2026-10-24, branch `claude/m8-shallow-nest-e3f664`; not tried in-game yet):** the user had seen the Matabele dig straight down like the harvester (the first 1d version's shaft and two rooms 3 and 5 down, gone since R1). The only straight shaft left, the bare-ground burrow's 2 blocks, is now a sloping animal burrow (`MatabeleNest.burrow`): the mouth, then one block down for each block forward, to one 3x3 room 3 blocks down (my pick: 2 blocks of soil over it). Checked: every dig a Matabele colony makes comes from its own site plan (`Nest.readyDigTarget`), nothing grows it deeper. Test: the burrow scene checks the room 3 down, the way at least 3 columns long and never more than 2 deep in one column. `colony` 22 of 22 on 6 servers.

- **The soil ramp planned, looks first (the user, 2026-10-24, branch `claude/m8-shallow-nest-e3f664`; nothing in the game yet):** a shared Colony Framework block that smooths a dug slope, carved from the step's own soil (`docs/plans/soil-ramp.md`; the user's picks: the same soil it is dug in, straight only for now, a player breaking one gets its plain soil). The burrow viewer (`python tools/burrow_viewer.py`, `references/burrow_viewer.html`) shows the Matabele burrow cut open in 7 soils with Minecraft's own textures, 45 degrees or gentler (one down per two forward), ramps against today's stairs, and a Matabele major for size; waiting for the user's pick of steepness.

- **The soil ramp BUILT at 45 degrees (the user's pick, 2026-10-24, branch `claude/m8-shallow-nest-e3f664`; not tried in-game yet):** a shared Colony Framework block (`block/SoilRampBlock`, `block/SoilRamps`): 8 steps of 2 pixels rising to a full block, one per soil (dirt, coarse dirt, rooted dirt, clay, mud, sand, red sand; grass carves into dirt), drawn with the soil's own texture, walked by the legs as a slope; a player breaking one gets what the soil gives (its loot table points at the soil's). Files written by `python tools/soil_ramp_blocks.py`. A nest plan may now name ramp cells (`Nest.settle(site, rooms, ramps)`, saved with the nest); a digger biting such a cell carves it into a ramp in place of taking it out (`DigGoal.bite`, and `BuildLife.dig` while away), and a ramp counts as dug out. The Matabele bare-ground burrow's floor is 3 ramps facing the mouth. Test: the burrow scene checks each column's floor is a ramp facing the entrance. Full run 156 of 156 on 6 servers (12.7 minutes). New guard film `p_burrow` (a major down the ramps into the room and back up), approved with a known small bug: feet, jaws and antenna tips dip up to 0.12 block into the ramps' steps, a few leg jumps of about 0.2 block a tick where slope meets flat (the same kind as the termite ramp scene).

- **Matabele: dead mounds only, for now (the user, 2026-10-24, branch `claude/m8-shallow-nest-e3f664`; not tried in-game yet):** after trying the nests in-game the user dropped the log, tree and burrow sites ("too complicated needlessly, we just keep the dead termite colony"). `colony/MatabeleNest` now only finds the nearest free dead mound with a way in within 32 blocks (the old rooms traced down the chimney, nothing dug); a queen without one has no colony and looks again every 10 seconds (`MatabeleQueenEntity.LOOK_AGAIN`); `/antcolonies matabele <workers>` lost its site word and fails with a hint (`/antcolonies newmound dead 30`) when no dead mound is near. Tests: the log, tree and burrow scenes gone; new `aMatabeleQueenWithNoDeadMoundNearWaitsForOne`; the colony scene puts two dead mounds in for its two colonies (and got sky and soil for them, and finds its colony by the queen, as the colony's middle up the chimney stands above the arena's box). Found by the tests: a queen put down 9 blocks from a dead mound could stand inside its hump and smother (the mound scene failed 2 runs in 8); now the command stands her on top of the ground, and the test ants are put on top too. `MatabeleNestTests` and `MatabeleColonyTests` 36 of 36 (every server, twice). The soil ramp block stays in the framework, unused for now.

- **Fix: far fewer giant trees in new savannas since the termite mounds came (the user's report, 2026-10-24, branch `claude/m8-shallow-nest-e3f664`; not tried in-game yet):** a giant tree of new land was dropped when its whole crown and fallen wood, plus 16 blocks, reached any place the mounds' spacing offered, even where no mound would ever stand (water, a slope, another biome): about 7 in 10 grown big trees were lost (an estimate from a model of the two spacings). Now (`worldgen/TermiteMoundStructure.crowds`, `stands`; `GiantTreeStructure.plan`) only a mound that will really be made counts, with the mound's own checks, and a crowded tree tries up to 7 more places within 24 blocks of its chunk's middle before it gives up (`SPOTS`, `MOVE`, my picks): by the same estimate about 2 in 10 of the widest crowns and a few in 100 of the smaller trees are lost now. Trees and mounds still never overlap (the user's rule). `colony,away,mound,trees` 63 of 63 on 6 servers (with the Matabele change above).

- **M8 step 1e, the Matabele prey pile and hunger BUILT (2026-10-24, branch `claude/m8-prey-pile-197841`; not tried in-game yet; `docs/DESIGN.md` "Step 1e"):** the colony's food is a heap of stung, paralysed Macrotermes in the queen's room, a new colony part `prey_pile` (`savanna/colony/PreyPile`, plugged in as the nurses' food and the colony list's store line). Blocks: `antcolonies:prey_pile` (layers 1 to 8, a layer about 10 termites; fresh, its legs twitch, an animated texture; `dead` once the youngest layer is three quarters of a day old, still and grey-brown) and `antcolonies:termite_remains` (the heap beside the entrance, weathering about a layer every 2 days); item `antcolonies:paralysed_termite` (the piece a nurse carries, in `colonyframework:nurse_food`). Looks made by `tools/prey_pile_art.py`, compared in `python tools/model_viewer.py preypile` (`references/prey_pile_viewer.html`): look B, the tumbled mass, picked by the user 2026-10-24 (`GAME_LOOK`). Rules: every piece is counted with the time it was laid; workers eat 1 termite a day (`worker.diet`, nurse food young and old, 10 meals a layer), the queen and larvae are fed from it by the shared `FeedQueenGoal` and `FeedLarvaGoal` (6 layers an egg, 5 a larva, as before: 60 and 50 termites, to confirm); a layer a day old has rotted; rotten layers and every 8 eaten layers' leftovers are a load carried up and out to the heap 2 to 4 blocks from the entrance (`entity/goal/CarryRemainsGoal`, the load `antcolonies:termite_remains`, `entity/MatabeleLoads`); a load nobody real takes for 2 minutes, and everything while away, is carried out by the numbers and its heap layer laid out of sight. Matabele workers now eat, feed the queen and the larvae, carry eggs and carry the waste out. `/antcolonies prey <layers>` stocks the nearest Matabele colony's pile until the raids exist. Framework change (species-neutral): the numbers' meals and the fold-in of carried nurse food no longer require a species that makes bread (`NestLife.takePiece`, `AwayLife`), so any nurse food is eaten and counted by the numbers. New game tests `MatabeleFoodTests` (category `colony`): stocked, eaten, rotted, carried out to the heap; and an empty pile, the workers starve. `colony,away` 34 of 34 on 6 servers (4 minutes).
- **M8 order and the test revamp scheduled (the user, 2026-10-24):** the test revamp (`docs/plans/test-health.md` step 4) moved to right after M8 step 4, so the Matabele hunt and eat termites before the user's trip; order 1e, 1f, 2, 4, test revamp, 3, 5, 6 (step 4 before 3 my recommendation, to confirm). Notes only.

- **M8 raids before drawn-only ants confirmed (the user, 2026-10-24):** step 4 (scouts and raids, up to about 50 real ants a raid) comes before step 3 (drawn-only ants), which is not to be rushed. Notes only.

## Handover notes for a code reviewer (written at the end of a very long session)
- State: branch `master`, all committed, `./gradlew build` passes, `./gradlew runGameTestServer` passes 47 tests (about 8 minutes, in one world, tests run in parallel arena grids). Some tests are timing-sensitive under load (`antsOnBumpyGroundReachTheFoodStore` and `aStarvingWorkerSlowsDownAndDies` have each failed once and passed on a re-run): re-run before blaming a change.
- Confirmed in-game by the user so far: everything up to and including dead ants and the refuse chamber, rich bread, worker hunger, colony starvation, and the hill-jam fix. Older bullets in "Current status" that still say "waiting for the user to confirm" are probably stale (the user tested them and reported no problems), but this was never written down: do not rely on those flags.
- Next planned step: M7 termites. The user wants to confirm the plan first (CLAUDE.md rule: ask before design decisions).
- Where the weight is (SPLIT in the code review session, step 3): `Colony` now only holds who the colony is, its headcount, worker roles and save/load (about 230 lines). The rest lives in its parts, reached as `colony.nest()`, `.walls()`, `.hill()`, `.food()`, `.bakery()`, `.queenCare()`, `.brood()`, `.refuse()` (classes `Nest`, `NestWalls`, `Hill`, `FoodStore`, `Bakery`, `QueenCare`, `Brood`, `Refuse` in `colony/`). Nest and hill constants moved with them (`Nest.SHAFT_DEPTH`, `Hill.moundHeightAt`, ...); the colony numbers (meals, timers, caps) later moved into the species profile (`colony.profile()`, M7 step 1). Saved key names did not change. Older bullets in this file still name methods as `Colony.something`: look for them in the part they belong to.
- Things worth a second look:
  - Species-neutrality: the colony numbers are in the species profile now (M7 step 1), but room sizes in `NestPlan`, the hill shape, caste stats, the tags and `SPECIES` in `QueenAntEntity` are still the red harvester ant's, in code.
  - Claims: the "who is already on the way" lists now use the small `Claims` helper (`colony/Claims.java`: claim until a game time, prune, contains, size). Leaks (several ants per hole) and hill spots (a claim carries a spot) keep their own maps.
  - Goal priorities in `WorkerAntEntity.registerGoals` are a delicate order (hunger first, then carrying jobs, then roles). Changing one priority can starve another job.
  - Anything that checks "is a cell empty" before placing a block (`placePellet`, egg and corpse spots) has to cope with ants not colliding with each other; the hill jam was this kind of bug.
  - Saved data: `Colony.save/load`, the larva `lastFed`, worker `lastAte` and `queenLastFed` use `Colony.NOT_SET` as "not started"; check old saves still load.
  - `hungerOn` is not saved (defaults to true); the game tests turn it off, which is the only reason they do not starve.
  - Workers of a colony whose queen died are left colonyless and keep wandering (not decided by the user yet). The abandoned-nest idea is only noted, not built.
  - Debug logging: `[pellet] ... gave up` lines in `CarryPelletGoal` are intentional (kept for bug reports); the test code has `[..-test]` log lines.


- Tidy-up, part 1 (branch `tidy-up`, made from `m7-food-finding`; build and all 88 unit tests pass, game tests NOT run yet, nothing to see in-game except the first point):
  - **A dying worker lets go of its load** (ant and termite alike, `AbstractAntEntity.dropLoadOnDeath`): an egg or a dead ant falls where it died (as before for the ant; termites used to lose an egg), bread or a fungus nodule is put down as a heap if there is a floor (else lost and logged), a seed or a piece of forage drops as an item. A soil pellet is just lost.
  - **Plants that are gone are forgotten** (`FoodStore.plants`): when the food plants are scanned again, the cut counts of spots that hold no food plant any more are dropped, so a new tuft in the same spot starts whole (they used to pile up in the save for ever).
  - **The mound lookup no longer breaks blocks** (`Mound.getParts`): crumbling the leftover pieces of earlier stages is now done once a second by the queen's tick (`Mound.tick`), not inside the lookup that the alarm, the route planning and the block-break event call.
  - **Breaking a block far from the nest is cheap** (`Alarm.isBreakIn`): the whole nest is only looked through when the broken block is in or next to a planned nest cell.
  - The `macrotermes.json` note now says the worker ages are used (jobs by age); a test step in `TermiteSoldierTests` fails with a message instead of crashing when its colony is missing.
- Tidy-up, part 2 (user's decisions, 2026-10-13: "realism over everything"; branch `tidy-up`; build and all 89 unit tests pass, game tests NOT run yet; the numbers are my picks, to confirm):
  - **Worker cap 60** (both profiles, `colony.max_workers`; the user's number). To keep the user's "a full mound takes 20+ hours", each worker now adds 0.25 mound blocks a day instead of 0.5 (`mound.blocks_per_day_per_worker`; a full mound in about 20 hours with 60 workers). The soldier test now checks the ranks at 20 workers and that a full worker nymph at the cap grows into a soldier.
  - **Dead termites leave a body** (`ModEntities.TERMITE_CORPSE`, the dead-ant body drawn pale, texture by `tools/corpse_textures.py`; workers and soldiers, `AbstractAntEntity.leavesBody`/`remove`, also starved nymphs). Not when a chicken killed it: the chicken ate it (any insect). Like real termites (no refuse chamber): a body younger than `refuse.eat_fresh_ticks` 2400 (2 minutes) is carried into a garden and eaten there (one layer of fungus comb, `Refuse.setDown`), an older one is walled up with soil where it lies (3 seconds of scraping, `Refuse.bury`); a termite only goes for a body it may be at (by day only inside); one nobody gets to still crumbles after `corpse_decay_ticks`. `CarryCorpseGoal` does both (termite workers have it now, builders' job, priority 3). A body's age is saved (`age`).
  - **Nest stages for the ant** (profile `nest.level_unlocks`, `Nest.levelsUnlocked`, saved as `nestLevelsUnlocked`; nests from older saves dig all levels): a level (stair, tunnel, room) is dug only once the colony has its workers and stored food: seed store 1 worker, nursery 6 workers and 10 seeds, the queen's deep room 12 and 30, the refuse chamber 20 and 30. Once open a level stays open. Before the nursery or the deep room the existing fallbacks hold (brood and bread in the queen's home, her founding chamber). Termites have no stages (one level). `/antcolonies list` shows `nest levels open n of 4`. `NestTests.startColony` opens all levels (tests about digging); new game test `theNestGrowsDeeperOnlyAsTheColonyGrows`; unit test `theNestOpensLevelsAsTheColonyGrows`.
  - **Queenless colonies carry on and dwindle** (`Colony.isQueenless`, `queenDied`, saved as `queenDiedAt`; `ColonyManager.queenDied`): when the queen dies the colony keeps working and raises the brood already there, but no new eggs come; new workers never join it (`findNearest`). It ends (the nest abandoned) once nobody is left: no workers or soldiers, no eggs or young. `/antcolonies list` shows `QUEENLESS`. Game test `aQueenThatIsNotFedForFiveDaysStarvesAndHerColonyDwindles` (replaces the old one).
  - **Workers die of old age** (needed for the dwindling, and real; the user confirmed old age for every worker and the lifespans): profile `worker.lifespan_ticks` (harvester ant 480000 = 20 days, termites 360000 = 15 days), 10% more or less per worker; checked once a second, leaves a body. In a living colony the queen's eggs replace them.
  - **The colony's own clock** (`Colony.tick`, run once a second by `ModEvents.onLevelTick` / `ColonyManager.tickColonies` for every colony whose middle is loaded and whose queen is there or dead): the garden, the mound's leftovers, the mushrooms, the alarm and the food map no longer run on the termite queen (the ant queen's food map tick too), so they keep going in a queenless colony.
  - Not done yet: a game test for the termite bodies; a refuse pile outside for young ant colonies (realistic, real harvester ants have a midden).
- Termite food, part 1: **Dry Grass** (realism pass "Food"; user's decisions 2026-10-13: termites take **only dead material**, green grass never; each tuft **dries with age**; **short and tall** versions; spawns in **savannas, in new chunks**). Branch `termite-food` (from `tidy-up`). Built, game test for it passes, waiting for the user to test in-game.
  - **Blocks:** `block/DryGrassBlock` (`antcolonies:dry_grass`, a tuft) and `block/TallDryGrassBlock` (`antcolonies:tall_dry_grass`, 2 blocks with seed heads), state `growth` = `green`, `drying` (tips tan, base still green, as real grass cures from the tips), `dry` (pale straw). Random ticks move it a step 1 in 27 (about a day and a half per step, dry after about 3 in-game days; my pick, to confirm), then it stays dry until eaten; both halves of the tall plant dry together. Placed by hand it is **dry**; the creative Natural Blocks tab also has "Dry Grass (green)" and "Tall Dry Grass (green)" (the same items carrying `growth=green`) to watch it dry. Bone meal grows a tuft tall (as green as it was). Only shears collect it (keeping how dry it was; tall gives 2 tufts). Burns like vanilla grass when dry or drying, half as well green (my pick). Replaceable like grass.

  - **Food:** `macrotermes.json` plants: `antcolonies:dry_grass` 4 pieces and `antcolonies:tall_dry_grass` 8, both only with `"state": {"growth": "dry"}`; `minecraft:short_grass` and `tall_grass` removed; saplings removed from `tags/item/termite_food.json` (sticks stay). New optional `state` field on a profile's plant (`SpeciesProfile.PlantFood.state`): block state values the plant must have (species-neutral).
  - **Tests:** game test `TermiteFoodTests.dryGrassDriesOutAndTermitesOnlyEatItDry` (passes); `termitesGatherDeadPlantsIntoTheirFungusGarden` now uses Dry Grass and checks a green tuft and a sapling are left alone, `aWorkerFindsAHayBale...` uses Dry Grass by the holes (both NOT run yet: long tests, wait for the full run); unit test `SpeciesProfileTest.termitesOnlyEatDryGrass`.

- Termite food, part 2: **Fallen branches** (user's decisions 2026-10-13: 2 to 3 blocks long, world generation in savannas **and acacias shed them**, **24 pieces with 3 foragers** a branch, **shrinks in stages**). Branch `termite-food`. Built, quick game test passes, waiting for the user to test in-game.
  - **Block** `block/FallenBranchBlock` (`antcolonies:fallen_branch`): state `axis` (x or z), `part` (`base` 5 pixels thick with a broken side stub, `middle` 4, `tip` 3 with a twig) and `eaten` 0 to 3: each stage a pixel thinner and more covered in termite mud (real Macrotermes plaster soil over the wood they eat); the last piece leaves nothing. No collision (thin, like a twig in the grass), needs solid ground under it, drops a stick, burns. Placed by hand: one middle piece lying the way the player faces (Natural Blocks tab).
  - **Eaten in stages** (species-neutral): new interface `block/EatenInStages`; `FoodStore.clippedGrass` asks such a block how it looks after each cut, and a block that already looks gnawed (by another colony, or after the colony forgot it) counts as that much eaten.
  - **Food:** `macrotermes.json` plant `antcolonies:fallen_branch` 8 pieces a block, 1 forager a block (so a 3-block branch is 24 pieces with 3 foragers).
  - **Where they come from:** `worldgen/FallenBranchFeature` (also used for shedding) lays a 2 or 3 block branch on flat solid ground (in air or short plants); world generation about one every 2 chunks of savanna (`neoforge/biome_modifier/fallen_branch.json`, my pick). **Shedding** (`worldgen/BranchShedding`, run from `ModEvents.onLevelTick`): every 5 seconds, one random spot within 48 blocks of each player; if it is under natural acacia leaves (not placed by a player), a branch drops near it with a wood-crack sound, unless 2 already lie within 6 blocks. About one branch per tree every day or two (my pick, to confirm). Only near players (loaded land).

Branch `termite-food`. Built, quick game test passes, waiting for the user to test in-game.

Drops itself, burns a bit more easily than a log. Natural Blocks tab.

World generation about one every 8 chunks of savanna (`neoforge/biome_modifier/fallen_tree.json`, my pick). To see one at once: `/place feature antcolonies:fallen_tree` (and `/place feature antcolonies:fallen_branch`).
