- Fix: **termites crowding the middle hole after a chicken alarm** (user's report 2026-10-13; `docs/BUGS.md` 20; user: "I would prefer for them to have a realistic emergency reaction"). Like real Macrotermes when a predator attacks the foragers, the foragers now run deep into the nest (not stopping in the doorway or the open mound shaft above it), the minors go for the enemy, and the workers seal the hole behind them. Changes: `Nest.isInside` (a mound shaft cell open to the sky is outside), `GoInsideGoal` (never stops in a doorway), `ForagingHoles.plug(level, hole, inside)` (nestmates in the hole are nudged down into the nest; a player, chicken or other colony's insect still stops it), `ForagingHoles.isDoorway`. Game test `TermiteAlarmTests.theMiddleHoleIsPluggedSoonAfterAChickenAlarm` (young roofless mound, 8 foragers, 2 minors, a chicken; passes, but also on the old code: it could not recreate the jam). All 25 termite game tests pass (including the two long termite food tests with Dry Grass). Waiting for the user's retest.
- **Dead Wood look changed** (user's pick 2026-10-14 in the model viewer, "D", their own reference picture): the untouched wood is now a dead version of Minecraft's savanna log, dark grey-brown bark in long strips; **3 versions picked at random per block** (the blockstate lists 3 models for each state), so a trunk never repeats; the termite mud stages kept (user: "I love" them); the cut ends redrawn in brown rings with a dark bark rim, the hollow core growing as before. `tools/dead_wood_art.py` (textures `dead_wood_<stage>_<version>.png`, the old `dead_wood_<stage>` files removed).
- **Model viewer** (the user's name, "save that code for future uses"): `tools/model_viewer.py` + `tools/model_viewer.template.html` build `references/model_viewer.html`, a 3D page (three.js, Minecraft's flat face shading) to turn and zoom; now the fallen tree with 5 Dead Wood looks (A bleached silver, B bark remnants, C old and lichened, D the user's reference, E reference + silver) and the 4 eaten stages. Concept textures in `tools/dead_wood_concepts.py`.
- Fix: **termites punched by the user in creative left no body** (user's report 2026-10-14). The death code only left a body for an insect linked to a colony; one that belongs to no colony (put down with a spawn egg farther than 32 blocks from a queen, or whose colony is gone) died like a vanilla mob. Not proven to be the user's case (the test with a colony passed before), but now every dead worker and soldier leaves a body: one of no colony leaves a plain body (`AbstractAntEntity.bodyType`, termites override it) that crumbles after 6000 ticks (`AntCorpseEntity.LONER_DECAY_TICKS`). Game test `aTermiteKilledByAPlayerLeavesABody` now also kills one with a creative-mode player and one worker of no colony (passes). If it still happens, the user should run `/antcolonies list` near the termites (does a colony show up?).
- **The brain, part 1: the virtual population** (roadmap step 3; user's decisions 2026-10-14: caps **scaled down** to 3,000 ant and 50,000 termite workers; a **small crew of about 10 real workers keeps working** while nobody is near). Branch `brain-population` (from `termite-food`). Built overnight while the user slept; waiting for the user to test in-game.
  - **Most of the colony is numbers** (`colony/Population`, the colony's new part, saved as `insideNest`): workers and soldiers that are not real mobs are counts inside the nest, by caste and by the minute they grew up (their age: it sets a worker's job when it comes out, and when it dies of old age). The headcount (`Colony.getCount`) is the real mobs and the numbers together, so everything that goes by the colony's size (nest levels, soldiers wanted, the queen's size, the mound's speed) sees the whole colony.
  - **How many are real** (`Population.targets`, once a second from `Colony.tick`): a player within `watch_radius` 48 blocks of the nest: up to `real_cap` 50 real mobs, soldiers as many as have a post to guard (6 more minors at a break-in, alarm 3+), at most a quarter of the cap, the rest workers. Nobody near: the `crew` of 10 workers (soldiers only while the alarm is 2 or more). New numbers in each profile's `colony` (all optional, with these defaults).
  - **Coming out and going in** (`swaps_per_second` 2): one comes out in a dug room of the nest (any room, the shaft if none) that no player can see (a sight line from each player's eyes within 64 blocks; `Population.outOfSight`), fed, at an age picked from the numbers (not one about to die of old age). One goes back in only if it holds nothing, is after no enemy, is out of every player's sight and is inside the nest or 24+ blocks from every player; inside the nest first, farthest first. Going in is no death: no body, the headcount stays, and a worker going in tells the colony of the food it found (`FoodMap.wentHome`). Entities: `AbstractAntEntity.nestCaste`, `comeOutOfNest`, `mayGoIntoNest` (ant workers, termite workers and both soldiers).
  - **Young ones** (`Brood.hatchLarva`/`hatchSoldier`): a grown larva or nymph becomes one of the numbers when the colony has all the real ones of its caste it wants and no player can see it; otherwise a real mob as before.
  - **Old age in the numbers** (`Population.growOld`): the workers of each minute die off one by one between 90% and 110% of the lifespan, like the real ones; a dead ant is laid in the refuse chamber while it has room (its nestmates carried it), termites eat their dead in the garden (nothing to see).
  - **Not yet (my pick, to confirm):** the numbers do not eat (they would starve the colony, since they cannot forage as numbers yet: part 2); the brood (eggs, larvae, nymphs) is still real mobs, at most the profile's `max_young`, which limits how fast a colony can grow; the real crew picks its jobs among themselves as before.
  - **Commands:** `/antcolonies population <workers>` adds that many workers to the nearest colony as numbers, of every age (and the soldiers so many workers want), to see a big colony at once; `/antcolonies list` shows `real n (wants m), in the nest k (workers young, middle, old; soldiers)`.
  - **Game tests:** off on the game test server unless a test turns it on (`Population.setOnForTests`, pretend players `setViewersForTests`), so the old tests are unchanged. New `PopulationTests`: the crew with nobody near and all out with a player near, then back to the crew, nobody lost; insects come out only where no player can see; workers in the numbers die of old age (bodies in the refuse chamber) and the numbers are saved; a young one grows up into the numbers; a big termite colony never shows more than the cap, no more soldiers than posts.
- **Game tests on the 65 x 65 arenas** (2026-10-14, overnight): first full run 16 minutes for 104 tests (now 109 with the brain's 5), no neighbour failures. Fixed the middle-hole test (`docs/BUGS.md` 22: it now accepts a hole roofed over by the mound's tunnel block, and counts the fight as over once the minors have gone in behind the workers, leaving the chicken outside, as real soldiers do). Tried removing the test-only arena guards: `FoodMap.setTestArea` and `Alarm.setTestArea` are still needed (full runs 2 and 3: without either, `aWorkerFindsAHayBale...` fails, alone it passes), so both stay.
- **Brain part 1, all 50 work while you are away (user's decision, 2026-10-14):** with nobody near a loaded nest the colony now keeps its full 50 real mobs (soldiers at their posts too) instead of a crew of 10, so a loaded colony plays exactly as before the brain. `crew` is 50 in both species files (a crew as big as `real_cap` counts as watched, `Population.targets`); the crew test uses `setCrewForTests(10)` and also checks all 40 stay out with nobody near. Population tests 5 of 5 pass.
- **New foraging holes near food (termite plan part 1, user's decisions 2026-10-14, branch `termite-holes`):** when the colony knows a **rich** plant (`"rich": true` in the species file: fallen branch, dead wood, hay bale) more than `min_distance` 8 blocks from every way out and within its **tunnel reach** (`nest.new_holes`: `reach_min` 10 blocks for a young colony up to `reach_max` 40 at `reach_full_at_workers` 500 workers, headcount including the numbers; my picks), it plans a **real 1-block tunnel** underground out to it (`colony/TunnelRoute`, a route search that follows the ground one block under the surface, climbs or drops at most 2 blocks between columns underground, goes round stone, water, sand and caves; unit test `TunnelRouteTest`), starting from the dug foraging tunnel block nearest the food, and the workers dig it like the other foraging tunnels (soil into the mound), **one new tunnel at a time**, at most `max` 6 open new holes. The hole opens beside the food (within 2 blocks) at foraging time. Once no rich plant is left within 4 blocks of the food the hole is **spent**: plugged when the foraging ends and never opened again; the tunnel stays part of the nest. New tunnels and spent holes are saved (`Nest.newTunnels`, `spentHoles`). The colony now **forages as far as its tunnels reach** (`Colony.foragingRadius()`: the food radius 24 or the reach, whichever is bigger; used by the plant scan, scouts, the food map, trails, the alarm and the brain). Also: a closed hole is now opened **before** the extra garden rooms are dug (a big colony wants all 6 gardens at once and its foragers were shut in until all were dug). `/antcolonies list` shows `new holes n (spent, being dug), reach r`. Game test `TermiteNewHoleTests.aBigColonyDigsATunnelToFarHayAndPlugsItsHoleWhenTheHayIsGone` (500 workers in the numbers, hay 22 blocks out: a 12-block tunnel planned from the east foraging tunnel, dug, the hole opened, foragers come through it and cut the hay, hay removed: hole spent and plugged, stays shut). Waiting for the user to test in-game.
  - Fix (first full run, 109 of 110 passed): when the nest's foraging tunnels ran north and south and the hay lay east, the planner started from the tunnel block nearest the food, right beside the mound, which boxed it in (no route). Now the route search starts from every dug tunnel block at once and takes the shortest route from any (`TunnelRoute.plan(List, ...)`, unit test `aBoxedInStartIsPassedOverForAnotherOne`); the game test passed 4 runs in a row (21-block tunnels from north/south nests, 12 from east ones). Every other test passed in the full run (16 minutes).
- **New holes fix (user's bug, 2026-10-14):** a 5-block fallen tree up a slope: the termites dug under it but never opened the hole. Surface foragers ate one block of the tree away, and the "is the food still there" check used the food list, which only holds plants near the nest's height, so the rest of the tree (higher up) was missed and the hole was given up for good. The check now looks at the real blocks within 4 of the food at any height, and a hole given up while its food is still there is taken back into use (fixes worlds that already hit the bug).
- **New holes up a slope (user's bugs, 2026-10-14, found with a new game test):** (1) the last block of a tunnel climbing a slope was never dug, because workers only dug blocks beside them or under their feet: a foraging tunnel block can now be dug upward from the tunnel block below it (`Nest.standSpotsFor`). (2) A fallen tree on a terrace more than 4 blocks above the nest was never seen (the plant scan only took plants 1 below to 4 above the nest's middle): rich food (`"rich": true`) is now seen up to 24 blocks above or below (`FoodStore.RICH_HEIGHT`; the user chose 24, "just in case"). Game test `aFallenTreeUpASlopeKeepsItsHoleWhenItsLowestBlockIsEaten` (a tree 5 to 7 blocks above the nest, its lowest block eaten away): it fails on each of the three bugs. The termite tests' food-map fence (`setTestArea`) is now 16 blocks taller. Full run before (2): 111 passed.
- **Hills, part 1 (branch `terrain`, user's decisions 2026-10-14):** the shared **ground reader** (`colony/GroundReader`: the real top ground block of each column, through plants, snow and fallen wood, not the mound; where a tunnel may be dug and a hole opened); the new holes near food use it. The nest's **side foraging tunnels follow the ground** on a hillside (user's bug: the side holes on a hill were opened one block and stayed shut under the soil above): a planned tunnel whose hole is not at the surface is planned again with `TunnelRoute.straight` (straight out, rising and falling with the ground, stopping before stone or a cliff, at least 4 long), worked out once and saved (`fittedTunnels`) so the growing mound never moves it; flat nests and nests whose holes work keep their old tunnels. **Digging reach:** a worker in a block touching the one it digs can always bite it (on a tunnel's step up a slope there is no floor, the workers clung to the far wall just out of reach and never bit). Tests: unit tests for the straight tunnel; game test `HillTests.termiteSideHolesInAHollowFollowTheGroundUpAndOpenAtTheSurface` (a nest in a hollow, ground 4 higher at the holes).
- **Flaky hay tests fixed (test flaw, not a game bug):** the food-finding, alarm and soldier tests put the hay 11 blocks out from a hole at a right angle to its tunnel, which with an unlucky nest direction lay outside the arena: the finder was put down inside the arena's barrier wall and suffocated, or off the ground and fell. The hay now always lies toward the open middle of the arena (`TermiteSoldierTests.intoArena`) and the finder beside it toward the nest. The full run after hills part 1: 112 of 113 passed, the one failure this flaw; the food-finding test then passed 6 times in a row.
- **Hills: side holes downhill too (branch `terrain`, 2026-10-14):** on a hillside the downhill side tunnel kept its flat plan and its hole hung in the air, because the planner tried to dig the first step down inside the nest's own shaft (which it may not touch). Now a side tunnel going downhill leaves the shaft a block lower (the shaft carries on down; real galleries leave it at different depths). A tunnel counts as "already at the surface" only when its hole really is the top ground block, or is open with ground under it. New game test `termiteSideHolesOnAHillsideOpenAtTheSurfaceUphillAndDownhill` (HillTests) and unit test `aStraightTunnelDownhillLeavesTheShaftLowerDown`; the hill test now checks the holes on the real blocks (test arenas can be turned round).
- **Hill arenas for the side holes (branch `terrain`, 2026-10-14):** three more game tests in `HillTests`, all passing first time: a **terrace** 2 blocks high from 7 blocks out (the user's exact bug, the tunnels climb it in one step and open on top), a **cliff** 5 blocks high (too high to tunnel up: the tunnels stop short and open at its foot) and a **ridge** (the ground falls away on every side, both tunnels run downhill).
- **Harvester ant queens walk to level ground (branch `terrain`, user's decision 2026-10-14, "each species its own way"):** a harvester ant queen landed on uneven ground (more than 4 of the 25 columns round her more than 1 block higher or lower) walks to the nearest level spot within 8 blocks before founding her nest (real Pogonomyrmex queens look for flat open ground), or the least uneven one if none is level; if she cannot get there within about a minute she founds where she is. Numbers in `red_harvester_ant.json` under `founding`: `site_search_radius` 8, `max_unevenness` 1 (my picks, to confirm); termites have none (0: they found where they land). Species-neutral parts: `colony/NestSite` (how level a spot is; later M9's site scoring), `GroundReader.surface` (the ground top with no colony yet) and the goal `FindNestSiteGoal` (mind layer, walks with the legs). A queen spawned underground or with no free will founds at once as before. New game test `antQueenOnAHillsideWalksToLevelGroundAndFoundsThere` (HillTests): she walks 5 blocks uphill onto a level shelf, founds, digs her founding chamber and seals herself in, closed all round.
- **Eating dead wood from below, part 1: the feeding gallery (branch `terrain`, user's idea and decisions 2026-10-14):** real termites reach a fallen log through the soil and eat it from underneath, covered, by day and night. Once a new tunnel to rich food is dug, the colony plans a **feeding gallery** (`colony/FeedingGallery`): the ground block under each block of the food (any rich food that is a whole block: a fallen tree and its stump, a hay bale), joined to the tunnel by a short passage underground; a cell that would open the gallery to the outside (air beside it) is left out. The workers dig it after the tunnel (`Nest.findDigTarget`; dug upward too, like a tunnel up a slope). A gallery cell counts as inside the nest while the food over it roofs it. **By day** (no foraging time) foragers of a colony that keeps a schedule still eat food that touches a dug cell inside the nest (`FoodStore.isFeedableFromInside`), walking to it only through the inside (`ClipGrassGoal`); the hole beside the food still opens at night. Saved with the new tunnel (`gallery`). New game test `aFallenLogIsEatenFromBelowByDay` (TermiteNewHoleTests): an 8-block gallery under a 5-block log, eaten by day. Next parts: mud casts (an eaten block becomes a tunnel block), mud on branches, the alarm sending foragers to the nearest hole.
- **Feeding gallery on the edge of a step (user's bug, 2026-10-14):** the user's fallen tree in the savanna got a gallery of 0 blocks: the planner left out every block under the wood with open air beside it, and the tree lay on the edge of a step. Now those blocks are dug too and the open side becomes a hole in the nest walls that the workers pack with soil (`NestWalls`, `SealGoal`, the same sealing as everywhere in the nest), a mud wall like real termites build; only blocks beside water are left out. New game test `aFallenLogOnTheEdgeOfAStepIsEatenFromBelowByDay` (the ground 2 lower on one side: the gallery is walled up and the log eaten by day).
- **Mud crust block and "wall first" (branch `terrain`, user's decisions 2026-10-14):** a new thin block, **Mud Crust** (`block/MudCrustBlock`): a 1-pixel plate of termite mud on any of a block's 6 faces (like glow lichen, but it needs nothing behind it), the look the user picked in `references/mud_crust_viewer.html` (**C, crumbly with pores**; drawn by `tools/mud_crust_art.py`, 3 versions per age). It dries with age like the mound soil (fresh, setting, dry, old), breaks with a punch or two (a break-in for the alarm, as it walls the nest), drops nothing, and a plate whose cell is filled in again (soil, stone) crumbles away. A plate walls off the cell it faces: insects inside can cling to it, the nest walls count it as closed (`NestWalls.isOpenToward`). **Wall first** (species with `nest.crust_walls`, termites, in `macrotermes.json`): the last bite that opens a nest cell first plasters a crust over every side of it that would open to the outside (air, a cave, grass), so a gallery is never open, not even for a tick (`NestWalls.wallFirst`, called from `DigGoal`); the nest's own spaces, the holes, the entrance, the mound and water are left alone. A whole missing block (a player broke in) is still packed full with mound soil as before; a crust missing a plate toward a dug cell gets the plate. Crust standing where the nest wants space is bitten away like soil (`Nest.isSoil`). Placeable by hand from the creative tab (a plate against the face clicked).
- **Feeding gallery planned with its tunnel (user's idea, 2026-10-14):** the user saw the first night's tunnel end in a plain hole beside the log and the gallery under it only start hours later. Real termites tunnel for the food from below from the start. Now the gallery is planned together with the new tunnel (`ForagingHoles.planGallery`), as one job: the workers dig the tunnel, then the gallery, then open the hole beside the food; the outside foragers carry on by the old holes meanwhile. Feeding from inside by day only starts once the whole gallery is dug and closed all round (`Nest.isGalleryClosed`, from `FoodStore.isFeedableFromInside`). Older saves still plan the gallery once the tunnel is dug. The two log tests (`aFallenLogIsEatenFromBelowByDay`, `...OnTheEdgeOfAStep...`) now also check that the gallery is planned with the tunnel, that no dug tunnel or gallery block is ever open sideways or below (wall first), and on the step that the open side is a crust wall.
- **Mud casts (dead wood part 2, user's choice, 2026-10-14):** real termite-eaten logs end up as log-shaped crusts of mud full of tunnels. Now a whole block of dead wood eaten away by a species with `nest.crust_walls` (termites) leaves a **Mud Cast** (`block/MudCastBlock`, a termite tunnel block of mud with the tunnel block's look and channel) instead of air (`FoodStore.castFor`). Its channel opens toward the feeding gallery or tunnel it was eaten from and joins the casts beside it, so the gallery keeps its roof and the termites eat on from inside: casts joined to a gallery count as inside the nest (`Nest.isInside`, `Nest.galleryFor`), and wood beside a cast can be eaten by day (`FoodStore.isFeedableFromInside`), up the stump too. A cast with no wood left on the casts joined to it crumbles away slowly (1 in 12 random ticks, my pick, about a few in-game days). It drops nothing. The wall-first check in the log tests is now strict about the roof too (a gallery block is never open to the sky). Also fixed: the colony's food scan only saw the top food block of each column, so a log block under a stump was never eaten; now food stacked under it is seen (up to 4 blocks, `FoodStore.plants`). New game tests: `aStumpIsEatenByDayFromTheMudCastUnderIt` (the log's cast joins the gallery and the stump, the stump eaten by day from the cast), `mudCastsAndCrustsCrumbleOnceTheWoodIsGone` (quick).
- **Mud over wood (dead wood part 3, user's decision, 2026-10-14):** a fallen branch is thin (nothing bumps into it), so it was no roof for a feeding gallery and branches got none. Now the gallery is dug under branches too (`ForagingHoles.planGallery`), and as the block under a branch is dug the workers **case the branch in mud** on every open side (`NestWalls.wallFirst`, plates of Mud Crust round it, like real termites covering a branch in sheeting); the mud holds the branch up over the gallery (`FallenBranchBlock.canSurvive`). A gap in the casing is a hole in the gallery's roof: the gallery is not closed (no eating by day) and the workers patch it with a plate (`NestWalls.getLeaks`, `wallFor`; a gap right over a foraging hole is left open). A branch block over the gallery eaten away leaves a mud cast too, and the casing round it, no longer needed, crumbles. **Log tops:** each piece cut off a block of dead wood plasters a plate of mud on its top, if nobody stands there and it is not one of the colony's own spaces (`FoodStore.plaster`); it crumbles once the wood under it is a cast. New game test `aFallenBranchIsCasedInMudAndEatenFromBelowByDay` (the branch cased, held up, eaten from below by day).
- **Fixes found by the full run after the mud casts (2026-10-14):** (1) the colony's plant scan began at the first block things bump into, so a thin branch over a dug gallery (and the plate of mud over it) was never seen; it now begins at the top block of any kind and looks past mud plates (`FoodStore.plants`), and ordinary plants count once a column (the two halves of tall grass are one plant; the stack below the top block is only for rich food like a stump on a log). (2) A harvester ant queen on a hillside could plan her founding chamber running out under the slope, its roof open to the sky (which side the plan picks changes with each colony, so the hill test only failed sometimes): a side is now only chosen if the chamber is under cover all round (`Nest.isCovered`; `docs/BUGS.md` 23).
- **Mud sheeting, runways (termite plan part 2, user's plan of 2026-10-14, built while the user tested `terrain`; branch `mud-sheeting`):** real Macrotermes plaster covered runways of soil over the ground out to food near a hole and forage under them unseen, by day too. Now rich food (dead wood, a branch, hay; not grass) the colony knows that lies **nearer than `new_holes.min_distance` (8 blocks) to one of its holes** (farther food gets a new hole, part 1) gets a **runway** (`Nest.Runway`): a row of cells along the ground from the cell over the hole to one beside the food, planned by `colony/RunwayRoute` (Dijkstra over the cells just over the ground, following it up and down one-block steps, climbing them inside the runway; unit test `RunwayRouteTest`). The runway is part of the nest like a feeding gallery: at foraging time the grass in it is cut away (no soil pellet from it), and the builders plaster a **Mud Crust** plate over every open side of it (roof and walls; the leak system, `NestWalls`, now reaches the runway up through its open hole). Once it is closed all round it counts as inside: **its hole stays open by day** (not plugged with the others) and the food at its end is eaten from inside it, by day and night. A hole with a runway is no way out for the outside foragers (it leads into the runway), so a runway is only planned while another hole stays free. When the food is gone (or the hole is spent) the runway is given up, its hole is plugged by day again, and its mud **crumbles** plate by plate (1 in 8 on each check every 10 seconds, about half a day). In `macrotermes.json`: `nest.sheeting` (`max` 3 runways, `max_length` 12 cells). `/antcolonies list` shows "runways n (m finished)". New game test `hayNearAHoleGetsARunwayOfMudAndIsEatenUnderItByDay`. My picks, to confirm: only rich food gets a runway, at most 3 runways of up to 12 blocks, the crumbling speed, runways leave from the holes only (not from other runways or the mound), branches at the end of a runway are cased by the mud-over-wood rules (not tested on their own yet).
- **Bare patches and grass growing back (termite plan part 3, user's plan of 2026-10-14, built during the full test of the `terrain` fix; branch `bare-patches`, from `mud-sheeting`):** real Macrotermes cut the dry grass round their foraging holes down to the ground, and savanna grass grows back from its roots. Now every tuft of Dry Grass (short or tall) the termites eat away is remembered (`colony/BarePatches`, saved with the food store) and **grows back as a young green tuft** of Dry Grass about **3 in-game days** later (it then dries over about 3 more days before the termites take it again). A tuft eaten **within 3 blocks of a foraging hole** turns the grass block under it into **coarse dirt** (bare soil; coarse dirt so the grass does not creep back over it at once) until it grows back, when the ground is grass again; grass eaten farther out leaves the ground as it was. Grass never grows back into a hole, a runway, the nest or under a creature standing there (it waits and tries again later), and not where something else now stands (a block, water); bare ground whose tuft can never come back turns back to grass anyway. In `macrotermes.json`: `food.regrowth` (`plants`, `grows_back`, `state`, `ticks` 72000, `bare_radius` 3); a species without it (the harvester ant) is unchanged. `/antcolonies list` shows "n eaten tufts growing back (m on bare ground by the holes)". New game test `dryGrassEatenByAHoleLeavesBareGroundAndGrowsBack`. My picks, to confirm: 3 days to grow back, a 3-block patch, coarse dirt for the bare soil, tall grass grows back short (bone meal still makes it tall), the patch comes back as soon as its tuft does (a busy hole stays bare because the young grass dries and is eaten again).
- **Fix: walls and runways no longer wipe out food (found by the garden food test while building bare patches, which failed about half the time):** the builders put a mud plate where a dead bush stood beside a new runway (and cut food out of a runway's way the same as green grass), so its 6 pieces of food were lost. Real termites eat the plants they build over. Now a food plant (dry grass, a dead bush) where a wall or a runway goes is cut down first and its uncut pieces are left lying on the spot as forage for the foragers to carry in (`FoodStore.cutAwayForTheForagers`, used by `NestWalls` and `DigGoal`); dry grass cut this way grows back like eaten grass. The garden food test passed 4 runs out of 4 after the fix. Then the cut pieces were dropped inside the spot that got the plate, where nobody could reach them, and vanished after 5 minutes like any dropped item: now they are left on an open spot beside it outside the nest and runways, and never vanish (cut grass lies until a forager carries it in; `FoodStore.dropSpotBeside`). 5 runs out of 5 after that.
- **Hill arenas for the termite mound and the ant hill (hills plan, the last open part; branch `hill-arenas`, from `bare-patches`):** new game tests `termitesBuildTheirMoundRoundAHillside` (ground rising a block every 3 blocks; the first two stages are built, the low side filled down to the ground, the hillside itself standing in for soil on the high side) and `antHillOnAStepKeepsItsRoundShape` (a 1-block step 2 blocks from the hole; the hill's top keeps its round planned shape; passed with no change). Bug found by the mound test: on the low side, the termites had plastered a mud crust over the open side of the dip beside the entrance shaft (wall first), right where the mound's foot needed fill soil, and the builders never built over a crust, so the mound's base was never finished. Now mound soil may go into a cell holding a mud crust (`TermiteMoundBlock.isRoomForSoil`; a whole block of soil walls the shaft as well).
- **Fix from the user's first in-game test of the mud over wood (2026-10-14):** termites eating a branch from outside at night were cased in with it (a plate of mud put down in the spot they stood in), could plan no route out and were shut out by day. Now a plate of mud is never put down over an insect: nestmates are nudged out to an open spot beside it first (`NestWalls.plate`, `plugLeak`), and an insect caught inside a block that is not open plans its route from the open spots beside it (`AntNavigator.plan`, a safety net in the shared legs). `docs/BUGS.md` 24, game test `aPlateOfMudIsNeverPutDownOnATermite`. The user's other report, the fallen tree "ignored": the world save shows it was eaten whole (a row of Mud Casts with mud on top, 4 blocks from the north hole); food that near a hole gets no new tunnel and is eaten from outside at night like grass (with mud sheeting, branch `mud-sheeting`, it gets a covered runway and is eaten by day too).
- **Game tests slimmed down and sorted into categories (user's idea, 2026-10-14; branch `hill-arenas`):** from 127 tests to 96 (plus the stress test). Tests that built the same scene were merged into one (every check of both kept), true duplicates removed. Categories in `TestFilter.CATEGORIES` (`legs`, `antnest`, `mound`, `termitefood`, `soldiers`, `colony`, `hills`, `stress`), run with `ANTCOLONIES_TESTS=<category>`. The rule for new tests is in CLAUDE.md ("Test categories and no duplicate tests").
  - **Walking (30 to 15):** removed the 3 walks to shallow rooms (the deepest-room walks cover them) and the 2 walks through the hole in the hill (covered by the climb-out and food-store tests); merged: the queen's two walks into `queenWalksFromTheRoomUpToTheSurfaceAndBackDown`, the 4 room climb-outs into `antsClimbFromEveryRoomThroughTheFinishedHill` (16 ants), the 2 pits into `antsClimbOutOfAOneBlockPit`, the 4 food-store scans into `antsFromAllAroundTheHillReachTheFoodStore` and `antsOnBumpyGroundOverAHalfBuiltHillReachTheFoodStore`, the shaft-wall pairs into `antsStayOnTheShaftWallWhenTheirWalkEndsOrIsStopped` and `antsHangingInTheShaftCrawlRoundToTheOtherWall`, the 2 reloads into `antsOnTheShaftWallAndTheCeilingAreStillThereAfterAReload`.
  - **Ant nest (27 to 23):** `workersCloseEveryHoleInTheNestWallsAndMendDamage` (holes closed first, then the damage mended), `manyAntsWithPelletsAndOneGapDoNotJam` now also sets a pellet among frozen ants and a sheep first, `workersCarrySeedsAndClipGrassIntoTheFoodStore`, and the milling-time check moved into `queenIsFedAndHerEggGoesToTheNursery`.
  - **Termites (70 to 58):** removed the old chimney crown peek (the mound no longer uses that block), the flat "first two stages" (the young cathedral and the hillside mound cover it), "stage 13 grows into 14" (the user's own half-built stage 14 test covers the same bug), the hollow and ridge side holes (the hillside test runs one tunnel uphill and one downhill), "a chicken hunts a worker" (the chicken-bite test starts with it) and "a soldier drums standing still" (the footsteps test drums). Merged: the hole-block collision check into `aTermiteWalksThroughAChimneyBlockIntoAClosedRoom`; the spire mouth's look-round checks (head just out, holding still, whole look round, no look on the way in) and the way back in by the mouth into `aTermiteCrawlsUpInsideASpireAndComesOutOfItsMouth`; ants and soldiers into `antsAndSoldiersFindNoWayIntoTermiteTunnels`; the garden test into `termitesGatherDeadPlantsIntoTheirFungusGardenAndRaiseTheQueensEggsOnIt` (`TermiteGardenTests` is gone; batch `garden`); the log eaten from below into `aFallenLogAndTheStumpOnItAreEatenFromBelowByDay`.
- **Wall first holds the last bite (user's report 2026-10-14, a mud wall missing on a 2-block-high open side of a branch's gallery on a hill):** `NestWalls.wallFirst` now returns whether every plate went in, and `DigGoal` keeps the block's last bite (cracks at full) until it has, so a gallery never opens without its walls; a refused plate is logged as `[walls] no plate yet`. `TermiteNewHoleTests.aFallenBranchOnTheEdgeOfAStepIsCasedInMudAndEatenFromBelowByDay` (was the flat branch test): the branch on a step with tall grass round it, and its casing checked every tick. `TermiteRunwayTests.hayNearAHole...` now climbs a step one block high (2-high runway column). The runway log says when a runway is dropped because its hole was plugged for good (it said "the food is gone"). See `docs/BUGS.md` 25.
- **Code health (user's request, 2026-10-14; branch `code-health`, from `hill-arenas`; no change in game):** (1) **Nest room sizes in the species file:** `nest.room_sizes` (`queen`, `nursery`, `food`, `refuse`, `founding`, each `{"length": n, "width": n}`, 1 to 7; optional, the defaults are the old sizes: 3x3 queen, 5x5 nursery, food store and refuse chamber, 3x3 founding chamber), read into `NestPlan.RoomSizes`. A nest keeps the sizes it was planned with (saved as `nestRoomSizes`; older saves get the defaults), so changing the file only changes new nests. A species with a fungus garden keeps its royal chamber 3x3 (the garden rooms are laid out round a 3x3 room; a warning in the log otherwise). Unit tests in `NestPlanTest` and `SpeciesProfileTest`. (2) **Big files split into parts** (same behaviour, same save keys): `Nest` (1388 lines to about 930) gave away `NestLayout` (where the rooms go, and choosing it from the ground) and `FoodWays` (the new tunnels to rich food, their feeding galleries, spent holes and runways of mud sheeting; reached by `colony.nest().foodWays()`); `Mound` (1003 to about 890) gave away `MoundShaping` (carving tunnels, reshaping and clearing pieces; `colony.mound().shaping()`). `AntNavigator` (about 1500 lines) is left whole on purpose: it is one state machine whose motions (walking, climbing, channels, doorways) share the same few fields, and the legs are where most bugs have been (`docs/BUGS.md`), so cutting it up would risk more than it gains; split it when a new kind of move is added. (3) **Flaky larva test fixed:** `aLarvaThatIsNotFedForTwoDaysStarves` failed in full runs with "0 bodies, expected 1" because the colony's own worker had already picked the body up to take it to the refuse chamber; a body being carried now counts too (`docs/BUGS.md` 18d).
- **Flaky tests fixed and CLAUDE.md slimmed (code health part 2, 2026-10-14; branch `code-health`):** (1) `hayNearAHoleGetsARunwayOfMudAndIsEatenUnderItByDay` failed now and then with "no runway was planned to the hay": the hay was put past the colony's first foraging hole, and when that hole faces north or west (the side is picked per colony) the hay lay 1 block outside the arena, under its barrier wall, so the colony's plant scan never saw it. It was never neighbouring tests. The test now uses a hole that faces into the arena. New `ForagingHoles.whyNoRunway` says which check stops a runway being planned (used in the failure message). (2) The three dead wood tests (`eatenFromBelow`) get 60000 ticks instead of 40000: once the stump was still on its last bitten stage when the time ran out. (3) CLAUDE.md went from about 69,000 to 23,000 characters: every design decision for the species and shared systems moved word for word into the new `docs/DESIGN.md` (read the section for a feature before working on it); CLAUDE.md keeps a short summary.
- **The brain, part 2, step 2a: going away and coming back** (user's decisions 2026-10-14, see `docs/DESIGN.md` "Brain part 2 decisions": colonies live while away, food only from known plants, building applied on return; realism over everything). Branch `brain-offline` (from `code-health`). New colony part `colony/AwayLife`. (1) **Frozen insects are written down:** when a chunk with one of the colony's workers, soldiers, eggs or larvae goes out of range, the colony writes down what it was (`AwayLife.freeze`, from `ModEvents.onLeaveLevel`: NeoForge fires that event when the chunk stops being tracked, before Minecraft unloads it, and the chunk can also come back without being unloaded, so the insect itself checks on its first tick back, `AwayLife.backFromFrozen`, called from `AbstractAntEntity`, `AntEggEntity` and `AntLarvaEntity`). (2) **Away:** once the nest's middle has been out of range for a minute (`GRACE_TICKS` 1200, my pick), the colony is away (`ColonyManager.tickColonies` runs `AwayLife.tickAway` once a second for it): every frozen insect is folded into the numbers (workers and soldiers into `Population` at their age; eggs and larvae into the new **young as numbers**, `Brood.Young`, with their time to hatch, meals and hunger; what a worker held is owed to the nest: a seed, nurse food, an egg, a body; a soil pellet is lost as on death). The numbers grow old while away; their bodies are owed (never a block read or changed in unloaded land). (3) **Coming back:** the insects that were folded in leave the world quietly on their first tick when their chunk loads (no death, no body; the headcount stays) and fresh ones come out of the nest as before. One frozen for less than the grace carries on; one frozen longer while the colony was here (a forager at the edge of the loaded land) has walked home: it is folded in then. Once the whole nest is loaded (`AwayLife.settle`, from `Colony.tick`), the queen's clocks (hunger, the egg she carries, the next egg) are moved on by the time away (she was frozen too; she cannot starve in the second before that, `QueenCare.queenStarves`), and what is owed is put in its place out of every player's sight: the young into the nursery as real eggs and larvae (clocks going on where they stopped), bodies into the refuse chamber (termites ate theirs), seeds into the store, nurse food back where the nurses keep it. **Fixes the old bug "hunger clocks run on in unloaded chunks"** (the queen and the young starved on the way back after a long time away). **Not yet (step 2b):** while away nobody eats and the young do not grow (their clocks stand still). `/antcolonies list` shows `away n ticks, frozen n (folded in n), owed to the nest: ...`. Off on the game test server unless a test turns it on (`AwayLife.setOnForTests`). Game test `PopulationTests.aColonyAwayFoldsItsFrozenInsectsIntoTheNumbersAndPutsThingsBackOnReturn` (3 workers, one with a seed, an egg, 2 larvae, the queen 100 ticks from starving, 4 old workers in the numbers: away, all folded in, 4 die; back: the frozen ones leave the world, the queen lives, the young are real again, 4 bodies in the refuse chamber, the seed in the store; two stragglers: back after a moment carries on, back after the grace walked home). Population tests 6 of 6 pass, the colony and antnest categories 34 of 34 pass; full run not done yet. Waiting for the user to test in-game.
- **The brain, part 2, step 2b: eating and raising the brood while away** (user's decisions 2026-10-14, see `docs/DESIGN.md` "Step 2b decisions"). Branch `brain-offline`. (1) **The numbers eat** (`colony/Meals`, part of `Population`): when each number last ate is kept by the minute next to its age; once a second the ones whose meal is due eat, the hungriest first, while there is food (workers a seed from the store, soldiers a piece of nurse food); a worker number gone its starve time dies and leaves a body; soldiers never starve as numbers. Always on, here or away (user's decision). A real insect going into the nest keeps its last meal and gets it back when it comes out (`AbstractAntEntity.getLastMeal/setLastMeal`). Termite workers do not eat yet (`worker.eats: false` in `macrotermes.json`; real eating is the next small step). (2) **Life in the nest as numbers** (`colony/NestLife`): the young that are numbers grow up (eggs hatch, larvae get hungry, are fed, starve or grow into worker or soldier numbers), here and away; while away the nurses (the young workers, `Colony.shareOut`) do one job each per `NURSE_JOB_TICKS` 600 (half a minute, my pick, to confirm): feed the queen first (rich bread first), then the hungriest larva, then mill a seed into bread while there is less than wanted (`Bakery.needsBreadAway`). The queen lives on in her room (`QueenCare.liveAway`): the founding batch, the termite queen's stream, the egg she carries; her eggs are numbers; she starves if nobody feeds her and dies on return (`queenStarvedAway`). Her clocks no longer stop while away (`QueenCare.pause` removed). The fungus grows nodules on the comb while away from what the brain last saw of the garden (`FungusGarden` keeps a snapshot each second: combs, spare layers, nodules; saved as `gardenSeen`). What the brain last saw of the queen (in her room, sealed in, size) is saved as `nestLife` (my pick). (3) **Counts first, blocks later** (`colony/NestLedger`, replaces the owed things in `AwayLife`): every change to the food store, the nurse food and the bodies is counted at once and the blocks catch up out of every player's sight once the nest is loaded (no room: the food is dropped from the count, the bodies carried out); older saves' owed things are read into it. Frozen young's clocks go on while they wait to be folded in; a frozen worker's last meal is kept. `/antcolonies list` shows `hungry n` and the ledger (`owed to the nest: n bodies, -n food, +n nurse food`). Game test `PopulationTests.aColonyAwayEatsMillsBreadAndRaisesItsYoungAsNumbers` (300 young workers, 48 seeds, an egg and 2 larvae as numbers, the queen carrying an egg: away, the young grow into workers (303), her egg comes out as a number, the nurses mill bread and feed her; with 19 seeds left and the workers nearly starved, 19 eat and 284 starve; back: the queen lives, her egg is real, the ledger is empty, bodies in the refuse chamber); the 2a test now checks the queen is alive instead of her clock standing still. Population tests 7 of 7 pass; full run (2026-10-15, 23 minutes) 98 of 99 pass, the one failure (`TermiteNewHoleTests.aFallenTreeUpASlopeKeepsItsHoleWhenItsLowestBlockIsEaten`, tunnel not dug in time) passes alone and with its class (a full-run-only clash, as before). Not covered by a test yet: the termite side while away (nodules, the queen's stream, soldiers eating). Waiting for the user to test in-game.
- **Full game test run after brain part 2, step 2a** (2026-10-14, branch `brain-offline`): **all 97 tests pass**, 23 minutes. - **Termite workers eat** (user's decisions 2026-10-15, the small step after brain part 2 step 2b, see `docs/DESIGN.md` "Termite worker eating"; branch `brain-offline`). Like real Macrotermes: young workers (nurse age) eat **fungus nodules**, builders and old workers eat **old comb** (a layer of a comb block over its bed; the bed stays for the fungus); a worker whose own food is missing eats the other kind. **One piece makes 4 meals** (`worker.diet.workers_per_piece`, my pick, confirmed by the user), once a day each, slow after 1.5 days and dead after 3 without (the ants' numbers); the **hunger clocks start only once the colony has had its first comb** (`hungry_from_first_food`; `FoodStore.hasHadFood`, saved as `hadFood`). Species-neutral: `worker.diet` in the profile (`young`/`older`: `store` or `nurse_food`; the ants keep the default, store only, so they never eat bread). New `colony/WorkerFood` (where a meal comes from, the meals left in a started piece, saved as `workerMealsLeft`), `entity/WorkerHunger` (the hunger clock and slowdown, moved out of `WorkerAntEntity` and shared by ant and termite workers; made on first use because the goals are set up before the fields), `EatSeedGoal` renamed `EatGoal` (any worker: walks to its meal through the legs and eats it). The numbers eat in `Meals` by the same rules: the young share of the worker numbers eats nodules, the rest comb, the blocks follow out of sight through `NestLedger`. Game test `PopulationTests.termiteWorkersEatNodulesWhenYoungAndOldCombWhenOlder` (no comb yet: two nearly starved workers stay fine; comb with nodules: the young one starts a nodule, the old one a layer of comb, no bed eaten; 100 worker numbers, 40 young and 60 old: exactly 10 nodules and 15 layers, nobody starves). Found on the way: with a fall-back to "the other food" for every species the ants ate the queen's bread once the seeds were gone (`aColonyAwayEatsMillsBread...` failed); now only a diet with two foods falls back. `termitefood` and `colony` categories: 33 of 34 passed on the first run (the bread one, then fixed and passing). Waiting for the user to test in-game. - **Mouth-to-mouth feeding** (user's request 2026-10-15, see `docs/DESIGN.md` "Mouth to mouth and the ageing comb"; branch `brain-offline`). A termite nurse now eats the nodule at the comb (`MouthToMouth.SWALLOW_TICKS` 20, crumbs and a soft chewing sound), carries it in its crop (`AbstractAntEntity.swallowToPassOn`, synced `isFoodInCrop`: nothing drawn in its jaws, saved as `FoodInCrop`; a nurse that dies with it loses it), walks to the other's head (`AbstractAntEntity.headPoint`, half its body length in front of its middle) and the two stay head to head for `FEED_TICKS` 40 (my picks) with tiny droplets between their mouths, then the food is passed (`FeedQueenGoal`, `FeedLarvaGoal`: new legs SWALLOW and PASS; where the head cannot be reached, beside the middle as before). **The king is fed** too (`TermiteKingEntity implements FedByNurses`: a nodule a day, the workers' meal interval, my pick; he does not starve; `Colony.getKing`). **The queen's court:** termite nurses waiting by the queen stand on a half ring of 5 places round her head (`StandByQueenGoal`). Species-neutral: `worker.diet.mouth_to_mouth` (true for Macrotermes; the harvester ant keeps carrying). The numbers are unchanged. Game test: `termiteWorkersEatNodulesWhenYoungAndOldCombWhenOlder` now also brings a hungry king and checks a nurse carried a nodule in its crop and fed him head to head. `soldiers`, `termitefood`, `colony` and `antnest` categories: all 63 pass. Also the **comb looks** for the ageing comb (the next step): `tools/comb_age_art.py` draws fresh, ripe and old comb in three looks (A field photo, B brain sponge, C bold pixels), shown by `python tools/model_viewer.py comb` in `references/comb_viewer.html` (template `tools/comb_viewer.template.html`: a life-of-a-comb row and a garden room well fed or going hungry). Waiting for the user to pick a look, and to test mouth to mouth in-game.
- **Ageing fungus comb** (user's request 2026-10-15, look **A, Field photo** picked in the comb viewer; see `docs/DESIGN.md` "Mouth to mouth and the ageing comb"; branch `brain-offline`). Like real Macrotermes comb, a conveyor belt: `FungusCombBlock` gets `old` and `fresh` (0 to 4 layers; ripe = the rest), drawn in bands from the bottom: old (pale buff, dry, holes), ripe (mid-brown with white fungus threads), fresh (dark wet pellets). New forage goes on top as a fresh layer (`FoodStore.addLayerAt`, with a soft squelch and dark specks when a forager lays it); in `FungusGarden.grow` once a second a fresh layer ripens about every `garden.ripen_ticks` (12000, half a day; a puff of white threads), a nodule grows from a ripe layer and turns it **old instead of using it up** (so one piece of forage feeds twice: a nodule, then old comb), a ripe layer goes old by itself after about `old_ticks` (48000, 2 days), and an old layer nobody eats **crumbles away** after about `crumble_ticks` (72000, 3 days; added so a small colony's garden never clogs with spent comb; my picks, to confirm). Old workers eat **old layers from the bottom** (`FoodStore.layersToTake`/`eatSeedAt`, crumbs where they chew); the "bed stays" rule is gone (a comb eaten right down frees its spot for fresh comb); never under an egg or nymph. While away (`NestLife.growNodules`): the brain knows the ages as last seen plus what is owed (`FungusGarden.freshKnown/ripeKnown/oldKnown`), ripens, ages and crumbles them, nodules turn ripe layers old; `NestLedger` owes `ripened` and `aged` layers (saved) and puts them right out of sight on return; the numbers eat comb only while old comb is known (`WorkerFood`). Art: `tools/comb_age_art.py` draws the looks and writes the game's 6 textures, 124 band models, the nodule models and a multipart blockstate (the old flat comb models and textures are gone; `tools/fungus_garden_art.py` now only writes the nodule and the loot table). Game test: the eating test's combs are aged (2 old, 1 ripe, 1 fresh) and it now also checks that forage goes on top fresh, and that a sped-up comb of 4 fresh layers ripens, goes old and crumbles from the bottom (`FungusGarden.setAgeingSpeedForTests`); old comb is eaten from below (no comb loses its fresh top). The garden test's bookkeeping now counts pieces eaten and crumbled instead of nodules grown. `termitefood`, `soldiers` and `colony`: 39 of 40 passed, the 40th (that bookkeeping) passes after the fix. Not covered by a test yet: the comb ageing while away. Waiting for the user to test in-game.

A Macrotermes colony (100 worker numbers, 40 young and 60 old; 2 minor and 1 major soldier numbers; aged comb with 3 nodules on each block; a full-grown queen in the royal chamber) goes away with the comb ageing sped up 100 times. Checks while away: fresh layers ripen, ripe ones go old, nodules grow, old ones crumble out of the food count, and the known ages still add up to the layers counted; the nurses feed the queen nodules and she lays her stream (eggs as numbers); then with the meals due the young share eat about 10 nodules, the old ones about 15 layers of old comb, the soldiers a nodule each, nobody left hungry. Back in range the ledger empties and the comb blocks hold exactly the layers, ages and nodules the brain counted. It found two real bugs, both fixed (`docs/BUGS.md` 26): the ledger took eaten layers off before marking the ones gone old while away (the extra layers stayed on the blocks), and a comb used up to its last layer took its nodules with it (first moved onto other comb; the user then decided they are lost with the comb, as in real life, see below). Also: `FungusGarden.setAgeingSpeedForTests` now speeds the ageing while away too, nodules grown while away count in `getNodulesGrown`, and `Population.hungry` tells how many numbers are due a meal. `colony` and `termitefood` categories pass.

- **Brain part 2, step 2c: the numbers forage** (user's decision 2026-10-15: always, not only while away, but only from plants no player can see right then; design in `docs/DESIGN.md`). New `colony/FieldLife`: while its land is loaded the brain remembers every known food plant (harvester ants: every plant in the area; termites: the ones found and told of), with its kind, the walk (nearest way out to the plant, plus way out to the nest's middle) and, for a species that keeps a schedule, whether it can be eaten from inside. Once a second the forager-age numbers (`Colony.shareOut`, as the nurses) go to the plants worth the most (`FoodStore.worth`), no more at a plant than may cut at it (less the real foragers there), each bringing one piece a trip of 200 ticks plus 8 ticks a block there and back (my picks, to confirm); a place at a plant stays taken from setting out to the cut (plus the 10-second rest of a one-forager plant), so a tuft of grass gives about a piece every 15 to 18 seconds. Same rules as the real foragers: foraging time only (termites by day only food eaten from inside), not while the alarm keeps everyone in, only while the store has room (`FoodStore.watchRoom` / `roomKnown`, the room last seen less what is owed). Pieces are counted at once (`NestLedger`); the pieces cut off a plant are `FoodStore`'s saved count (`cutByNumbers`), and the plant's block catches up once it is loaded and out of every player's sight (`FoodStore.showCuts`: eaten in stages, or gone; dead wood leaves its mud cast; quiet, no crumbs). A used-up plant is forgotten (`FoodMap.forget`) and eaten dry grass grows back counted from when it was eaten (`BarePatches.eatenAway(.., eatenAt)`). Real foragers now skip a plant the numbers have used up but whose block still stands. New sight check for one block, `Population.outOfSight(level, BlockPos, eyes)` (a solid plant like a hay bale the look runs into counts as seen; the old point check would call it hidden). Away report: `pieces of food brought in`, `plants eaten up`. `/antcolonies list` shows `foragers out as numbers n (remembers m plants, k to show)`. Saved: the remembered plants and the plants to show (`field`), the store room (`seenRoom`). Off on the game test server unless a test turns it on (`FieldLife.setOnForTests`). Game tests (category `away`): `antNumbersForageOnlyOutOfSightAndFromWhatTheyRemember` (hidden tufts eaten up and gone, tufts in sight left alone, while away the remembered ones eaten too but not a tuft grown after the brain last looked; the report counts it) and `termiteNumbersForageKnownDryGrassOnlyAtNight` (nothing by day, known dry grass eaten up at night, the unknown tuft left, regrowth 3 days from when it was eaten). Both need `skyAccess = true`: the game test arena otherwise gets an invisible barrier roof, and the plant scan reads the top block of each column. `away` passes (5 of 5).
- **Flaky eating test fixed** (`termiteWorkersEatNodulesWhenYoungAndOldCombWhenOlder`, category `termitefood`, failed about 3 runs in 4 on its own, before and after step 2c; `docs/BUGS.md` 27). The insects were right: old workers only ever ate old layers off the bottom. Two causes, both the comb ageing while the test watched. (a) "a comb lost its fresh top": the two workers take 10 to 20 seconds to eat, and in that time a fresh top layer sometimes ripened by itself (about 1 chance in 600 a second for each of about 9 combs), as real comb does; the test now stands the comb's ageing still (`setAgeingSpeedForTests(0)`) while it checks the eating, and the ageing is still checked at the end. (b) "the comb never went old before it crumbled": `FungusGarden.grow` let one layer go old and crumble in the same second, so at the test's 400 times speed the test never saw it old; now a layer goes at most one step a second (only layers ripe when the second began can go old, only layers old then can crumble). At normal speed no change in game. Passes 5 runs in 5 alone; `termitefood` and `away` pass.
- **Brain part 2, step 2d: building while away, done on return** (user's decision 2026-10-14: "building while away is applied when the player returns"; picks in `docs/DESIGN.md`). New `colony/BuildLife`: while away the builder-age numbers (`Colony.shareOut`, as the nurses and foragers) each work every second; the brain only adds up that time (`work`, ticks of one builder's work; it never touches unloaded land). Once the whole nest is loaded again, `AwayLife.settle` spends it first (before the young, seeds and bodies are put back, so they have the new rooms), up to 200 blocks a second, only where no player can see and nobody stands: the nest's plan dug in the real diggers' order (`Nest.findPlannedDigTarget`: the same order as `findDigTarget` without opening the plug or a hole, cutting runway grass or the mound's own digging; mud walls first through `NestWalls.wallFirst`), every bite's soil heaped on the ant hill while it has room (`Hill.placePellet`; if a player can see the next hill spot the digging waits), and the termite mound built stage by stage, lowest first, each cell the plan's own block (`Mound.jobAt`: soil put in finished and set at the plan's age, at least 1; pieces; tunnels carved and pieces reshaped by `MoundShaping.shape`; old soil cleared out of the shaft), its soil from the nest's digging and then the galleries under it (a dug block gives 5 pellets, a mound block takes 5, a piece 1). A finished stage moves on to the next earned one (`Mound.nextStage`; as the real termites, a stage stuck on a spot nobody can build moves on anyway). Pace (my picks, to confirm): a bite 10 ticks (as `DigGoal`), a pellet carried 8 ticks a block there and back, a mound block shaped in 30 ticks (as `ShapeMoundGoal`). The plan still sets how far they go (nest stages by colony size, the mound's stage by what it has earned), so time left over once nothing more may be built is dropped (`[away-build] ... is done` in the log). Away report: `blocks dug`, `pellets of soil heaped on the hill`, `mound blocks built`; `/antcolonies list` shows `owed to the nest: n builder-hours of building` until it is spent. Saved (`awayBuilding`: the time and the soil not built in yet). Game tests (category `away`): new `anAntColonyAwayDigsItsRoomsAndHeapsItsHillOutOfSightOnReturn` (two rooms left undug: nothing dug while away; back with a pretend player watching the hill nothing is dug; the player gone, both rooms dug, 64 pellets on the hill, the report counts them, the left-over time dropped; needs `skyAccess`, the hill reads the top block of each column), and new step 2d checks in `aTermiteColonyAwayAgesItsCombFeedsItsQueenAndEatsAsNumbers` (stage 2 earned: nothing built while away; nothing while a player watches; then the mound goes up out of sight, every block finished and set, its soil dug). `away`, `antnest` and `mound` pass (38 of 38); the full run passes (103 of 103, 2026-10-15, no neighbour failures).
