Read first what the `away` and `termitefood` tests already covered (each of steps 2a to 2d had its own scene), so the new checks fill the real gaps. New game tests (category `away`): `anAntColonyAwayLivesOnThroughASaveAndLoadAndCatchesUpOnReturn` (the whole ant colony at once: 300 worker numbers of every age, 48 seeds, the refuse chamber undug, 4 remembered tufts seen by a pretend player while here; away, then the game is **saved and loaded again while away** (`AwayTests.saveAndLoad`: the loaded copy must write exactly the same save, lists compared in any order, and takes the colony's place through the new `ColonyManager.replaceForTests`) and the copy lives on: bread milled, queen and young fed, the young grow up, the 8 seeds foraged, builders' time counted, the report counting from before and after the save; back: the room dug, its soil on the hill, the tufts gone, the ledger empty); `aStarvingAntColonyAwayLosesItsYoungAndItsQueen` (no food at all: 2 long-hungry larvae starve as numbers and leave bodies, a third lives, the queen starves frozen in her room, the workers live; back, she dies as her land loads, the colony carries on queenless, the bodies lie in the refuse chamber); `aFoundingAntQueenRaisesHerFirstWorkersWhileAway` and `aFoundingTermitePairRaiseTheirFirstWorkersWhileAway` (one species-neutral scene, `foundsWhileAway`: the real queen, or royal pair, seal themselves in; away, she lays the rest of the founding batch, the founders feed the young from their fat, they grow up into the first 3 or 5 workers as numbers, she does not starve; back, the workers come out as real ones and dig the entrance open). New checks in existing scenes: `aTermiteColonyAwayAgesItsCombFeedsItsQueenAndEatsAsNumbers` now saves and loads while away before coming back (the comb ages, nodules, food, eggs, young, meals and building time survive, and the comb and mound checks on return run on the loaded copy); `termiteNumbersForageKnownDryGrassOnlyAtNight` is now `...OnlyAtNightOrInTheRain` and goes on with the species' own rule through a pretend sky (new `Colony.setSkyForTests`, only that colony sees it, unlike the test world's real rain): a dry day nothing, rain by day eaten, alarm at 2 nothing, store full (new `FoodStore.setRoomForTests`) nothing, room again eaten; the hay test (`aWorkerFindsAHayBale...`) checks the brain never remembers the hay before the colony knows of it, then remembers its walk by the nearest way out (new `FieldLife.walkTo`) and that it cannot be eaten from inside; the log test (`aFallenLogOnTheEdgeOfAStepIsEatenFromBelowByDay`) turns the numbers on by day and checks they go only to food they can eat from inside (`FieldLife.remembersFromInside`). No brain bug found: the three failures on the way were the tests' own (too few seeds for the queen's 20-piece meal and the larvae, too little time for grass cut one forager at a time, and the queen's own egg growing up in the extra time). 108 game tests now; `away` passes (10 of 10, twice) and `termitefood` passes (19 of 19).
- **Brain part 4, the king fix** (user's plan 2026-10-15, done first, before 4a; see `docs/DESIGN.md` "Brain part 4 decisions"). Branch `brain-offline`. Real Macrotermes kings are fed mouth to mouth and die without it. New `colony/KingCare` (part of `Colony`, saved as `kingLastFed` and `kingStarvedAway`): the king's hunger clock moved from his body into the brain, so it runs while he is frozen in unloaded land. He wants a nodule a day (the workers' meal interval, my pick as before) and starves after the queen's `starve_ticks` of wanting it (6 days unfed in all). While away the nurse numbers feed him right after the queen (`NestLife.nurse`; the report counts `pieces fed to the king`); unfed, he starves away (`the king starved` in the report) and dies when his land loads, as the queen does. Here, the real nurses feed him as before (`TermiteKingEntity` asks `KingCare`), and unfed he starves here too. A king who joins a queen starts a fresh clock. **Also fixed** (found while building): the termite queen never starved while the player was near (only the ant queen's body checked her clock), but the brain could starve her while away and nothing then killed her, leaving a living queen the brain counted dead and who laid no more; `TermiteQueenEntity` now checks the same clock. Both termite royals only get hungry once the colony has had its first comb (`QueenCare.royalsGetHungry`: hunger on, not founding, and `hungry_from_first_food` met; real first workers feed the royal pair on what they forage before there is a garden; my pick, to confirm). The ant queen is unchanged. Old saves: the king's clock starts fresh. `macrotermes.json` note updated (the queen's `starve_ticks` is used by termites now). Game tests (category `away`): `aTermiteColonyAwayAgesItsCombFeedsItsQueenAndEatsAsNumbers` now has a king, a day and more unfed, whom the nurse numbers feed while away; his clock survives the save and load, and he is alive back home; new `aStarvingTermitePairAwayDieWhenTheirLandLoads` (no nodule at all: the queen and the king starve while away, the workers live, both die as their land loads, the colony carries on queenless). The termite scene now goes away 20 ticks later (the king has to join the queen first), so its later checks moved 20 ticks too (with them where they were, the queen fell short of her next egg: the test's timing, not a bug). 109 game tests now; `away` with the king feeding test (12 of 12) and `colony` with `termitefood` (30 of 30) pass. Hard to see in-game (a king needs 6 days unfed); `/antcolonies list` shows `pieces fed to the king` after time away.
- **Brain part 4, step 4a: lasting trunk trails** (user's plan 2026-10-15; decisions and my picks in `docs/DESIGN.md` "Brain part 4 decisions"). Branch `brain-offline`. Real *Pogonomyrmex barbatus* keep a few cleared paths straight out from the nest for years; the foragers walk out along one and fan out at its end. New `colony/TrunkTrails` (part of `Colony`, saved as `trunkTrails`; any species whose profile has `food.trails.trunk`, only the harvester ant now): a trail is a straight line of blocks from the hill's edge (`start` 4) to the food radius. Every trip counts: a real forager setting out (`ClipGrassGoal` asks `setOut` and walks out along the trail to the block nearest its plant, then goes to the plant; `StoreSeedGoal` walks back onto the trail before going home, `wayHome`) and every piece the forager numbers bring in (`FieldLife`). A trip goes by the trail within `spread_degrees` 35 of its food and wears every block out to its leave point; `new_trail_trips` 8 trips within a day where no trail goes lay a new one, while the colony may keep more (`trailsFor`: 1 young, up to `most` 8 at `full_at_workers` 1,500). A block walked by `bare_after_trips` 60 trips wears bare: its plant is cut (grass tufts through `FoodStore.cutAwayForTheForagers`, their seeds left lying), grass, dirt, podzol and mycelium turn to coarse dirt; only out of every player's sight, or where one of the colony's real workers is within a block (at most 32 blocks a look, every 5 seconds, never in unloaded land). Each trip adds 1 strength (at most 64); unused it halves every `half_life_ticks` 24,000 (a game day); below `fade_below` 1 the trail is forgotten, also while away, and its bare blocks grow back to grass out of sight about `regrow_ticks` 48,000 later (half to one and a half times). `/antcolonies list` shows ` | trunk trails: east 12 blocks (strength 61, 12 bare)`. Termites: `ForagingTrails` keeps a saved remembered trail per way out (`trailMemory`) to where most of its foragers' recent spots lie, 1 stronger per visit, fading by the same numbers (`food.trails` in `macrotermes.json`, no trunk: Macrotermes forage under cover); `remembered()` is the hook for 4b's recruitment; the soldiers' sentry posts still use the 2-minute trails; `/antcolonies list` shows ` | remembered trails: ...`. All numbers are my picks, to confirm. New game test `TrailTests.foragersWearLastingTrunkTrailsThatFadeAndGrowBack` (category `colony`): 7 trips lay no trail, the 8th does, with the leave point by the food; a young colony keeps 1 trail and a 1,500-worker one lays a second from the remembered trips; 60 trips wear the east trail bare out to the food and not past it (grass cut, seeds lying), the south trail waits while a pretend player looks and wears bare once nobody does; saved and loaded the same; a week unused both fade and their ground grows back to grass. New checks in `aWorkerFindsAHayBaleAndTheForagersGoForItRatherThanTheGrass`: a remembered termite trail leads to the hay, is saved, and is forgotten a week unused. 110 game tests now; `colony`, `antnest`, `away`, `termitefood` and `soldiers` 72 of 73 pass, the one failure (`aFallenTreeUpASlopeKeepsItsHoleWhenItsLowestBlockIsEaten`, tunnel not dug in time) is the known big-run clash and passes alone with its class (5 of 5). **Confirmed in-game by the user 2026-10-15** (trails laid, walked and worn bare).
- **Founding fixes: grass in the hole, founders dig while away** (user's bugs, 2026-10-15: "they dont dig down when the chunk isnt loaded" and queens and kings not breaking grass over their hole). Branch `brain-offline`. **Grass:** a founder digs only standing on its hole, and `Nest.findStandSpots` needs air over the block dug, so short grass, tall grass, a flower or a sapling in the entrance cell left the shaft's first block with no place to stand and nobody dug. Now a plant in the entrance (`Nest.isPlantInEntrance`) is the first dig target and `canDig`; `DigGoal.bite` bites it off in one bite (break sound and bits, no pellet, no mud wall; `Nest.clearPlantInEntrance`: a grass tuft goes through `FoodStore.cutAwayForTheForagers`, so its seeds are left lying; confirmed by the user 2026-10-15). **Away:** `BuildLife` skipped founding colonies and the founding eggs wait for the founders to be sealed in, so a founding nest stood still while away. Now `NestLife` works out, while the nest is loaded, what the founders still have to dig (`Nest.foundersDigLeft`: the shaft and founding chamber, or the shaft and first rooms; `BuildLife.bites` 10 ticks each, plus `SETTLE_TICKS` 600 to settle in, confirmed by the user 2026-10-15) and saves it (`foundingWorkLeft`, `foundedAway`); while away the founders work it off at the real pace (the queen as one digger, a king as a second; confirmed by the user 2026-10-15), then count as sealed in (`foundWhileAway`), so the queen lays her founding batch and the young grow while away. On return `BuildLife.settleFounders` digs what is left out of sight (walls first), puts the queen (and the king beside her) in the home chamber, and packs the hole shut while they are still alone (if their first workers are already out, the hole is left open); the builders' time while founding is the founders' only (no soil on the hill). The away report: `the founders dug their chamber and closed themselves in` (`AwayReport.didFoundersSeal`, saved). Game tests (category `away`, no new scene, `foundsWhileAway`): both founders start on short grass; the ant queen is then put 2 blocks off with the grass back in her entrance and must bite it away and seal in here; the termite pair go away at once, before any digging (1,520 ticks of work left), dig and close themselves in while away (the report says so), lay all 5 eggs away, and back home the queen is in her chamber with the king by her and the grass is gone. `away`, `antnest`, `colony`, `termitefood` 64 of 65 pass; the one failure (`aFallenTreeUpASlopeKeepsItsHoleWhenItsLowestBlockIsEaten`) is the known big-run clash (see 4a) and passes alone with its class (5 of 5). **The grass bite confirmed in-game by the user 2026-10-15.** The away part failed the user's test (2026-10-15): the ant queen dug in, sealed and raised her 3 first workers while away, but they then starved as numbers, so back home she sat alone with no eggs (fix below).
- **Fix: a founding ant queen's first workers starved while away** (user's report 2026-10-15, `docs/BUGS.md` 29). Branch `brain-offline`. While away the worker numbers only forage into food store room the brain has seen, and a nest with only its founding chamber has none, so the 3 first workers never foraged and starved after 3 days (the queen then sat alone). Now the brain notes, while the nest is loaded, what is left to dig before the food room is open (`Nest.foodStoreDigLeft`: the entrance plug, the shaft and every level down to the food room; costed as each block's bites plus each bite's soil carried to the hill, `BuildLife.trip`; saved as `storeWorkLeft`, `storeLevel`, `storeCells` in `nestLife`); while away, once the food room's level is unlocked, the builder numbers work it off at the real pace (`NestLife.digStoreAway`; about 28,000 ticks, a bit over a game day, for a young nest's one builder in the test) and the brain then counts the room as an empty room of that size (`FoodStore.storeDugAway`), so the foragers bring seeds in; the log says `the workers dug out the food store while away`. The blocks are dug on return as before (rooms first, then the seeds owed are put down). Only species with a food room (the harvester ant); termites keep their food in the gardens and their workers only get hungry once there is comb, so they never starved this way. Game test `aFoundingAntQueenRaisesHerFirstWorkersWhileAway` (category `away`): grass round the nest; after the first workers grow up the colony stays away past their starve time (72,000 ticks plus 12,000): nobody starves, the store gets dug, food is brought in, then the workers come home as before (timeout 160,000 ticks). Shown to fail without the fix (`3 starved`). `away` and `antnest` pass (34 of 34). **Confirmed in-game by the user 2026-10-15** ("everything works now").
- **A lone founding queen lays one more batch, then starves** (user's decision 2026-10-15, while the founding fix was tested). Branch `brain-offline`. Real claustral queens whose first workers all die can lay a small second batch from what is left of their fat, and die if that fails too. Species file: `founding.second_batch_eggs` (2 for both species, my pick, to confirm; 0 means none). `QueenCare`: once the founding batch is all laid, the colony has no workers and no brood left, the queen begins a second batch (`startSecondBatch`, saved as `secondBatch`; the log says `her first workers are all dead; she lays a second batch`, the away report counts `second founding batches begun`), here or while away alike; once that batch is gone too she is spent (`isSpent`) and starves like an unfed queen (her hunger clock runs out). Game test `aLoneQueenFoundsHerColonyAndRaisesHerFirstWorkers` (category `antnest`, same scene, timeout 180,000): after her first 3 workers are out they are killed: she lays 2 more eggs, raises them, they are killed too, she is spent and starves, and the colony is gone. Passes (the test turns the colony's hunger back on for the starving part, as the nest tests run with it off).
- **Brain step 4b, part 1: the harvester ants' daily clock** (user's decisions 2026-10-15: the midday rest goes by the biome's heat; never at night or in the rain). Branch `brain-offline`. Real *Pogonomyrmex* forage in the morning, stop when the ground gets too hot, go out again in the afternoon and stay in at night and in the rain. Species file: `food.day_clock` (`SpeciesProfile.DayClock`): out from `out_from` (0, sunrise at 6:00) to `in_by` (11000, 17:00), except a midday rest centred on `rest_middle` (7000, 13:00) whose length goes by the biome's base temperature: none at `rest_from_heat` (0.5) or cooler, `rest_most` (4000 ticks, 4 hours: 11:00 to 15:00) at `rest_full_heat` (2.0) or hotter, in proportion between (plains 0.8: about 50 minutes, 12:36 to 13:24; badlands, desert and savanna 2.0: 4 hours); `not_in_rain`: in while rain falls on the nest (a dry biome such as the badlands gets none). All times are my picks, to confirm. `Colony.isForagingTime` reads the clock when the species has one (termites keep `forage_at`), so the real foragers (`ClipGrassGoal`, `GatherSeedGoal`) and the worker numbers (`FieldLife`, here and away) keep it. Only the foragers keep it so far: diggers and refuse carriers still go out at any hour. `/antcolonies list` shows it: `foraging now (it is 9:12; out 6:00 to 17:00, resting 12:36 to 13:24 (biome heat 0.8), never in rain)`. The game test server's sun keeps moving, so there every day clock reads 7:00 unless a test sets its own (`Colony.setTimeOnTestServer`, `setClockForTests`). Tests: unit test `theHarvesterAntForagesByDayAndRestsAtMiddayByTheBiomesHeat`; game test `antNumbersForageOnlyOutOfSightAndFromWhatTheyRemember` (category `away`, same scene): away, nothing is foraged at midnight nor at 7:00 in the rain; on a dry morning in a hot biome the tufts are eaten up. `antnest`, `away` and `colony` pass (34 of 34, with the lone queen). Waiting for the user to try it in-game.
- **Spare harvester ants rest inside by the entrance** (user's report 2026-10-15: back at a young colony the ants "just wander around and do nothing for a while", strolling on the surface, then went back to work after a couple of minutes; user's decision: the real behaviour). Branch `brain-offline`. The cause: a 3 or 4 worker colony whose nest is dug as far as its size allows (the next level needs 6 workers and 10 seeds), with no young to tend and the foragers between rounds, has no work at all for a while (workers already help with any job after 5 seconds with nothing to do, `AbstractAntEntity.isIdle`), and an idle worker strolled about wherever it was, outside too. Real *Pogonomyrmex* keep their spare workers inside, in the upper chambers by the entrance, ready to go out; only foragers, patrollers, nest maintenance and midden workers are out on the surface. Now, for a species with `worker.rest_near_entrance` (the harvester: 8 blocks, my pick, to confirm; 0 keeps the old strolling, termites unchanged), a worker with nothing to do goes inside (`GoInsideGoal`, now also given to ant workers, priority 10; it waits 200 ticks before looking again after finding no way in, so a colony with no nest dug yet does not search for a way in all the time) and strolls only inside the nest, within 8 blocks of the entrance or, deeper down, a little nearer to it each time (`AntWanderGoal.restsAt`, `AbstractAntEntity.restsNearEntrance`). Game test `theNestGrowsDeeperOnlyAsTheColonyGrows` (category `antnest`, same scene): once the first level is dug and nothing is left to do, every worker must be inside within 8,000 ticks and stay in. `antnest`, `away` and `colony` pass (46 of 46). Waiting for the user to try it in-game.
- **Brain step 4b, parts 2 and 3: Gordon's door rule and the patrollers** (planned with the user 2026-10-15, decisions in `docs/DESIGN.md` "4b parts 2 and 3"; branch `brain-offline`). Real *Pogonomyrmex barbatus* (Deborah Gordon's work): **patrollers** go out first each time the day clock opens (dawn, after the midday rest, after rain), over and round the hill and out along the first part of the trails; **foraging starts only once one is back, and only the ways they came back from**; then **no ant is in charge of how many forage**: the foragers wait inside the entrance, and a homecomer with a seed (or a patroller back) touches them; one touched often enough in a short time goes out, so quick returns bring more out and scarce seeds let the outflow die down. (1) **`colony/Patrol`** (saved; part of `Colony`, ticked once a second here and away): each round's patrol is `food.patrol.share` of the workers (0.16, the user's scaling: 2 at 10, 4 at 25, 8 at 50, 16 at 100; at least 1), later rounds half; the ways are the trunk trails, strongest first, then new compass points where the colony knows the most food; real workers patrol as far as they go (builders first, then foragers, then nurses; one busy with something else for 20 seconds is passed over and another goes), the rest as numbers (back after the walk's time; always safely while away, until step 4g). `isOpen(level, pos)`: round the hill (6 blocks; a small colony: every direction within the patrol's reach, 10 blocks, my pick so a young colony is not slowed) and within the trails' spread (35 degrees) of a way a patroller came back from; never a way shut for today. A patroller hurt, with a hostile mob within 4 blocks, or killed: its way is shut for the day, as many again patrol the hill, and only half the foragers may be out the rest of the day; another colony's ants met: that way shut. A day whose patrols never came back has no foraging (the away report says so). (2) **`entity/goal/PatrolGoal`** (priority 6, the mind; legs only through `AntNavigator`): 4 points round the hill, out along its way to its reach and home to the entrance, where it stays 5 seconds touching the waiting foragers; it looks for trouble every half second and turns home at once; stuck for 3 minutes, it counts as home. (3) **`colony/Door`**: real foragers count their own touches (`food.door`: 3 within 600 ticks; under 10 foragers 1, and a forager's own seed counts, the user's early-game rule); `ClipGrassGoal`, `GatherSeedGoal` and `ScoutGoal` ask `door.mayGo` and only go for food the patrol has opened (`FoodStore.findGrass`/`findSeed` filter); a homecomer with a seed touches the waiting ants within 2 blocks and its 3 nearest by the entrance (about 10 crowd the entrance chamber, `Door.AT_THE_DOOR`, my pick); the forager numbers (`FieldLife`) go by the same rule worked out each second (`Door.step`: the touches of the last 30 seconds on the ones by the door, a Poisson guess), and their homecomings touch the real ants waiting by the entrance too. (4) **Surface hours for all workers:** `AbstractAntEntity.mayGoOut` now keeps the harvester ants' day clock for every colony worker (not the queen), so builders mending the hill and refuse carriers stay in at night, in the rain and in the midday rest. **Night soil:** soil dug while the surface is shut goes on a heap just under the entrance (`Hill.addNightSoil`, a count of pellets, at most 512, saved; not visible) and `CarryNightSoilGoal` (builders, priority 8) carries it up onto the hill once the clock opens. (5) **`/antcolonies list`** adds ` | patrol: 8 out at 6:00, 7 back, 1 lost; open: north, east; shut today: west (danger)`, ` | foragers out 14 of 40 (12 back with seeds in the last minute)` and the night soil waiting; the away report adds `patrols sent`, `patrollers lost`, `days with no foraging`. (6) Tests: unit tests `DoorRuleTest` (quick returns grow the outflow, none or sparse returns let it die down, empty returns don't count, a small colony goes at one touch, after danger half, the patroller counts); game-test checks in existing scenes: `workersCarrySeedsAndClipGrassIntoTheFoodStore` (no forager before the first patroller is back; the first forager out within a minute of it), `antNumbersForageOnlyOutOfSightAndFromWhatTheyRemember` (away, the numbers patrol first, nothing foraged before one is back, the report counts the patrol), `foragersWearLastingTrunkTrailsThatFadeAndGrowBack` (the patrol walks the two trails first; open once back; a killed patroller shuts its trail for the day only; fewer foragers after it; saved and loaded), `workersDigTheWholeNestAndBuildTheMound` (an hour at midnight mid-dig: no worker outside, the soil heaped under the entrance, carried up in the morning). Patrols and the door are off on the game test server unless a test turns them on (`Patrol.setOnForTests`). My picks, to confirm: everything marked so above and in `docs/DESIGN.md`.
- **Ways for Claude to see animations (2026-10-15, the user's request: "as many ways that you can see what your making before doing any of the animations"):** built before any new animation, nothing changes in the game itself. (1) **The animation lab** (`tools/anim_lab.py`, species-neutral, reads the game's own geo, texture and animation files): film strips from the side, front, above and three-quarter with a treadmill floor that moves at the walk's speed and each foot's path drawn on it; graphs of every bone's turn, the footfall (gait) diagram and foot heights; automatic checks (feet sliding, fewer than 3 feet down, parts in the floor, legs through the body, loop seams, snaps) with the real-ant numbers (stride, duty factor, steps a second at the game's walking speed); `compare` (clips side by side), `sheet` (contact sheet of screenshots) and `match` (each in-game picture above the lab's drawing of the same clip at the same moment). Pictures go to `references/anim_lab/` (not in git). (2) **The pose viewer** (`tools/ant_pose_viewer.py`, `references/ant_pose_viewer.html`) upgraded: shadows, foot trails, pause, frame steps, 1/4 and 1/10 speed, walking across the floor, camera buttons (sides, front, back, above, low at the feet), and `window.lab.set(...)` so Claude can drive it in its browser. (3) **The in-game camera** (branch `anim-camera`; `client/FilmDirector`, `./gradlew runFilm`, shot lists in `tools/film/*.film`, development only): opens a fresh flat creative world, puts one insect at a time on the floor playing one clip (an insect named `film:<clip>` plays only that clip), takes real game screenshots from the side and front (level, narrow lens, like the lab's drawings), above and three-quarter, and bursts of frames 1 game tick apart (the game frozen and stepped), writes each picture's exact clip moment to `run-film/screenshots/times.txt`, then quits. Found while building it: a frozen game's in-between-tick time jitters and stopped GeckoLib's clips (the camera now draws every picture exactly on a tick and starts each clip while the game runs). **Checked:** for all six worker clips the game and the lab agree moment by moment from every side, so the lab shows what the game plays. **Found in today's animations (recorded, not fixed; the user said tools first):** the walk's feet slide (2 to 3 times the walk speed: eased keyframes stop the foot mid-stance and the swing about the hip moves it sideways), the stride is short (0.30 body lengths; at the game's 2.75 blocks a second the walk would need about 18 cycles a second), `leg_l1` touches the head at the start of the walk, and `walk_hot` has its middle legs never on the ground (sometimes no foot down at all). These go into step 1 of the animation plan.
- **Confirmed in-game by the user (2026-10-16):** the virtual population (brain part 1, `/antcolonies population`), the away report, the numbers foraging while away (step 2c), building on return (step 2d), the termite king fed while away and starving unfed (`KingCare`), the lone founding queen's second batch, spare harvester ants resting inside by the entrance, and the 4b door rule and patrollers. (The numbers marked "my pick, to confirm" are not covered by this.)

- **Brain step 4c part 1, searching alone with site fidelity (built 2026-10-16, branch `brain-offline`; plan `docs/plans/4c-searching.md`).** Real red harvester ants do not tell nestmates of seeds: each forager walks out a trunk trail, searches alone in a meandering walk until it finds food, and goes back trip after trip to where it last found some (Hölldobler 1976, Gordon). Now: a harvester worker only notices a tuft or seed within `food.find_radius` (3 blocks) and what it finds stays its own (never told to the colony). `colony/ForagerSpots` keeps each forager's **spot** (where it last cut or picked up food; 2 empty trips there and it is dropped) and, after a search that came home empty, its **lead** (the same way again, from as far out as that search drifted; dropped once it reached the range plus the stretch). `ScoutGoal` (harvester ants): own spot first, else its lead, else out along a trunk trail by strength (1 search in 5 a new way; a **small colony's searchers always take a new way**, as young colonies have no lasting trunk trails yet), then hops of up to 4 blocks round the search centre; every minute without a find the centre moves 3 blocks farther out (`drift`), at most 8 blocks past the foraging range (the user's decisions: search until found; the range stretches from the ants' own failures); it comes home empty only when foraging time ends or after 5 minutes. The numbers do the same in `FieldLife.forageAlone`: holders of a spot work the remembered plants near it, the rest search (a band that grows a block with each empty search and settles back with each find); spots move between real ants and numbers with the ant (`intoNumbers`/`outOfNumbers`, never doubled or lost) and are saved. `Door.LONGEST_TRIP_TICKS` 6000 to 9000 (a search can take 5 minutes). `/antcolonies list` shows `searching N, spots M (K held by numbers), E home empty`. Numbers in `red_harvester_ant.json` `food.search` (my picks, to confirm). Termites keep scouting and telling. Tests: `SiteFidelityTest` (5 unit tests); the seed test also checks that an ant goes back to the tuft it last cut from (site fidelity); the queen's egg test now accepts the egg having hatched into a larva in the nursery (it hatches while the foragers are out searching); the numbers' away foraging test waits 1000 ticks longer (searching takes time); the nest-growth test's "nothing to do" check skips searchers (the door rule that holds them in is off in game tests). Bugs found by the tests and fixed: a lone searcher forgot the grass it found when it stepped inside (it now remembers its own finds, `FoodMap.tick`); a searcher that found food was walked home by `GoInsideGoal` before cutting or gathering took over (it now stands by its find up to 5 seconds, and `ClipGrassGoal` is priority 6, above searching). **Seed test fixed (2026-10-16):** it failed now and then because the game tests' clock stands still in the morning, so there is only one foraging round: once every searcher came home empty, the door rule (no touches from homecomers with food) kept everyone in for good, where in the game the next round (after the midday rest, or the next morning) starts foraging again. The test now starts the next round once nobody has been out for 3000 ticks (`NEXT_ROUND_AFTER`); it passed 5 runs out of 5. Its debug lines (`[seed-test-dbg]`, tufts, the north pile, loose items) stay for now. Found while looking: a stray wheat seed 30+ blocks from the nest (outside the food range) lay in one run; where it came from is not known yet. **Open:** a searcher's next search after an empty one starts as far out as the last drifted, so ground near the nest can go unsearched for long (seen in one run: a last seed found only after about 30000 ticks while 1 to 4 ants searched); possible fix (to ask the user): each search starts near the nest and drifts outward from there. The 4 categories and the full run are owed. Not yet tested in-game.

- **Fix: the morning clear-out of night soil** (user's bug 2026-10-16: once the workers dug the food store they stopped building the hill and walked back and forth "stuck on the same block"; the user picked option A; `docs/BUGS.md` 30). Branch `brain-offline`. The night soil was a hidden count under the entrance that grew without end (181 and 48 pellets in the user's two colonies, a hot biome with the surface shut about 71% of the day). Now, like real *Pogonomyrmex*, whose nest maintenance workers are the first out each morning carrying out the soil dug in the night: (1) **visible piles**: the new block **Loose Soil** (`ModBlocks.LOOSE_SOIL`, a layered block with no collision, coarse dirt look, no item) lies in up to 4 layers a cell on the floors near the top of the nest (`Nest.nightSoilCells`: the way into the top chamber, and the founding chamber once the queen has moved down; never on brood, never on top of a block still to be dug); (2) **`colony/NightSoil`** (part of `Hill`): the piles in the world are the count; at most `per_builder` (12) pellets a builder may wait (`Colony.buildersNow`), never more than the cells hold; a digger's pellet goes on the smallest pile (`CarryPelletGoal`); an older save's heap is laid out as piles (the rest packed into the walls); once the hill is finished the piles are packed into the walls (every 20 seconds); (3) **carry first**: `CarryNightSoilGoal` claims a pile (one carrier to a pile, no fixed cap of 4 any more) and `DigGoal.soilWaits` keeps a builder from digging while a free pile waits and the surface is open; (4) **rest**: while the surface is shut (or the entrance plugged) the diggers rest once the piles are full (the plug and a plant in the entrance are always bitten); (5) **away**: the builder numbers carry the piles up before they dig on return (`BuildLife.carryNightSoil`). Species file: `nest.night_soil` (`layers_per_cell` 4, `per_builder` 12; my picks, to confirm). `/antcolonies list` shows `night soil in piles: n of at most m pellets`. Test: new checks in `workersDigTheWholeNestAndBuildTheMound` (a 3000-tick midnight: loose soil only on the night soil cells and never more than they hold; once full no more than 3 cells dug till morning; all carried up within 3000 ticks of morning; in the run: full at 12 pellets with one builder, carried up 2279 ticks after morning). Found by the new checks: a pile on a stair still to be dug left its diggers no place to stand, and a full hill left the piles there for good (both fixed above). Test runs: `antnest,away` (34 tests) passed but for the dig test (fixed, then passes alone); the rerun after the fix had 3 foraging tests fail side by side (`workersCarrySeedsAndClipGrassIntoTheFoodStore`, `anAntColonyAwayLivesOnThroughASaveAndLoadAndCatchesUpOnReturn`, `antNumbersForageOnlyOutOfSightAndFromWhatTheyRemember`); all three pass alone, and the last fails and passes the same way on the code before this fix: neighbours, not this change. **Confirmed in-game by the user 2026-10-17.**

- **Fix: builders falsely idle and drafted onto long food searches** (the flaky hill test on a step, `HillTests.antHillOnAStepKeepsItsRoundShape`, failing about 3 runs in 4 alone; `docs/BUGS.md` 31). Found on branch `claude/elegant-shannon-db1086` (from `brain-offline`). The hill was built every time; the nest was not dug out in time because the 1 or 2 builders kept going out to search for food: the random "look for work one time in 20" gate left a builder with blocks still to dig looking idle for 5 seconds in about 1 pause in 13, and an idle worker helps the foragers, whose lone searches (brain step 4c) last up to 5 minutes. Now an ant looks for its own work at every chance in its last 2 seconds before it would count as idle (`AbstractAntEntity.looksForWork`, in place of the random gate in the 14 goals that look for a job's work: digging, sealing, plugging, the hill, night soil, corpses, the mound, eggs, larvae, the queen, bread, grass, seeds, searching), so only a worker whose own job has nothing for it helps elsewhere. Ants and termites alike; no change to what the jobs do. The test's message now says whether the hill or the digging is unfinished. Tests: the hill test passes 6 runs in a row alone, the nest dug by tick 14000 to 24000 (before: 5 of 6 runs failed, the passing ones at tick 43000 to 55000 of the 58000 allowed); `./gradlew build` and the 52 tests of `hills`, `antnest`, `colony` and `away` pass. The termite categories were not run for it (termite workers use the same rule).
- **A shared test clock for the game tests (roadmap 3a part 1, branch `claude/test-clock-88c551`; plan `docs/plans/test-clock.md`; user's decisions 2026-10-16: real speed, a moving clock only for the time-of-day tests, real nights).** New `gametest/TestClock`: each test that cares about the time has its own clock, found by where a colony is (so a colony founded during the test, or a "saved and loaded" copy, reads it too). `TestClock.moving(helper, hour)` runs at real speed (a day is 24,000 ticks), `TestClock.still(helper, hour)` holds one hour; `skipTo(hour)` and `skipTo(colony, phase)` only move forward, like sleeping (game time, hunger and ages do not jump); phases come from the species file (`MORNING`, `MIDDAY_REST`, `AFTERNOON`, `EVENING`, `NIGHT` for a day-clock species; `DAY`, `NIGHT`, `BEFORE_DAWN` for termites). A test with no clock reads a still 7:00, termites too (they used to get a random day or night from the shared test sun). In `Colony` one hook (`setTestTime`, installed by `TestFilter`) replaces `setClockForTests`, `setForagingTimeForTests`, `setSkyForTests` and `setTimeOnTestServer`; kept: `setRainForTests` and `setHeatForTests` (the midday rest's length). `Patrol.newRoundForTests` is gone too. A player's game reads exactly what it read before. Every test was moved (no check deleted): the seed test runs on a moving clock from 5:30 (the patrol goes out at the real 6:00, the next rounds come after the real midday rest and the next mornings), so its `NEXT_ROUND_AFTER` round-starter and its `[seed-test-dbg]` lines are gone (timeout 140000; it passed twice, finishing on day 2 and day 3); the away tests skip forward (midnight, the next 7:00 in the rain, 8:00 in a hot biome; a termite night and a rainy day); the trail test needs no forced round; the termite holes, new hole, runway, dead wood from below, sentry and cathedral tests walk real nights and days; the rest get a still midday or midnight. Checks reshaped for real days (timeouts raised): the runway test (70000) counts building time by night and eating time by day, its crumble limit 16000 → 30000 ticks; the new-hole hay test (60000) splits "spent within 6000" from "plugged by sunrise" and checks the next real night; the sentry test (timeout 12000) runs a real night from 20:00, the minors must stand guard before 4:00, and once they have (a forager at the hay) the clock skips on to 4:00, so the night ends with a forager out (on a real night the five foragers were sometimes all home by 4:00, leaving "the minors go in last" unchecked); dead wood from below 90000. Found by the real clock: a hay bale (48 pieces) is eaten in one night by 500 termites, and once a tunnel reaches it, by day from inside: the runway and hay tests now keep the bale from running out (`setPiecesCutForTests`) until their checks are done, standing for a bigger stack of hay. Also found: `theFoundingBatchGrowsIntoWorkersWhoOpenTheEntrance` had no clock and counted on the shared test sun bringing a night (termites open their entrance only at night); it now has a moving clock from 18:30. The seed test's final count missed the bread fed to a larva that grew into a worker over the real days; it now remembers what each larva was fed. The dig test's night (another session is reworking it, night soil piles) only swaps its frozen hour for `TestClock.still` at midnight and at 7:00. Seen once and not again: `aFallenTreeUpASlopeKeepsItsHoleWhenItsLowestBlockIsEaten` failed in one category run (tunnel dug, hole not opened in 28000 ticks at a still midnight) and passed alone and in the next category run; watch it. Still open from 4c: searches after an empty one start too far out (the seed test's north pile took until day 3). **Full run (2026-10-16, 110 tests): 41 minutes** (was 16 for 104: the real days and nights make the time-of-day tests longer), 107 passed. The 3 failures: `foragersWearLastingTrunkTrailsThatFadeAndGrowBack` checked 15 ticks after sending the patrollers home, but colonies think only every 20 ticks (an old flake, about 1 run in 4; now 25 ticks); `richBreadStartsABurstOfEggs` timed out in the full run only and passed alone twice (no clock change touches it: it reads the still 7:00 as before); `antHillOnAStepKeepsItsRoundShape` fails about 1 run in 4 alone too, **on the code before the clock as well** (a hill block 1 high where 2 were planned; open, not looked into).

- **Test health, part 1: tests side by side, kept apart, and on GitHub** (the user's problem 2026-10-16: every feature cost about 40 minutes of game tests, and tests broke when a feature landed; plan `docs/plans/test-health.md`, the order picked by the user). Branch `brain-offline`. (1) **The 5 failures of the quick run** all passed alone; the cause was three leaks between tests (`docs/BUGS.md` 32), fixed in `gametest/TestIsolation`: each colony rolls its own dice (`Colony.random()`, in place of the world's shared `level.getRandom()` in 37 places of the colony code and `FeedLarvaGoal`; no change for a player, the dice are as random), a test's colonies and all their insects go when the test is done, and the jobs test counts the colony's own workers wherever they are; the queen test counts the larva's bread with the queen's. (2) **Tests side by side**: `tools/run_game_tests.sh [n]` starts n test servers at once from one Gradle call (Gradle runs tasks side by side with the configuration cache on), each in its own folder `run-test-shards/<i>/` with its share of the tests (`TestFilter.keepShard`, system property `antcolonies.shard`; tests with a batch of their own dealt out first), and lists every failure at the end; `ANTCOLONIES_TESTS` still picks categories. Run configs `gameTestShard1..n` in `build.gradle` (`-PtestShards=n`, 5 if not given). The quick run of `antnest,away,colony,hills` (52 tests): 42 minutes and 5 failures before, **9 minutes, all passed** after, on 5 servers. (3) **On GitHub**: `.github/workflows/game-tests.yml` runs every game test on 4 GitHub computers at once, on a push to `master`, on a push whose commit message has `[full run]`, or by hand (Actions tab, "Game tests", "Run workflow"); failures are listed on the run's page, logs kept 7 days. Also fixed: `gradlew` was not marked runnable in git, so the old "Build" check could not run on GitHub's Linux computers.

- **Fix: harvester ant trunk trails faded a second after being laid (2026-10-16, branch `trail-fade` from `brain-offline`, merged into `brain-offline` the same day; `docs/BUGS.md` 33).** Seen in the user's live game log (colony 26d15ad1: "laid a new trunk trail toward north", then "the trunk trail toward north faded" a third of a second later, again and again in every direction). A new trail started at strength 0 and the trip that laid it added 1, so it sat exactly at `fade_below` (1.0); the colony's next once-a-second look found it a hair below (it had decayed for 20 ticks) and forgot it. Since step 4a the bug was there, but the 4a test sends all its trips in one tick, and on 15 Oct (before the door rule) foragers probably went out many at a time, so a second trip came before the look; with Gordon's door rule (4b) and lone searchers (4c) trips come one by one, so no second trip came in time. Fixed: a new trail is as strong as the trips that laid it (`new_trail_trips`, 8; `TrunkTrails.maybeLayNewTrail`), so unused it lasts about 3 days (half-life 1 day) as the profile intends. Test: new check in `TrailTests.foragersWearLastingTrunkTrailsThatFadeAndGrowBack` (category `colony`): the east trail laid by 8 trips is still there and stronger than 7 after a minute unused and the colony's next look; it failed before the fix. The same test's patrol check waited only 15 ticks after bringing the patrol home, less than the patrol's once-a-second look, so it passed only by the luck of the tick timing (moving the steps by 25 ticks made it fail); it now waits 25 ticks. All 12 `colony` tests pass. Not yet tested in-game. **Confirmed in-game by the user 2026-10-17.**

- **Everything merged into `master` (2026-10-16, user's OK: "merge everything").** `trail-fade` into `brain-offline` (the trail test's two fixes of one flaky check merged: `trail-fade`'s timings kept; colony and away: 23 of 23 pass), then `brain-offline` into `master` (a fast-forward), then `anim-engine` into `master` (`build.gradle` keeps both the test shard runs and the film run; the animation bugs renumbered 34 to 36 in `docs/BUGS.md`; notes keep both sides). First full run on `master`: 108 of 111 passed in 16 minutes on 8 servers. Two tests were too strict (`docs/BUGS.md` 37, fixed in the tests: the noticing test's players keep beside their insects until they notice, 10 of 10; the refuse test kills the worker nearest the nest); one is quarantined (the fallen tree up a slope, about 2 runs in 10 beside other tests, a gallery block that stops being dug; `docs/BUGS.md`, "Open, quarantined"; its progress line now logs the gallery, the next dig target and the workers).

- **Second full run on `master` and the test clock's sunset (2026-10-16).** Full run after the two test fixes: 109 of 111 in 12 minutes; the noticing and refuse tests passed. New failure: the stump test's "foraging time" at dusk, the test clock's sunset 9 ticks later than the game's (`docs/BUGS.md` 38): fixed, sunset and sunrise now come from `Colony.isSunUp`. Full run with the fix: 110 of 111 in 13 minutes (the quarantined fallen tree test passed). The one failure, `queenLivesInTheFoundingChamberUntilHerOwnRoomIsDug` ("no egg has been put down in the nursery yet"), is the **open 4c search problem**, not the clock: 12 of the 30 seeds lay 6 blocks from the nest from tick 13000 to the end (90000), so the queen stayed at 18 of her 20 bread. It passed 4 of 4 alone, but slowly: in two runs the last 5 seeds lay 6 blocks from the nest for 20000 to 50000 ticks while up to 5 searchers came home empty (up to 20 empty trips), because a search after an empty one starts as far out as the last one drifted. The test now logs the jobs, the searchers, the door, the patrol and every seed lying about every 4000 ticks. The fix (each search starts near the nest and drifts outward) is the user's decision.

- **Searches start near the nest (brain step 4c fix, user's OK 2026-10-16: "it must not break anything or slow the ants down").** The open 4c problem: a searcher that came home empty searched the same way next time from as far out as it had drifted, so ground near the nest went unsearched (the queen's founding chamber test failed in a full run: 12 seeds 6 blocks from the nest lay from tick 13,000 to 90,000). Now (`ScoutGoal.planSearch`, `ForagerSpots.cameHomeEmpty`): every new way's search starts just past the hill, 6 to 8 blocks from the nest's middle (`NEAR_START`, was anywhere in the inner half of the range), and drifts outward as before; after an empty trip the ant tries another way, at least 60 degrees off the one that failed (`ANOTHER_WAY`, my pick); the lead keeps only the failed way's angle (saves no longer hold how far out). Searches at an ant's own spot or along a trunk trail are unchanged. Measured: the queen test cleared its last seed at 15,600 to 74,000 ticks before (and failed past 90,000 once), at 29,600 to 38,400 after (10 runs, all passed); the seed test had every seed in at tick 94,400 before (one full run), at 23,400, 38,200 and 63,200 after (3 runs): faster. Unit test `SiteFidelityTest` changed to the new rule. `antnest,colony,away`: 46 of 47 (the hunger test failed once, then passed 7 of 7; open, `docs/BUGS.md`). The queen test logs each worker's place, search and goals every 4,000 ticks. Seen while looking: in the 25-block test arenas a search drifting out ends at the arena's walls for the rest of its 5 minutes (in a real world there is ground beyond). Not tried in-game yet. The full run is owed (the `AntNavigator` clean-up's full run, after both are on `master`). **Confirmed in-game by the user 2026-10-17.**

- **The legs tidied (`AntNavigator` clean-up, roadmap 3a part 2; plan `docs/plans/navigator-tidy.md`, approved by the user 2026-10-16; branch `claude/navigator-tidy-5f5fee`).** No change in how ants and termites walk. The 200-line block that moves an insect one tick along a step is split into one small method per move (`walk`, `fall`, `ontoWall`, `offWall`, `climb`, `turnCorner`, `roundEdge`, `overTop`, `overEdge`, `ontoCeiling`, `offCeiling`, `crawlCeiling`, `underEdge`, `outFromUnder`; the code moved as it was); the four ways a mind starts a walk share one `begin`; leftovers cleared (unused parameters, the unused `toBody`, one `describe` for the stuck and blocked log lines, named distances `OFF_THE_ROUTE`, `HOLD_REACH`, `STILL_ON_IT`); `isWalkingADoorway` renamed `isMovingExactly` (it is also true in tunnels); the file sorted into 11 labelled sections (a token-by-token check showed nothing else changed). Looked for "deciding what to do" in the legs: none. **One real fix (the user's OK):** `follow` (walk a route a mind planned: trails, marching columns later) forgot the route when the insect had to plan again and walked straight to its end; now it gets back onto it where it left it (or, if that spot can't be reached, onto a later step) and goes on along it (`shared`, `sharedFrom`, `sharedOffset`). Nothing in the game uses `follow` yet. New check in `antToldToGoToItsOwnCellSurvivesBeingMovedAway` (a route round three sides of a square, the ant moved far off half way), shown to fail on the old behaviour. `docs/BUGS.md`: where lessons 2 to 5b live in today's code. For the quarantined fallen tree test: `DigGoal` logs a give-up (`[dig] ... found no way` / `gave up ... out of reach`), the test's progress line logs the next dig block's stand spots and each worker's role and running goals. Tests: `legs` 21 of 21; full run (merged with the 4c search fix, c776b82) **111 of 111 in 20 minutes on 4 servers** (the user was gaming); the fallen tree test passed, so its cause is still open. Seen in the full run: one insect (id 104, in a harvester nest test) could not plan a way to its dig blocks 22 blocks off and asked again about 7 times a second for a minute (1,840 `[dig] ... found no way` lines), without moving; followed up the same day: a termite worker walled in in the far-hay test, not a harvester test (`docs/BUGS.md` 42, the fix below).
- **Fix: walled in alive, the king digging for ever, and the dig loop** (2026-10-16, branch `claude/heuristic-moser-d1235c`, from `claude/navigator-tidy-5f5fee`; the user's OK to all three; `docs/BUGS.md` 42). The insect seen in the tidy-up's full run asking for a route 7 times a second was a termite worker walled into a spent feeding gallery (far-hay test), and a second one was the king in the user's-mound test. (1) Seals wait: `NestWalls.plugLeak` never closes a hole while one of the colony's insects is in a small closed pocket behind it (`shutsInANestmate`, 32 cells; real termites backfill a gallery they give up, not over a nestmate). (2) Royals dig only while founding (`QueenToRoomGoal.mayDig`, harvester queen, termite queen and king). (3) `DigGoal` leaves a block it found no way to alone for 20 seconds (`UNREACHABLE_TICKS` 400, my pick, as the other goals). Checks added to `aBigColonyDigsATunnelToFarHayAndPlugsItsHoleWhenTheHayIsGone` (a worker kept in the gallery for 30 s when the hay goes) and `termitesFinishTheUsersHalfBuiltStageFourteen` (no royal digging once there are workers). `workersDigTheWholeNestAndBuildTheMound` now lets the queen dig the shaft and founding chamber alone before her 6 workers come, as in a real founding (with the queen no longer digging beside the workers, their soil filled the hill before the test's night, so no night soil piled up).
- **Merged into `master` (2026-10-17, the user's OK):** branch `claude/range-and-danger-cc35f9` with grama grass, long trails steps 1 to 4, the seed balance, the stuck-insect fix (`docs/BUGS.md` 42), the freeze fix (43) and the dropped-seed fix (44). The user tried it all in one world and said "everything works now" (2026-10-17). Its last full run: 111 of 113 (the two known termite flakes, both passing alone).
- **Termites underground, step 0: the safety net** (2026-10-17, branch `termites-underground` from `master`, plan `docs/plans/termites-underground.md`; the user asked for steps that lower the risk and for tests as strong as the task is complex). No new behaviour in the game. (1) **The gallery proof** (`GalleryProofTest`, unit test): 400 made-up landscapes (rolling hills, terraces of 3, stone, ponds, caves); tunnels to food 12 to 50 blocks out and the nest's own tunnels in 4 directions are planned by `TunnelRoute`, dug into the landscape and checked: never open to the sky, and walked out of the hole and back by the real route finder with the real search budget. It found a real bug at once (`docs/BUGS.md` 45, fixed: `TunnelRoute.isWalledIn`; the only change in the game: tunnels go round, or stop short of, the edge of a drop). (2) **The stuck watcher** (`gametest/StuckWatcher`, its rule unit tested in `StuckWatcherTest`, shown to catch a stuck insect): fails a game test when an insect walking a route under the ground has not moved 0.3 of a block in 20 seconds; on in every `TermiteNewHoleTests` scene. (3) **The long walk** (`foragersWalkALongGalleryOutAndBack`): a ready-made 37-block tunnel to hay 38 blocks out, 16 workers; a trip counts only when a worker reaches the far end (the tunnel's last blocks, the hole, or the feeding gallery under the hay: the hay is eaten from below, so few use the hole), is then seen in the middle of the tunnel and gets back into the middle of the nest, within 2,400 ticks (catches a worker going round in circles); 3 trips wanted; the watcher must have seen insects walking underground. Numbers (2,400 ticks, 20 seconds) are my picks. (4) The watcher is on in the fungus garden scene too, and a stuck insect's failure lists everything at its spot (its position, its legs' state, the blocks round it and at its next step). (5) The far-hay test failed only beside other tests (box 3): "not plugged by sunrise" became the real rule, the spent hole is plugged within the plug grace plus a minute after foraging ends. (6) The watcher's first catch: the termite queen swelling too big to walk on her way somewhere kept her half-walked route (her goals stop, so nothing ended it); now her legs drop it (`TermiteQueenEntity.tick`). Lying still where she is, as a swollen real queen does, is unchanged.
- **Termites underground, step 1: holes take turns** (2026-10-17, branch `termites-underground`; the user's four answers in the plan, `docs/plans/termites-underground.md`; on behind `nest.galleries.enabled` in `macrotermes.json`). `colony/Galleries`: (1) **4 arms** (the user's answer while building: the full mound leaves no room for holes along the 8-block tunnels): once the nest's own 2 tunnels are dug, the 2 side tunnels go on out to about 16 blocks (`arm_length`) and 2 more arms branch off past the mound to the front and back (planned by `TunnelRoute` round the mound, saved); they are dug like the other tunnels. (2) **Holes along them** about 4 blocks apart (`hole_spacing`), bitten up through the roof, none nearer the middle than the foraging-hole distance, each one kept as a short foraging tunnel so the alarm, soldiers, runways, bare patches and the numbers foraging use them unchanged. (3) **Turns:** when foraging time starts the colony picks tonight's holes: 1 per 6 foragers (`foragers_per_hole`, real ones and numbers), at least 1, half as many again in rain (`rain_more`); first the holes with known food within 6 blocks (`food_radius`) or a remembered trail to food still there, then 1 or 2 (`explore`) of the holes rested longest; only those may open (`ForagingHoles.mayOpen`). A runway's hole and the hole of a tunnel dug to rich food while its food lasts are always in turn. Soldiers keep their posts in the 2 own tunnels; out on the arms only at holes in use. Plugged holes stay. `/antcolonies list` shows "galleries: N arms, M holes, K in turn". Tests: the gallery proof walks every arm and hole in its 400 landscapes (1,537 arms, 4,640 holes, 0 failures); new scene `holesTakeTurnsNearFoodFirstAndMoreInTheRain` (a full 65-wide ground: arms planned and dug, on the first night fewer holes open than there are and the hole by dead bushes past an arm's end is one of them, on a rainy night more open, the food hole again and at least one new, and every hole that opens is one in turn; the switch off gives back the 2 tunnels); the night scene now checks the holes in turn open, not "both"; the hill side-hole scenes and the far-food scenes (far hay, the fallen tree up a slope, eaten from below) switch the arms off (they are about where the holes open on a slope and about tunnels out to far food: with the arms, a hole along an arm may land near food 20 blocks out, and the colony then rightly uses that hole and a runway, so whether it dug a tunnel depended on timing). Runs: `termitefood,mound,soldiers,hills,away` 54 of 55 on 8 servers (the night scene's "both holes plugged" became "every hole that opened is plugged and none is open": with 6 workers only 1 hole takes its turn), then `termitefood` 21 of 21. Numbers 16, 4, 6, 0.5, 6, 2 are my picks, to confirm. Confirmed in-game by the user 2026-10-17 ("the tunnels are working"; the termites seeming to vanish by day were the numbers and the real ones underground until night, as meant).
- **Termites underground, step 2a: the galleries grow tip by tip** (2026-10-17, branch `termites-underground`; the user's answers in the plan, `docs/plans/termites-underground.md`: reach by colony size, planned with the Matabele ants in mind; tips spread evenly; a fork every 8 to 12 blocks; 2a before 2b; on behind `nest.galleries.enabled`). `colony/Galleries`: once the 4 arms are planned each arm's end is a **tip**. When everything planned is dug, the shortest tip grows one **piece** of about 4 blocks (`piece_length`) with a hole at its end (holes along it as in step 1, taking turns). The way wanders up to 20 degrees (`wander`): 4 ways are tried and the one farthest from the other tips' holes is kept, never more than 60 degrees off straight out from the middle, so the web spreads round the mound like spokes. Every 8 to 12 blocks (`fork_min`, `fork_max`, the colony's dice) a tip **forks** in two, 30 degrees either side (`fork_angle`). A tip stops at about 50 blocks from the middle (`reach`), or after 3 tries with no way found. **Length by colony size:** the total length of gallery may grow to 1 block per 4 workers (`workers_per_block`, numbers counted, at least the arms, at most 600, `max_length`), so a young colony stays close and a big old one reaches 50 blocks. The pieces are dug like the other tunnels (soil to the mound), day and night. **The range** (`Colony.foragingRadius`, used by scouts and the alarm) is the farthest hole plus the 6-block graze round it while the switch is on. `/antcolonies list` shows "galleries: N pieces, T tips, L of A blocks, reach R, H holes, K in turn". Ready for M8: the galleries stay 1 block under the ground, and the holes and feeding spots stay on record. Tests: the gallery proof now also grows whole webs to 600 blocks over 100 landscapes (99 run: 12,652 pieces, 2,783 forks, 13,826 holes, every one walked out and back, none open to the sky, every side of the mound reached on 98; it first found pieces of 2 blocks with holes every 2 blocks, and 6 holes with no way out: pieces now aim at their full length plus the hole's reach, 0 failures); new scene `theGalleriesGrowTipByTipWithTheColony` (a full 65-wide ground, reach 26: a colony of 38 workers keeps to its arms; 600 more join and the tips grow out, fork, lay holes along the new pieces, keep to the budget and the reach, and the range follows the farthest hole). From the category run: the user's half-built mound scene's "the queen digs though the colony has workers" check was too strict (box 3: a dig begun while founding runs a tick or two after the workers join, as the game checks a running goal only every other tick; now only longer counts), and the new scene's fork check counted the tips at the end (forked tips stop at the reach): it now counts the most at once (6). - **Termites underground, step 2b: hidden trips** (2026-10-17, branch `termites-underground`; the user's rule: a termite going into a long gallery travels unseen and comes out at the far end after the walk's time; anything breaking in shows every termite travelling there where it would be; planned with the Matabele ants in mind; the user tests 2a and 2b together in-game). **Legs:** `nav/HiddenWays` (is a cell hidden, the shortest stretch, hand a stretch over) and `AntNavigator.goUnder`: when an insect stands on a hidden cell at the start of a step, the navigator looks ahead along its route while the steps stay hidden (no falls), and if the stretch is at least `nest.galleries.hidden_trip` steps (12, my pick) it hands it over, with the time it takes at the insect's own measured pace (`pace`, the average ticks of its last walking steps; a fresh one uses its speed); `cameUp` picks the route up again where it comes out. **Colony:** `colony/HiddenTrips` owns the trips. The hidden cells are the galleries' tunnels, minus the holes, closed all round. The insect is taken out of the world (not killed, not saved as a mob) and put back as the same insect, with its job, its load and its route, at the far end when its time is up. **Break-ins** (looked for once a second): a block of the stretch filled, a block beside it opened (a player digging, an explosion, later a raiding ant), a creature that is not one of the colony's inside it, or a hole the termites open onto it: every insect travelling that stretch is put back at the floor it would have reached by then, and walks on. Travellers count among the colony's real mobs (`Population`), so none is made to take their place. Away: if the colony goes out of range they are folded into the numbers with what they carry (`AwayLife.foldInNow`); a far end not loaded waits a minute, then the same. Saved with the colony (the insect as it went under). `/antcolonies list` adds "hidden trips: N underway (M made, A out at the far end, B broken into, F folded in)". Switch: `hidden_trip` 0 walks everything. **Tests:** the long walk scene is now shared (`LongGallery`; the long walk runs with hidden trips off, as the walk a break-in leaves them to do); new scene `hiddenTripsGoUnseenAndShowUpWhereTheyAreWhenTheGalleryIsBrokenInto`: travellers are in no place in the world, stretches of 12 or more, 2 to 20 ticks a step (measured 3.8 to 10), out at the far end both ways; the game saved and loaded with 4 travelling (all back, each once); the roof over a traveller's stretch dug out: the travellers there back where they were within a second (6 put back), and they walk on (the stuck watcher). First try: the break-in check took a termite's next trip for the one broken into (box 3, fixed). Run: `termitefood,mound,soldiers,hills,away,colony` 70 of 70 on 8 servers (10 minutes). Full run owed before merging. Picks to confirm: 12 steps, the pace from the last steps, the minute's wait at an unloaded end, travellers counted as real mobs, a hole the termites open counting as a break-in. Confirmed in-game by the user 2026-10-17, together with 2a ("it all works well").
- **Termites underground, step 3: growing toward food** (2026-10-17, branch `termites-underground`; the user's six real answers, `docs/DESIGN.md` and the plan; planned with the Matabele ants in mind: galleries stay 1 under the ground, feeding spots stay on record, hidden trips keep working). All in `colony/Galleries` behind `nest.galleries.enabled`. **Food leads:** rich food the colony knows (branch, fallen tree, hay) gets a gallery piece from the nearest dug gallery (`Galleries.pieceTo`, `addFoodPiece`; `ForagingHoles` still plans the feeding gallery and the hole beside the food, as before); if the workers do not allow its length yet, the food waits and nothing else grows (`ForagingHoles.isFoodWaiting`). Then known grass within 12 blocks past a tip (`pull_reach`, my pick) that no hole grazes yet pulls the tip with the most of it, straight (no wander). Only then the even spreading of 2a. **Stops at the food:** a tip at a live feeding spot does not grow; once the food is gone it may grow on. **One system:** today's new-hole tunnels join the web as pieces (old saves too: parents and first arms worked out on load); new tunnels to food are only dug as pieces; the reach of food is the galleries' reach (the 10-to-40 table is only used with the switch off). **Used = led to food:** a piece is used while one of its holes has known food or a remembered trail near it, a runway, or a live feeding spot; use counts for the pieces it grew from; the 10 days (`fill_days`) count while away. **Filling in:** every 10 seconds at most one piece, the farthest, nothing growing from it, not a first arm, unused for 10 days, with every block loaded, its holes shut, nobody inside, no hidden traveller on it and no player able to see it: packed with the soil round it (grass round it gives dirt, else dirt), its feeding gallery too, blocks other tunnels use kept open; its holes, tips, food spot record and new tunnel go; the piece it grew from is a tip again. Filled length may only be spent again on growth toward food (spreading stays within what it was). **A tip whose branch was filled in no longer spreads at random** (that way led nowhere), only toward food (found in the first run: the spent hay tip regrew at once into the free length, its new piece was filled, and so on for ever). `/antcolonies list` adds "(N out to food)" and "N filled in (B blocks)". **Tests:** the growth scene `theGalleriesGrowTipByTipWithTheColony` grew from 2a's checks into the whole story: hay past the web waits while the workers are too few, then is the first growth, the tip stops at it and nothing grows from it; the hay gone and 11 days passed, the web fills back to the first arms, except the chain to a dead bush the colony knows; the hay's feeding spot leaves the record and its blocks are packed; no filled-in way is dug again at random; dead bushes past an arm pull that tip and spend filled length. - **Fix: termite workers stuck on fungus comb** (the far-hay test, about 4 runs in 10 on `termites-underground`; `docs/BUGS.md` 48). Two causes. The route planner let an insect walk up off a slope (the comb, half a block high) onto the block beside it with a block right over the comb, no room for the step: that walk now needs the cell over the slope open (`AntPathFinder.neighbours`; `passesThrough` names that cell, so a route replans if it closes), species-neutral. And a new layer of comb (or a seed on a harvester heap) grew round an insect standing in the cell, leaving it sunk inside with a whole block to climb: it is now lifted onto the new layer (`FoodStore.addLayerAt`, with `Mound.liftOnto` as the mound's soil does). Checks: unit test `noStepUpOffALowCombUnderACeiling`; a worker on the comb when forage goes on in `termiteWorkersEatNodulesWhenYoungAndOldCombWhenOlder`; both fail without their fix. The far-hay test 6 of 6 alone; `termitefood,mound,soldiers,hills,away,colony` **70 of 70 on 6 servers** (8.5 minutes). Full run owed before merging. Behind `nest.galleries.enabled`. **The night's holes, near or away** (`Galleries.pickTurns`, `holesInTurn`; `FieldLife.forageWith`, `grazedFrom`): the termite numbers graze only round the holes open tonight (the night's turns, runway holes, holes by rich food), within the graze (`food_radius`), plus food under a feeding spot from inside. While away the brain picks each night's holes from what it remembers (the holes it last saw dug, `dugHoles`, saved; the plants it remembers near each), never reading unloaded land; the night's holes stay on record for the Matabele raids. **The far holes** (the user's idea): the holes opened to explore come from the outer half of the web first. **Finding food while away** (`FieldLife.unknown`, the brain's picture of plants round the holes the colony does not know yet, saved; `findRound`): a plant last seen within the graze of a hole that opened while away becomes known. **Growing on return** (`BuildLife.growGalleries`, `Galleries.growNow`): once the nest and the mound are done, the builders' saved time grows the galleries out of sight, a piece at a time by the same rules as near (rich food first, then grass pulling a tip, then the even spreading), each dug before the next, within the budget and the reach; ground not loaded waits for the real workers. **Filling in on return** (`Galleries.cameBack`, `upkeep`): for 30 seconds after coming back every branch due (10 days unused, counted while away) is filled in as soon as it may be (up to 20 at a time) instead of one every 10 seconds; a piece the numbers foraged from while away counts as used (`foragedFrom`, saved). **The plant scan** (`FoodStore.plants`, `Galleries.scanSpots`) looks past the nest's own ground only round the holes, the tips, the plants the colony knows and the workers out. **The away report** adds foraging nights, holes opened, food found from the holes, gallery pieces grown (toward food), blocks of gallery grown, branches filled in and blocks packed with soil. **Tests:** a new `away` scene, `aTermiteColonyAwayForagesFromTheNightsHolesAndGrowsItsGalleriesOnReturn` (a night away: the holes from memory, the one by known food and the far ones, the graze only round them, the unknown tuft found, the report; back: 17 pieces grown out of sight, one out to the known hay first, all dug, within the budget and the reach; away 11 days and back: 12 branches filled in at once, the piece foraged from while away kept). The old step 2c termite scene (tufts 6 blocks from the middle) now runs with the galleries off, as it checks the 2-hole rule (box 2). Also: the test's `Galleries.ageForTests` ages the foraging record too. `termitefood,mound,soldiers,hills,away,colony` 71 of 71 on 8 servers (6 minutes); full run owed before merging; not tried in-game yet. Ground within 6 blocks of a place is dangerous while the strengths there add to 1; the nest's own ground (6 blocks round its middle) never is. So a death is kept away from about 2 days and remembered about 3, more deaths longer. Hooks: `AbstractAntEntity.hurt` (hurts and deaths, next to the alarm and the patrol), `lookForHostiles` once a second; fall, fire, drowning and starving are not danger (hazards later). Species file `colony.danger` (my picks, to confirm). `/antcolonies list` has a "danger:" part; the away report counts places forgotten. **Step 2** (`keep_away`, the harvester ant only; termites plug holes instead, step 3): `Colony.mayForageAt` (the patrol's open ways and no danger) now decides every forager's plant and seed (`FoodStore`), the numbers' plants, spots and searches (`FieldLife`) and the real searchers' spots and search cells (`ScoutGoal`); a trunk trail through a dangerous place counts too (`TrunkTrails.passesDanger`: food out past the danger along it is left alone, so the trail loses its traffic and fades by its own rules) and draws no searchers (`pickForSearch`); a forager hurt within 6 blocks of its own spot gives the spot up (`ForagerSpots.giveUp`), the numbers' spots in danger are let go (`dropHeld`); no recruiting to a dangerous patch, and recruits and scent lines to a patch that turns dangerous are let go (`Recruits`). **Tests** (checks added to existing scenes): the trunk trail scene (`TrailTests`): a forager killed at the east food and one hurt on the hill: two places; the east food and food past it along the trail kept away from, the south food, the hill and the trail short of the danger open; no searcher sent east; saved; still kept away from after nearly 2 days, open at 2.5 days but remembered, forgotten at 3.5; the termite body scene: two termites killed by players are two remembered places. `colony,antnest,away,termitefood` 72 of 72 on 8 servers (13.5 minutes). - **Brain step 4g, step 3: termites plug and close** (2026-10-18, branch `brain-danger`; plan `docs/plans/4g-danger-neighbours.md`; part of it, `ClosedHoles` and the `DangerMap` and `SpeciesProfile` changes, went into commit `cce3e7c` by accident, made by another session working in the same folder). Real Macrotermes attacked at a foraging site pack the holes there with soil from inside, seal the gallery behind them and forage elsewhere; a site raided again and again is given up and new holes opened along the galleries. (1) **`DangerMap`** now tells an attack (a member hurt or killed) from a sighting (`Place.hurts`, `attacked()`), keeps each place's highest strength (`peak`, saved), counts its changes (`version`), and answers `isAttacked` (attacked places within `reach` adding to `dangerous_from`) and `hitHard` (attacked places once as strong as `leave_from`). (2) **`colony/ClosedHoles`** (part of `Colony`, ticked once a second): a hole is **closed** while `isAttacked` at it or at any block of its tunnel (worked out again once a second and at once when the memory changes; not saved); a hole within `merge_within` of a hit-hard place is **left** for good (saved) and the galleries open a new hole in its place (`Galleries.openInstead`, the pure `holeInstead`: at least `new_hole_from` blocks from the place, the nearest such on a gallery under the left hole, else on any other gallery, off the mound, never beside another hole, never reached through a sealed block; it waits while its gallery is not loaded). (3) **What a closed hole means:** plugged at once from inside, whatever the hour (`ForagingHoles.wantsPlugged`, `pickHoleToPlug`, `PlugHoleGoal`), never bitten open (`mayOpen`), no way out (`waysOut`), no turn (`Galleries.pickTurns`, `holesInTurn(level)`, so the numbers do not graze there either), no tip grows on from it or toward an attack (`growTips`, `clearOfAttacks`, `pullTowardGrass`), no hidden trip through the gallery by it (`HiddenTrips.isHidden`, `closesCell`), and no food fetched round it, from outside or inside (`DangerMap.avoids`, `FoodStore.findGrass`, the runways and new holes' food). (4) Species file `colony.danger`: `close_holes` true, `leave_from` 6, `new_hole_from` 7 for Macrotermes (my picks, to confirm; the harvester ant keeps away instead, step 2). `/antcolonies list` adds ` | 3 holes closed for danger, 1 left (1 new hole instead)`. **Tests:** the holes-in-turn scene (`holesTakeTurnsNearFoodFirstAndMoreInTheRain`) goes on: the next night a worker in the food hole is killed by a zombie: at once the food hole and every hole whose gallery runs within 6 blocks closed, the rest not, no hidden trip there, the dead bushes not fetched, the list tells it; the hole plugged from inside at foraging time (about 250 to 300 ticks); still closed a day and a half on; back in the turns, its food fetched again, two days and a bit on; then a death and a hurt more at the place: the hole left, saved, still shut once the memory is gone, and a new hole at least 7 blocks off, not closed. The gallery proof leaves the first hole of each arm on its 399 landscapes and checks the new hole (395 found): 7 blocks from the attack, off the mound, at the top of the ground, not beside another hole, on the left hole's gallery, its way clear of the attack, and with the rest never open to the air and walked out of and back in. Its first game run found two things: a hole 14 blocks out had no room on its own short piece (fixed: the nearest other gallery), and a new hole reached only past the attack was closed at once (fixed: a sealed block ends the search along a gallery). Also fixed a too-strict check in the away termite scene (`docs/BUGS.md` 50). `termitefood,away` 35 of 35 on 6 servers (5.5 minutes); the full run is owed at the end of 4g; not tried in-game yet.

(1) **Side by side:** `check`, `all`, `film` and `graphs` run the clips on several processes, `--jobs <n>` (my pick, to confirm: half the computer's threads, 10 on the user's PC; `--jobs 1` is one after another as before); the output still comes clip by clip in the same order. (2) **Remembered clips:** `check` and `all` keep each clip's fingerprint (the lab's own code, the model's geo file and texture, the clip's key frames, the options) in `references/anim_lab/.lab_cache.json` and skip a clip whose fingerprint has not changed since its last run, printing its findings from memory (my pick, to confirm: failing clips are remembered too, since the check is exact and gives the same findings for the same clip); `all` keeps the pictures ("kept ...") unless something wrote over them since (their size and time stamp are remembered); `--fresh` runs everything; a line `lab: ...` before the problem count says how many clips ran, on how many processes, and the time. (3) **Faster per-frame work, same numbers:** each pose's bone turns worked out once (`Model.transforms`, `place`) instead of per point; the leg-through-body test (73% of the old time) works out each box's sums once per moment (`box_test`, `in_box`) and the pose once instead of once per leg; key frames found by halving (`Keys`); each clip's footfalls worked out once for its film, graphs and check (`gait_of`); the triangle filler and the PNG writer do less per pixel. numpy is not installed, so plain Python stays (no new library). **Found on the way:** since Python 3.12 `sum()` adds floats with a correction term, so a written-out `a + b + c` differed in the last digit and flipped 8 pixels of the termite queen's films where two faces lie exactly on each other; the sums stay `sum()`. **Times** (worker's 17 clips): `check` 45 s before; now 6.4 s on one process, 1.6 s on 10, 0.35 s with nothing changed; one changed clip about 0.4 s. `all` 115 s before; now 36 s on one process, 7 to 8 s on 10, 0.2 s with nothing changed. All six models (worker and five termites): `check` 71 s before, 5 s now (0.6 s unchanged); `all` 209 s before, 22 s now (0.7 s unchanged). **Proof the quality is the same:** the check output of the worker and the five termite models saved before the change, and after (cold and with everything remembered): every line the same apart from the new `lab:` line; all 74 pictures of `all` byte for byte the same; old and new lab compared on 321,500 random bone placements, box tests and sums: 0 differences; the motion library (`tools/ant_models.py`) makes byte-identical clips with the old and the new lab; a walk broken on purpose (leg_l1's swing 1.8 times as big) is still caught ("BAD foot l1 slides on the ground: off by 76%"), the old and new lab agreeing line for line, and only that clip was checked again (16 remembered). Nothing in the game changed.
